## E0001 TRAINER_BRENDAN_ROUTE_103_MUDKIP class=rival
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Pikachu uses Fake Out only on an interruptible threat or Electroweb when the speed drop enables its partner; the starter attacks or sets up only behind a genuinely safe turn. On turn two, exploit the created speed/position advantage rather than repeat Fake Out. Alternate deployment: Pikachu and Shroomish trade immediate starter pressure for disruption and recovery. Reserves & disruption: Taillow is an Orb-activation finisher, not a guaranteed turn-one Guts attacker. Preserve an attack line when sleep is blocked; respect the actual unchosen regional starter. Counterplay & audit: Grass/powder immunity, Electric immunity, priority denial, double targeting, and denying free setup. Disposition: Keep the printed Hoenn seeds; regional generated starter sets require the explicit expansion register, not a false claim that six printed aliases cover all generations.
crack: Grass/powder immunity, Electric immunity, priority denial, double targeting, and denying free setup. Disposition: Keep the printed Hoenn seeds; regional generated starter sets require the explicit expansion register, not a false claim that six printed aliases cover all generations.

## E0002 TRAINER_CALVIN_1 class=regular
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Poochyena uses Taunt or Helping Hand according to whether denying setup or securing damage matters more; Zigzagoon drums only when its post-cost HP and legal Gluttony/Figy recovery survive the opposing pair. On the next turn, cash in with Extreme Speed rather than continue setting up. Alternate deployment: Hoothoot Tailwind plus Starly gives an immediate attacking mode without Belly Drum. Reserves & disruption: If Drum is unsafe, attack with Zigzagoon or move to the bird pair. Figy is compatible with Adamant; Unnerve, item suppression or removal can deny recovery. Rattled is an opponent-dependent bonus, not an activation plan. Counterplay & audit: Priority immunity/denial, Fake Out on the support, physical mitigation, berry denial or focused damage before Drum resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Figy replaces Sitrus to make Gluttony meaningful. Benchmark the first forced route fight against multiple starter pairs, not just a compliant setup fixture.
crack: Priority immunity/denial, Fake Out on the support, physical mitigation, berry denial or focused damage before Drum resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Figy replaces Sitrus to make Gluttony meaningful. Benchmark the first forced route fight against multiple starter pairs, not just a compliant setup fixture.

ZIGZAGOON @FIGY_BERRY GLUTTONY ADAMANT 252/252/4/0/0/0 2 | BELLY_DRUM, EXTREME_SPEED, SEED_BOMB, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

## E0003 TRAINER_RICK class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Scarf Spinarak chooses String Shot when the two-stage Speed drops let Dwebble or Karrablast act usefully before the player; Electroweb offers damaging one-stage control when that is enough. Turn two switches Spinarak when its locked support action no longer produces value. Alternate deployment: Fast control beside Karrablast creates stronger immediate offensive conversion. Reserves & disruption: Dewpider supplies the special Water lane when physical attacks are contained. Do not let a Choice-locked utility move become repeated empty turns. Re-evaluate both targets, turn order, protection, stat-loss immunity, Contrary and retaliatory abilities. Counterplay & audit: Ground immunity blocks Electroweb damage but does not inherently block String Shot. Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, White Herb, speed reversal and punishing switches offer counterplay. Disposition: RETAIN; String Shot replaces Toxic Thread for direct paired speed control, while the Scarf control-and-switch identity remains.
crack: Ground immunity blocks Electroweb damage but does not inherently block String Shot. Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, White Herb, speed reversal and punishing switches offer counterplay. Disposition: RETAIN; String Shot replaces Toxic Thread for direct paired speed control, while the Scarf control-and-switch identity remains.

## E0004 TRAINER_ALLEN class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Pikachu can buy Paras a Spore or use its own damage while Paras selects Wide Guard against real spread pressure. On turn two, attack or reposition; do not automatically shield both supports. Alternate deployment: Pikachu and Bunnelby create a faster damage-focused opening; Mienfoo can supply Fake Out while Paras acts or pressure Rock/Steel targets that resist Bunnelby's Normal attacks. Reserves & disruption: Mienfoo's Eviolite and Regenerator support repeatable U-turn positioning with Bunnelby. Choose a legal useful reserve, account for entry hazards and the incoming attack, and credit Regenerator only when a switch actually occurs. Drain Punch supplies an attacking recovery line; Knock Off disrupts equipment. Paras is not protected from single-target double targeting by Wide Guard. Counterplay & audit: Powder immunity, spread-versus-targeted mix-ups, Ghost immunity to Fake Out/Fighting attacks, Psychic/Fairy pressure on Mienfoo, trapping and punishing the incoming reserve. Disposition: RETAIN; Mienfoo replaces the unsupported Truant/Choice Band Slakoth role. Preserve Bunnelby's Frustration friendship zero. Do not force switches or Fake Out when an immediate attack is better.
crack: Powder immunity, spread-versus-targeted mix-ups, Ghost immunity to Fake Out/Fighting attacks, Psychic/Fairy pressure on Mienfoo, trapping and punishing the incoming reserve. Disposition: RETAIN; Mienfoo replaces the unsupported Truant/Choice Band Slakoth role. Preserve Bunnelby's Frustration friendship zero. Do not force switches or Fake Out when an immediate attack is better.

## E0005 TRAINER_TIANA class=casual
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Skitty chooses Fake Out when interruption is necessary, or timely Fake Tears on the same target Swirlix can exploit with special damage; Helping Hand remains the damage-support alternative when defense drops are blocked or less useful. Do not spend a turn lowering Special Defense after the relevant partner attack has already happened. Alternate deployment: Buneary threatens Klutz-assisted Flame Orb Switcheroo while Swirlix attacks. Reserves & disruption: Eevee can receive a Wish on a planned switch or keep itself alive for another attack. An unconsumed Sitrus does not grant Unburden, and switching removes the earned speed bonus. Fake Tears must respect protection, reflection, stat-loss immunity, Contrary, White Herb and retaliatory abilities; it does not help Eevee's physical attacks. Counterplay & audit: Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, status immunity, item protection where supported, and burst damage before Wish resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Fake Tears replaces Sing to give the whimsical support core a reliable special-damage partnership. No automatic credit for Unburden or guaranteed Orb disruption.
crack: Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, status immunity, item protection where supported, and burst damage before Wish resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Fake Tears replaces Sing to give the whimsical support core a reliable special-damage partnership. No automatic credit for Unburden or guaranteed Orb disruption.

## E0006 TRAINER_BILLY class=regular
strategy: RAIN
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Lotad chooses Rain Dance only when Ducklett's next attack and its own rain turns repay the support action; Ducklett can use Icy Wind or a safe attack while weather is established. Turn two exploits rain instead of resetting it unnecessarily. Alternate deployment: Lotad and Tandemaus combine weather pressure with Encore/Population Bomb rather than another Water attacker. Reserves & disruption: Ditto is a reactive reserve, not an arbitrary turn-one transformation. Ducklett Surf still hits Lotad despite its resistance; use a safe attack when ally damage loses the exchange. Counterplay & audit: Weather replacement, Wide Guard, Grass/Electric pressure, and denying a favorable Ditto target. Disposition: Preserve the rain lesson; attach an ally-damage decision to Surf rather than claiming rain partners are immune.
crack: Weather replacement, Wide Guard, Grass/Electric pressure, and denying a favorable Ditto target. Disposition: Preserve the rain lesson; attach an ally-damage decision to Surf rather than claiming rain partners are immune.

## E0007 TRAINER_DARIAN class=regular
strategy: SETUP,PRESSURE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Chinchou's Discharge is a safe ally line beside Ground-type Barboach while Barboach either attacks or uses Dragon Dance behind adequate survival. Turn two evaluates the new speed order before spending another setup turn. Alternate deployment: Tentacool resists neither Ground nor Electric but takes Grass neutrally and threatens Grass attackers with Sludge Bomb. Acid Spray supports an actual special follow-up from Chinchou or Clamperl; value same-turn support only when it lands before the partner attack. Clamperl retains the special Shell Smash mode. Reserves & disruption: Eviolite gives Tentacool working bulk; Liquid Ooze punishes eligible draining attacks, not all Grass damage. Muddy Water attacks both opponents without allied damage. Discharge damages Tentacool and Clamperl: re-evaluate every switch and choose a safer action when warranted. Counterplay & audit: Ground/Electric pressure on Tentacool, Grass pressure on the other three, Taunt, immediate damage and revenge priority. Disposition: Approved Corphish replacement removes the universal Grass weakness while preserving the Chinchou/Barboach engine and marine theme. Shared support forecasts honor order and stat-drop blockers; no forced Acid Spray or Discharge script.
crack: Ground/Electric pressure on Tentacool, Grass pressure on the other three, Taunt, immediate damage and revenge priority. Disposition: Approved Corphish replacement removes the universal Grass weakness while preserving the Chinchou/Barboach engine and marine theme. Shared support forecasts honor order and stat-drop blockers; no forced Acid Spray or Discharge script.

BARBOACH @EVIOLITE OBLIVIOUS ADAMANT 252/252/4/0/0/0 4 | DRAGON_DANCE, WATERFALL, HIGH_HORSEPOWER, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

## E0008 TRAINER_CINDY_1 class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Snubbull's Intimidate softens eligible attackers while Milcery selects Helping Hand or an active Fairy attack. Turn two should exploit a damage threshold or heal a relevant slot, not maintain two harmless supports. Alternate deployment: Snubbull and Furfrou offer a physical-pressure mode with U-turn positioning. Reserves & disruption: Slakoth is a one-hit reserve with an exit; Aroma Veil protects only its enumerated effects and is not a blanket anti-disruption shield. Counterplay & audit: Special offense, Defiant/Competitive, Taunt outside Aroma Veil's protected set, and targeting Milcery. Disposition: Keep the species identity; reject any AI trace that values pure durability above protecting an actual threat.
crack: Special offense, Defiant/Competitive, Taunt outside Aroma Veil's protected set, and targeting Milcery. Disposition: Keep the species identity; reject any AI trace that values pure durability above protecting an actual threat.

## E0009 TRAINER_LYLE class=casual
strategy: PRESSURE
mega_slots: NONE

The fourth slot is a native regional substitution: all six gender/Hoenn aliases and the 24 non-Hoenn middle-stage alternatives must remain coherent. Hoenn keeps its printed bespoke sets; Marshtomp Earthquake is safe beside Taillow but not Electrike/Shroomish. Prinplup's replacement uses Hydro Pump instead of unsupported Scald; Drizzile uses Mud Shot instead of unsupported Ice Beam, preserving Sniper/Focus Energy and adding Electric coverage/speed control. No automatic sun or ally Electric activation is assumed. Counterplay: physical attacks bypass Eerie Impulse, powder/status immunity, priority, special-drop prevention and attacking the exposed partner during Protect. Design: continuity earns retaining the familiar species, with differentiated support and individual Medium level seeds; full native branch/difficulty acceptance remains pending.
crack: Physical pressure, powder immunity, stat-drop prevention, priority and punishing a passive Orb turn; each regional starter changes the matchup.

Dragonair goes to +5. The interaction to remember is a Hustle-boosted Bolt Beak from full Speed on a switch-in.

## E0151 TRAINER_DREW class=regular
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: NONE

Counterplay & audit: Fighting/Ground pressure, Taunt, slower opposing teams and targeted attacks. Disposition: Keep; distinguish true Steel amplification from a generic teamwide damage bonus. Could this team be cooler? The concept is unusual and worth keeping - a Beast Boost Stakataka setting Trick Room behind Wide Guard, with Steely Spirit amplifying a real Steel attack rather than acting as a generic team buff - but it was broken in execution: all four members are Brave and every one carried a 31 Speed IV, so the room it sets was working against its own team. Zeroing those Speed IVs is the whole difference between a Trick Room team and four slow Pokemon that happen to have a Trick Room user. The moment to remember is Wide Guard blanking a spread attack on the exact turn the room goes up.
crack: Fighting/Ground pressure, Taunt, slower opposing teams and targeted attacks. Disposition: Keep; distinguish true Steel amplification from a generic teamwide damage bonus.

For a casual trainer this is already above its weight and stays: Protean Kecleon changes type to whatever it just used, so its Fake Out is Normal and its Shadow Sneak is Ghost with same-type damage, and Topsy-Turvy on Malamar inverts a player's boosts rather than merely removing them - a rarer and more memorable answer than Haze. What would be cooler is making the Contrary and Protean pieces talk to each other; as built they are two good solo ideas sharing a field. Flagged, not changed - a casual-class trainer should not out-design the nearby aces. Offsets stay low and individual to match the class.
crack: Fairy/Bug pressure, Taunt where relevant, Haze and physical mitigation. Disposition: Keep; the boosting action itself does damage, so don't waste a separate imagined setup turn.

## E0164 TRAINER_RANDALL class=gym
strategy: REDIRECTION,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room concept - the swarm. Every member either hits many times or protects the one that does. First two useful turns: Maushold takes Follow Me while Cinccino fires Skill Link Tail Slap/Bullet Seed into whichever slot the redirection has exposed; Ambipom opens with Technician Fake Out instead of being locked out of it. Turn two converts the free turn - Population Bomb behind Wide Lens, or Encore to trap a support the player already committed. Alternate deployment: Ambipom and Dodrio form a fast, item-free-of-lock physical pair when redirection is a bad idea (Ghost leads, Rocky Helmet). Reserves & disruption: Greedent recycles its own Sitrus through Cheek Pouch as the attrition answer; Dunsparce is the Eviolite disruptor whose Serene Grace Glare and Body Slam paralysis ruin a speed plan. Friend Guard reduces damage to the partner only, and Skill Link fixes hit count but not accuracy - Population Bomb still misses. AI executability: REDIRECTION is backed by a real Follow Me user and ALLY_COMBO by Friend Guard plus Helping-Hand-style protection of Cinccino's turn; no flag is unbacked. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it now is: the Choice Scarf on Ambipom made its Fake Out a one-shot self-lock, which is the opposite of a swarm - it now runs Life Orb with Fake Out/Double Hit/Knock Off/U-turn so the Technician multi-hit identity is real in every slot. The signature the player should remember is Follow Me eating a double-target while three separate multi-hit attackers break Focus Sash, Substitute and Disguise in one turn - the honest counterplay is Ghost typing, contact punishment and Wide Guard. Level offsets are individual: Dunsparce +6 as an Eviolite pre-evolution, the evolved swarm +1 to +2. Counterplay & audit: Ghost immunity, Rocky Helmet/contact punishment, priority denial and physical mitigation.
crack: Lead a Ghost so Follow Me redirects nothing worth having, or a Rocky Helmet/Iron Barbs body so every multi-hit turn costs Randall more than it gains. Wide Guard blanks nothing here - the damage is single-target - so the real answers are contact punishment, physical mitigation and removing Maushold before Population Bomb resolves.

strategy: TRICK_ROOM,ALLY_COMBO

tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT GIRAFARIG
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT SPINDA
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT WYRDEER
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT FARIGIRAF
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT LICKILICKY

Turn two brings Lickilicky in and Oranguru Instructs its Earthquake - Telepathy means Oranguru is the one ally the repeated spread Ground move cannot hit. Alternate deployment: Spinda's own Fake Out plus Own Tempo covers the case where Armor Tail is not up yet and the player leads a confusion or flinch package. Level offsets are individual: Girafarig +6 and Spinda +4 as fragile members, the evolved room core +2.
crack: Taunt or Encore Oranguru before the room lands, or set your own Trick Room to cancel it. Levitate, Flying typing or a switch denies the Instructed Earthquake, and Farigiraf's Assault Vest means it can never Protect a losing turn.

## E0167 TRAINER_BERKE class=gym
strategy: SNOW,SETUP
mega_slots: 2
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Room concept - the Normal gym's cold room, where Normal moves stop being Normal. First two useful turns: Aurorus opens the snow with Snow Warning and immediately has a 100%-accurate Blizzard, while Glalie Mega-Evolves and its Refrigerate turns Double-Edge and the rest of its Normal kit into Ice. Turn two is the payoff: snow raises every Ice-type's Defense on Berke's side, Cetitan's Slush Rush doubles its Speed, and Ice Shard cleans up whatever the spread left. Alternate deployment: Exploud beside Kangaskhan is the pure-Normal mode - Throat Spray fires on the first Hyper Voice and Scrappy makes both of them ignore Ghosts - for when the player brings a Steel or Fire lead that punishes the snow core. Reserves & disruption: Stoutland is the Choice Band Intimidate breaker. Snow does not damage anything and does not stop status; Refrigerate boosts only Normal moves it converts, and Ice Shard is already Ice so it gains nothing but priority. AI executability: SNOW is backed by a real automatic setter and two abilities that read the weather; SETUP is backed by nothing but the Mega transition, which is why it is listed second and must not be treated as a free boost turn. Could this team be cooler? It is now, and this was the room's biggest defect: the old Glalie carried Facade with no status source on the team (a dead move behind Refrigerate) and a Steel-type Perrserker sat in a Normal gym doing nothing thematic. Replacing Perrserker with Aurorus gives Berke a real weather engine, a second rare Ice-type, and the memorable line the player will remember - an always-hitting Blizzard from a fossil while a Mega Glalie throws Ice-type Double-Edges beside it. Honest counterplay: replace the weather and the room loses Blizzard accuracy, Cetitan's Speed and the Ice Defense boost in one move; Fighting, Steel and Fire all cut through it. Level offsets are individual: Mega Glalie -1, the rest +2.
crack: Bring your own weather. One Drought, Drizzle or Sand Stream turn removes Blizzard's accuracy, Cetitan's doubled Speed and the snow Defense boost simultaneously. Otherwise Fighting damage hits five of six for super effective, Steel types wall the converted Normal moves, and Ghost typing blanks Exploud only until Scrappy is accounted for.

## E0168 TRAINER_MARY class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Room concept - the price of power. Every member of Mary's six pays for its damage with a drawback: Hustle's misses, a Choice lock, a self-inflicted burn, Fluffy's doubled Fire damage, Berserk's requirement to be hurt first, and recoil. Mega Lopunny's Scrappy is the deliberate exception - the clean blade in a room full of costs. First two useful turns: Drampa fires the best Choice Specs option while Rufflet pressures or U-turns; Drampa's Berserk is reactive, so the opening attack is not pre-boosted and the AI must not plan as though it were. Turn two respects the lock and Hustle's 80% accuracy instead of assuming both attacks connect. Alternate deployment: Mega Lopunny's Fake Out buys Drampa a clean Specs turn and Scrappy means Ghost leads do not blank it. Reserves & disruption: Obstagoon converts its own Flame Orb into Guts Facade, Dudunsparce's Serene Grace Coil endgame doubles Body Slam paralysis and Rock Slide flinch, and Bewear's Fluffy halves every contact move aimed at it. AI executability: SETUP is backed by Coil; there is no weather, terrain or room flag claimed that the loadout cannot deliver. Could this team be cooler? Yes - and the change is exactly the member that had no price and no payoff. Silvally with a bare RKS System was a Normal-typed Multi-Attack and nothing else; Bewear replaces it with a rare, genuinely scary Normal/Fighting body whose ability is a double-edged trade (contact halved, Fire doubled), which is the room's whole thesis. The interaction to remember is Obstagoon's burn turning into a Guts Facade the same turn Lopunny's Fake Out denies the answer. Honest counterplay: Fairy and Fighting hit most of the room, Fire melts Bewear, Wide Guard is useless here but Rocky Helmet is excellent, and every Choice lock and Hustle miss is a free turn the player can bank. Level offsets are individual: Rufflet +6 as an Eviolite pre-evolution, Mega Lopunny -1, the rest +2.
crack: Punish the drawbacks rather than out-damaging them. Stall a Hustle miss, Encore or Taunt the Choice user, use a Fire move on Bewear where Fluffy doubles it, and keep Drampa below the Berserk threshold or kill it through the threshold in one hit. Fairy/Fighting coverage and contact punishment handle the rest.

## E0169 TRAINER_ALEXIA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room concept - the berserkers, every one of whom poisons or burns itself on purpose. First two useful turns: Zangoose and Ursaring let their Orbs land at end of turn and attack or take one justified guard; the AI must not Protect a harmless slot while the partner is removed. Turn two runs on real Toxic Boost and Guts numbers, not anticipated ones - before the Orb activates these are ordinary attackers and should be played that way. Alternate deployment: Swellow's Toxic Orb Guts Facade is the fast third orb, giving Alexia an immediate-speed mode when the slower bears are a bad matchup; it also pivots with U-turn to bring a bear in safely. Reserves & disruption: Linoone takes a benchmarked Belly Drum only when Extreme Speed can actually close, Bibarel stacks Simple Curse (+2 Attack, +2 Defense per use) and now finishes with priority Aqua Jet, and Ursaluna's Bulletproof is the room's answer to Focus Blast, Sludge Bomb and Shadow Ball. AI executability: SETUP is backed by Belly Drum, Curse and Swords Dance. Stats/legality fixes applied: Bibarel's Superpower was actively anti-synergistic - Simple doubles the Attack and Defense drops as well as the Curse boosts - so it becomes Aqua Jet, which is what a slow +2 sweeper actually needs. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it is: Delcatty with Wonder Skin and a Choice Scarf was the one member that neither hurt itself nor benefited from anyone who did. Swellow replaces it with the Hoenn Guts icon and completes the thesis - three self-inflicted status conditions on one team, each converting a "bad" status into the room's best damage. The interaction to remember is a Toxic Orb ticking on turn one while the player is still deciding whether it was a mistake. Honest counterplay: burst them before the Orbs pay, deny priority, Taunt the setup and remember that a Guts or Toxic Boost attacker is on a clock its own poison is running. Level offsets are individual: the fragile Orb users +2 to +3, Ursaluna +1 because Bulletproof plus Headlong Rush is already the room's strongest body.
crack: Race the Orbs. Every one of these six is weaker on turn one than on turn two, so the first exchange is the player's cheapest. Priority denial, physical mitigation and Taunt on Linoone/Bibarel/Ursaluna all work; Rocky Helmet and Intimidate tax a room built entirely on physical contact; and the poison damage finishes what the player starts.

## E0170 TRAINER_JODY class=gym
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 2
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Room concept - Normal moves that stopped being Normal, in the other direction. Berke's cold room converts to Ice; Jody's converts to Flying and snowballs. First two useful turns: Tauros lands Intimidate on entry and attacks while Pinsir Mega-Evolves; Aerilate then makes Double-Edge a Flying-type nuke and, critically, makes Quick Attack a Flying-type priority move - which is the room's whole point and what the old Facade slot was wasting. Turn two keeps pressure: base Moxie and the post-Mega Aerilate do not coexist, so the AI must not plan a Moxie chain through the transition. Alternate deployment: Tauros beside Porygon-Z when the player's answer to the room is a physical wall - Adaptability Tri Attack is the special mode and does not care about Intimidate. Reserves & disruption: Sawsbuck's Sap Sipper blanks a Grass support and Horn Leech heals it back, Bouffalant's Reckless Head Charge is the Assault Vest body, and Choice Band Huge Power Diggersby is the pure breaker. AI executability: no strategy flag is claimed, which is correct - this room is raw pressure plus one Mega transition, and Ace Pokemon is the only trait it needs. Could this team be cooler? It is now: Mega Pinsir was carrying Facade with no status source anywhere on the team, so the ace's fourth slot did nothing; Aerilate Quick Attack turns it into the memorable moment of the room - a priority Flying attack off 155 Attack that ignores the player's speed control entirely. Honest counterplay: Rock and Electric punish the converted Flying damage, Steel walls it, and a Ghost blanks Tauros and Bouffalant outright. Level offsets are individual: Mega Pinsir -1, Diggersby +1 because Huge Power plus a Choice Band is already a tier above its level, the rest +2.
crack: Rock and Electric coverage answer Aerilate Pinsir and most of the room; a Ghost type blanks the pure-Normal attackers that lack Scrappy. Intimidate and Reflect blunt five of the six, and Diggersby's Choice lock plus Bouffalant's Assault Vest both remove their owner's ability to react.

## E0171 TRAINER_NORMAN_1 class=leader
strategy: ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon, Conservative
tactic: SUPPRESS WEEZING_GALAR NONE SLAKING
plan: Leader concept - Norman answers his own gym's thesis. Six rooms have each shown a Normal-type paying a price for its power; Norman brings the Pokemon with the worst price in the game and simply switches it off. First two useful turns: Galarian Weezing's Neutralizing Gas suppresses every ability on the field, Truant included, so Choice Band Slaking attacks every single turn while Weezing burns or attacks a real threat. Turn two preserves the Gas only while it is actually protecting Slaking's output - if Weezing is about to die anyway, it should spend its last turn on damage or a burn rather than a pointless guard. Alternate deployment: once Weezing is gone, Indeedee-F and Meloetta form a completely different battle - Psychic Surge under a Terrain Extender turns Expanding Force into a spread attack and Follow Me protects the pair, and both the Surge and Porygon2's Download only come online because the Gas has left. Reserves & disruption: Porygon2's Eviolite bulk plus Download and Recover is the long game; Mega Staraptor is the Reckless closer, and its ability too is suppressed while Weezing is out - the AI must read the live suppression state rather than count all six abilities at once. AI executability: ALLY_COMBO is backed by the printed SUPPRESS tactic, which is a genuine bespoke consumer, and by Follow Me; no weather or room flag is claimed. Could this team be cooler? This is the strongest concept in either gym and it is kept intact - a leader whose signature is a support Pokemon that disables the whole field so that his ace can exist is exactly the kind of interaction the player remembers. The one thing raised is pressure: the gym trainers now sit around +2 and a leader must be a clear step beyond, so Slaking and Porygon2 go to +4 and the support core to +3, with Mega Staraptor held at +1 because the stone is already a tier. Honest counterplay is unusually rich: remove or suppress Weezing and Truant returns mid-battle, Ghost typing blanks Slaking's entire Choice-locked Normal option, and the post-Gas Psychic mode has to be beaten a second, different way.
crack: Kill Weezing and the ace breaks itself - Truant comes back and Slaking gives you a free turn every other turn while Choice-locked. A Ghost type walls Double-Edge outright, redirection or Intimidate blunts whatever Slaking is locked into, and the second half of the fight is a Psychic Terrain battle that wants Dark, Steel and your own terrain rather than the answers that beat the first half.

## E0231 TRAINER_TAYLOR class=regular
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Vivillon uses Rage Powder or Sleep Powder only against legal targets while Ribombee attacks or safely uses Quiver Dance. Turn two converts the boost into damage or healing that preserves a useful attack. Alternate deployment: Vivillon can support Pinsir's immediate physical pressure instead of a setup turn. Reserves & disruption: Araquanid's Wide Guard is the spread-defense fallback; Sweet Veil has a distinct sleep-protection role and is not Friend Guard. Could this team be cooler? Retained, and it is better built than its class suggests: Compound Eyes makes Vivillon's Sleep Powder a 100%-accurate move, Rage Powder on the same Pokemon then eats the retaliation, and Ribombee's Quiver Dance converts the bought turns into a real sweep. Araquanid's Water Bubble doubles its Water damage and halves incoming Fire, and Wide Guard protects the frail setup pair from spread attacks. That is a complete redirect-sleep-setup package with an honest answer - powder immunity, Safety Goggles or a Grass lead turns off half of it at once. Offsets individual: Vivillon -1 because a 100% sleep lead needs no level help, the rest +1.
crack: Powder immunity, spread damage, Taunt and Fire/Flying pressure. Disposition: Keep; both supports must retain an offensive or partner-preservation payoff.

## E0243 TRAINER_GRUNT_WEATHER_INST_1 class=grunt
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Raid stage 1 - the lobby. These are the grunts who got through the door before anyone touched the machines, so this is the one room in the Institute with no weather at all: pure piratical disruption and burst. First two useful turns: Golbat Taunts a support or U-turns out while Crawdaunt swings Adaptability Crabhammer/Knock Off - Knock Off stripping a Focus Sash or Eviolite on turn one is what makes the rest of the raid harder. Reserves & disruption: Tentacruel is the black-flag anchor - Liquid Ooze punishes every Drain/Leech recovery the player has, and Acid Spray now drops a target's Special Defense by two stages to set up Sludge Bomb or Muddy Water. The Dark/Poison/Water palette is exactly the faction identity the raid should open with. ## E0244 TRAINER_GRUNT_WEATHER_INST_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Raid stage 2 - the cold storage. The crew here has the Institute's Ice data and a Contrary squid that gets stronger every time it hits something. First two useful turns: Walrein opens with Icy Wind to reverse the speed order, or Encore to lock a player into a move that has already stopped being useful, so that Malamar's Superpower lands under Aqua's terms. Turn two runs on real Contrary boosts: Superpower raises Malamar's Attack and Defense instead of dropping them, so the second Superpower is strictly stronger than the first and the AI must value repetition rather than treat it as a one-shot. Alternate deployment: Gourgeist-Super's Flare Boost special pressure beside Speed Boost Sharpedo is the faster, frailer mode when Walrein's bulk is the wrong tool. Reserves & disruption: Walrein is support, not the damage engine - Thick Fat makes it the crew's answer to Fire and Ice, not a win condition. Stats/legality fix applied: Malamar's Clear Amulet was an active anti-synergy - Contrary wants opposing Intimidate, Snarl and Icy Wind to land, because it converts every one of them into a boost, and Clear Amulet was blocking exactly that. Lum Berry replaces it and covers the burn or paralysis that actually stops a physical Contrary sweeper. AI executability: SETUP is backed by Contrary Superpower accumulating real boosts; no weather flag is claimed because nothing on this team can set one. Could this team be cooler? The concept is good - a squid that grows off the player's own debuffs is a genuinely memorable Aqua idea - and it is now mechanically honest rather than quietly self-defeating. The interaction to remember is a player's Intimidate lead making Malamar stronger. Gourgeist-Super's form alias and its configured Flare Boost remain a catalogue permission to confirm against the resolved form tables rather than a tactical problem. Honest counterplay: Bug and Fairy hit Malamar, Haze erases the whole Contrary curve, and Taunt shuts Walrein's support kit. Level offsets are individual and flat at +1 - this is the raid's second room and all four are evolved.
crack: Haze or Clear Smog undoes every Contrary boost at once and is the single cleanest answer. Otherwise refuse to hand Malamar free stat drops (no Intimidate lead, no Snarl, no Icy Wind), hit it with Bug or Fairy damage, and remember Sharpedo needs turns it does not have before Speed Boost matters.

## E0246 TRAINER_GRUNT_WEATHER_INST_2 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Raid stage 4 - the machine room, and the first grunt who has actually turned something on. Pelipper's Drizzle is Aqua getting what it came for, and the rest of the raid escalates from here. First two useful turns: Pelipper establishes rain and chooses Tailwind, or Wide Guard when that is what protects Ferrothorn's turn; Ferrothorn attacks or takes a survivable Iron Defense behind Iron Barbs, which taxes every contact move aimed at it. Turn two converts Defense into Body Press, or abandons the slow plan for a special reserve if the rain matters more than the wall. Reserves & disruption: Inteleon's Snipe Shot ignores redirection entirely, which is the answer to a Follow Me or Rage Powder player, and Sniper multiplies a critical hit rather than guaranteeing one - without Focus Energy it is a chance, not a plan. Rain weakens Fire damage; it does not create immunity. AI executability: RAIN has an automatic setter, TAILWIND a real user, SETUP an Iron Defense; all three flags are backed. Could this team be cooler? The composition earns retention and the contrast is the point - a defensive Steel mode and a fast Water mode in one grunt, chosen against what the player leads, is more interesting than a fourth interchangeable rain sweeper, and the Snipe Shot answer to redirection is a genuinely uncommon tool at this stage. The moment to remember is Snipe Shot going straight through a Follow Me. Honest counterplay: replace the weather and Ludicolo, Pelipper and the Fire-resistance all weaken at once.
crack: Take the weather back - one Drought or Sand Stream turn undoes the room's whole reason for existing. Grass and Fighting coverage answer Ferrothorn, Electric answers the Water half, and Taunt on Pelipper denies Tailwind, Wide Guard and the rain-support plan in one move.

## E0248 TRAINER_SHELLY_WEATHER_INSTITUTE class=admin
strategy: SNOW,REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Raid boss - Shelly does not steal the weather machine, she reprograms it. Five grunts escalate from no weather, to a stolen rain switch, and then the admin turns the whole Institute to snow and fights the player inside a building that is now working for Aqua. That is the reason this boss is not a second rain team, and it is also why two of her six are the Institute's own Pokemon, commandeered rather than caught. First two useful turns: Alolan Ninetales sets Snow Warning on entry and takes Aurora Veil behind Light Clay - eight turns of halved damage on both sides is the single biggest number in the fight - while Empoleon applies Hydro Pump/Flash Cannon pressure and punishes only genuine opposing stat drops through Competitive. Turn two exploits the Veil: Clefable's Follow Me absorbs a double-target so Mega Feraligatr can Dragon Dance safely, and Sheer Force Liquidation and Ice Punch lose their secondary effects but gain 1.3x damage in exchange. Alternate deployment: Castform beside Ninetales is the Institute's own research Pokemon turned against its keepers - under Shelly's snow Forecast makes Castform an Ice type, so Weather Ball is a 100-power same-type attack and Blizzard never misses; it is the thematic heart of the fight. Reserves & disruption: Rotom-Wash is the Levitate Electric pivot, and Clefable's Unaware means a player who answered the grunts by setting up finds those boosts simply ignored. AI executability: SNOW has an automatic setter, REDIRECTION a real Follow Me user, SETUP a real Dragon Dance; the custom Mega Feraligatr transition remains a source and native gate to confirm. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and one slot was quietly dead: Kingdra's Damp does nothing outside Explosion and Aftermath, so the boss was carrying a 540-BST body with a blank ability and a generic special kit. Castform replaces it with the one Pokemon that belongs in this building more than any other, and its ability is not flavour - Forecast is the mechanic that ties the boss to the room she is standing in. Clefable is likewise re-read as the Institute's own, which resolves the only other thematic loose end. The moment to remember is a Castform turning white as Shelly's snow lands and immediately throwing a perfect-accuracy Blizzard. Honest counterplay: take the weather back and four of her six get worse at once. Level offsets are individual and boss-grade: Castform +5 because it is a frail 420-BST body carrying a key role, Mega Feraligatr +3, the support core +3 to +4.
crack: Replace the snow. Drought, Drizzle or Sand Stream simultaneously ends Aurora Veil's reason for existing, Castform's typing and Blizzard's accuracy. Failing that, remove screens with Brick Break or Defog, attack Clefable rather than routing around Follow Me, bring Grass and Electric for the Water core, and keep your own stat boosts off the table while Unaware and Competitive are on the field.
NINETALES_ALOLA @LIGHT_CLAY SNOW_WARNING TIMID 4/0/0/252/0/252 3 | BLIZZARD, AURORA_VEIL, ENCORE, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
EMPOLEON @SHUCA_BERRY COMPETITIVE MODEST 4/0/0/252/0/252 4 | HYDRO_PUMP, FLASH_CANNON, ICE_BEAM, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
CLEFABLE @SITRUS_BERRY UNAWARE BOLD 252/0/4/0/252/0 3 | FOLLOW_ME, MOONBLAST, HELPING_HAND, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
CASTFORM @FOCUS_SASH FORECAST TIMID 4/0/0/252/0/252 5 | WEATHER_BALL, BLIZZARD, ICY_WIND, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
ROTOM_WASH @WISE_GLASSES LEVITATE MODEST 4/0/0/252/0/252 3 | HYDRO_PUMP, THUNDERBOLT, VOLT_SWITCH, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
FERALIGATR @FERALIGITE SHEER_FORCE ADAMANT 4/252/0/0/0/252 3 | DRAGON_DANCE, LIQUIDATION, ICE_PUNCH, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

## E0249 TRAINER_BRENDAN_ROUTE_119_MUDKIP class=rival
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Rival concept - the Hoenn team the player could have built, assembled better. All six branch aliases are one design: the first five slots are fixed and only the fourth member and the ace change, so the rival always covers the starter the player chose and always leads the same way. First two useful turns: Swellow's Flame Orb lands at the end of turn one and from turn two its Guts Facade is a 140-power attack from a burned body that can no longer be burned again, while Manectric decides between Thunderbolt pressure and a Volt Switch pivot - Lightning Rod is the reason this pair can stand together at all, because it draws the Electric attacks that would otherwise delete Swellow. Turn two exploits whatever the Orb and the pivot bought; before the burn lands Swellow is an ordinary bird and must be played as one. Alternate deployment: Breloom's Toxic Orb Poison Heal is the second, opposite orb - the same self-inflicted status converted into recovery instead of damage - and a 100%-accurate Spore plus priority Mach Punch is the disruption mode when the bird lead is a bad matchup. Reserves & disruption: the branch-specific fourth member exists to threaten the regional starter the player did NOT pick (Shiftry and Sceptile against a Mudkip player, Ludicolo and Swampert against Torchic, Camerupt and Blaziken against Treecko), and Mimikyu's Disguise guarantees it one free turn for Will-O-Wisp or Taunt while Mental Herb covers a Taunt of the player's own. Shiftry's Pickpocket cannot trigger while it still holds its Sitrus Berry - it comes online only after the berry is eaten, which is a sequencing note rather than a dead ability. AI executability: no strategy flag is claimed and none is backed; this is a disruption-and-orbs team, not a weather or room team, and Ace Pokemon correctly points the AI at the starter. Could this team be cooler? The composition earns retention and should not be churned: two orbs paying off in opposite directions, a redirection ability protecting the member that needs it, guaranteed sleep, a guaranteed free turn, and a starter that always answers the player's own - that is a genuinely tight six-slot Hoenn design and it reads instantly as a rival rather than a route trainer. The one change worth considering later is giving the ace a Mega stone so the rival escalates visibly across the campaign; it is deferred here because Blazikenite and Sceptilite both already have homes earlier in the game and duplicating them at Route 119 would flatten those showcases. The moment to remember is Lightning Rod eating a Thunderbolt aimed at a burned Swellow that then hits back for 140. What did need fixing was the levels: a blanket zero made the campaign's mid-game rival the same difficulty as the regulars around it. Every member goes to +2 and the ace to +4. Counterplay & audit: powder immunity, priority, Ground pressure and denying repeated pivots.
crack: Kill Swellow on turn one, before the Flame Orb resolves - it is the whole team's tempo and it is a 60/60/50 body until the burn lands. A Grass type or Safety Goggles blanks Spore and leaves Breloom a slow Mach Punch user with no Poison Heal trigger. Ground damage answers Manectric and removes the Lightning Rod umbrella at once; a Steel or Fire type walls Mimikyu once Disguise is broken, and breaking Disguise with any cheap attack rather than your strongest is the correct line. The ace is always the starter that beats yours, so bring the coverage move your own starter lacks.

MANECTRIC @FOCUS_SASH LIGHTNING_ROD TIMID 4/0/0/252/0/252 2 | THUNDERBOLT, VOLT_SWITCH, SNARL, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
BRELOOM @TOXIC_ORB POISON_HEAL JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 2 | SPORE, MACH_PUNCH, SEED_BOMB, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
SHIFTRY @SITRUS_BERRY PICKPOCKET JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 2 | FAKE_OUT, LEAF_BLADE, KNOCK_OFF, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
MIMIKYU @MENTAL_HERB DISGUISE JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 2 | PLAY_ROUGH, SHADOW_SNEAK, WILL_O_WISP, TAUNT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

## E0272 TRAINER_MIU_AND_YUKI class=regular
strategy: SUN,SNOW
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Ninetales establishes sun while Weepinbell attacks or uses a legal Sleep Powder; turn two exploits Chlorophyll before considering a switch. Counterplay & audit: Weather control, Taunt, Fire/Flying pressure and exploiting the setter switch. Disposition: Keep as a genuine two-weather puzzle only with explicit mutually exclusive modes and a reason to change them. Could this team be cooler? It is kept exactly because of what looks like a bug: two sisters, two Ninetales, two incompatible weathers. Kantonian Ninetales's Drought and Alolan Ninetales's Snow Warning overwrite each other, and the deployment order is the design - the lead pair is sun (Drought plus Chlorophyll Weepinbell), and the Alolan half with Aurora Veil is the second mode after the first falls. The player therefore fights the same trainer under two different skies, and the memorable moment is the weather flipping when the second sister's Ninetales comes out. Torterra's Solid Rock is the grounded body that does not care either way. The honest caution is that the AI must never send both Ninetales out together, which would waste one of them. Offsets individual: the Eviolite Weepinbell at +1, the rest low.
crack: Weather control, Taunt, Fire/Flying pressure and exploiting the setter switch. Disposition: Keep as a genuine two-weather puzzle only with explicit mutually exclusive modes and a reason to change them.

Retained: Absol's Justified turns an opposing Dark move into an Attack boost, Goodra's Poison Heal converts its own Toxic Orb into recovery on a 150-base-Special-Defense body, Jolteon's Volt Absorb makes Electric attacks a heal, and Mega Lucario's Adaptability doubles Close Combat and Meteor Mash. Every one of the four abilities converts something the player wants to do into something Jazmyn wants. That is a coherent thesis for an ace and it needs no new species. The improvement worth naming is that Sucker Punch and Bullet Punch give the team two priority moves and no way to protect a setup turn - Jazmyn wins fast or not at all. Offsets individual: Mega Lucario +2 as the ace.
crack: Fairy/Ground pressure, ability suppression, status denial before Orb activation and special mitigation. Disposition: Keep the confirmed custom ability and no invented self-activation engine.

crack: Protect on the attack turn, trapping, physical mitigation, special pressure and priority denial. Disposition: Keep only with an explicit attack-and-switch role; do not import Norman's suppression plan.

## E0283 TRAINER_JARED class=gym
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: Room concept - the treetop's opening room: setup that actually gets a turn. First two useful turns: Murkrow uses Prankster Tailwind before anything else on the field moves, which is the fix for this room's worst old defect - a six-Flying roster with no speed control at all - and Noctowl attacks under it, its Tinted Lens turning the resists that normally wall Air Slash and Hyper Voice into neutral damage. Turn two is Swoobat's: Simple makes one Calm Mind a +2/+2, so a single protected turn converts a frail bat into the room's real threat. Alternate deployment: Eviolite Technician Scyther beside Choice Band Crobat is the immediate physical mode when the player leads Taunt or Rock coverage and setup is a losing plan. Reserves & disruption: Bombirdier is the room's inversion - a Flying-type whose Rocky Payload boosts Rock Slide, so Jared answers the player's own Flying and Ice switch-ins from inside a Flying gym. Prankster Haze on Murkrow also erases a player's Dragon Dance before Tailwind expires. AI executability: TAILWIND now has a real user with priority access to it, and SETUP has Calm Mind plus Swords-Dance-grade payoff through Technician; neither flag is unbacked any more. Could this team be cooler? It is, and the block's own old note admitted the problem - "weak overlapping bird reserves" and "no Tailwind move despite the type theme". Sniper Fearow and a Focus Sash Swellow were two interchangeable Normal/Flying attackers with no ability doing any work. Murkrow and Bombirdier replace them with a Prankster engine and a Rock-typed answer nobody expects from a bird gym, and the interaction to remember is Tailwind going up at priority +1 on turn one while Swoobat sets up behind it. Honest counterplay: Taunt beats Murkrow outright, Rock and Electric coverage sweep the room, and Tailwind runs out. Level offsets are individual: Murkrow +6 as an Eviolite pre-evolution that is otherwise paper, Scyther +5 for the same reason, the evolved birds +2.
crack: Taunt or a faster priority user removes Tailwind and Haze before they matter; after that the room is six Rock-weak, Electric-weak bodies. Count the Tailwind turns and simply survive them - Swoobat and Murkrow both fold to a single neutral hit - and bring a Steel or Rock type that Bombirdier's Rocky Payload cannot punch through.

## E0284 TRAINER_EDWARDO class=gym
strategy: TAILWIND,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room concept - the carrion flock: nothing here fights fair. First two useful turns: Jumpluff is now a dedicated support - Rage Powder pulls both player attacks onto a Focus Sash body while Honchkrow takes a free Brave Bird or Sucker Punch, and Sleep Powder or Encore replaces the redirection turn when the player has a powder-immune lead. Turn two is the Moxie turn: a single knockout behind redirection snowballs Honchkrow's Attack, and Life Orb Brave Bird from +1 is what actually ends the room. Alternate deployment: Mandibuzz Tailwind supporting Choice Band Braviary is the honest plan when sleep and powder are off the table entirely - Overcoat also means Mandibuzz never eats an opposing Spore. Reserves & disruption: Vespiquen is the Defend Order pivot that heals the pair, and Skill Link Toucannon breaks Focus Sash and Substitute with Bullet Seed and Rock Blast while Beak Blast punishes contact. Defiant on Braviary triggers on opposing stat drops only - friendly Intimidate or a self-inflicted drop must never be credited. AI executability: TAILWIND has a real user; REDIRECTION is now genuinely backed by Rage Powder rather than implied by the theme. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the fix is small and exact: Jumpluff was carrying Acrobatics, a fourth mediocre attack on a Pokemon with 55 Attack, in a room whose whole win condition is giving Honchkrow one safe turn. Rage Powder converts it into the enabler the Moxie plan needs, and the interaction to remember is a Focus Sash Jumpluff eating a double-target so a Life Orb Honchkrow can start snowballing. Honest counterplay: powder immunity (Grass types, Overcoat, Safety Goggles) blanks both of Jumpluff's key moves at once, Rock and Electric punish the whole flock, and Sucker Punch fails against any non-attacking turn. Level offsets are individual and flat at +2 across an evolved, well-rounded six - nothing here is a fragile pre-evolution needing compensation.
crack: Bring powder immunity and the support half of the room stops existing. After that, deny Honchkrow its first knockout - Moxie never starts - punish Sucker Punch by setting up or guarding instead of attacking, and remember Braviary's Choice Band locks it the moment it commits.

## E0285 TRAINER_FLINT class=gym
strategy: SETUP,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Room concept - the room that uses Earthquake in a Flying gym. First two useful turns: Yanmega attacks or takes a Speed Boost turn behind Focus Sash while Archeops fires Earthquake beside it - both of Flint's leads are airborne, so the spread Ground move hits only the player's side. Turn two rechecks the state rather than the species list: a Roost, a Gravity, an Ingrain or an ability suppression grounds the partner and the same Earthquake becomes a mistake. Alternate deployment: Choice Scarf Flygon and Mega Salamence are the fastest version of the same idea, Levitate and Aerilate respectively, with Salamence's Double-Edge converting to Flying. Reserves & disruption: Tropius is the Harvest/Sitrus recovery body that keeps recycling its berry and draining with Leech Seed, and Dragonite is the Multiscale Dragon Dance option - note that Dragonite is grounded, so it is the one member that must never be on the field for an ally Earthquake. Archeops deliberately carries no item so its Acrobatics is at full 110 power, and its Defeatist is the honest price. AI executability: ALLY_COMBO is backed by a real, mechanical airborne-Earthquake relationship rather than a flavour claim, and SETUP by Dragon Dance. Could this team be cooler? This is already the best-conceived room in the gym and it keeps its composition - the itemless Acrobatics Archeops is a genuinely clever build and the Ground/Flying spread engine is the most distinctive thing in Fortree. What it lacked was individual levels: a blanket zero offset made a room with two legend-tier Dragons read the same as the filler rooms. The interaction to remember is a spread Earthquake that the player's team eats twice while Flint's pair floats above it. Honest counterplay: Ice and Rock hit almost everything, Wide Guard blanks both Earthquake and Rock Slide, and grounding effects turn the room's engine against itself.
crack: Wide Guard is unusually strong here - it blanks the spread Ground and Rock moves that are the room's whole plan. Otherwise Ice coverage sweeps five of six, Rock handles the rest, and anything that grounds Flint's own pair (Gravity, Smack Down, Mold Breaker) converts his Earthquake into a friendly-fire turn.

## E0286 TRAINER_ASHLEY class=gym
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
plan: Room concept - the storm room, and the only one in Fortree where the sky is wet. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle lands on entry and it chooses between Tailwind and immediate Hurricane/Muddy Water pressure - under its own rain Hurricane never misses, which is the reason this room exists. Gyarados leads beside it, drops Intimidate on both player slots and either attacks or takes one Dragon Dance behind Lum Berry. Turn two converts: Mantine's Swift Swim comes online, Swanna's Hydration scrubs any status at end of turn and its Weather Ball is a 100-power Water move, and Cramorant's Surf is now safe to fire because every partner on the field resists Water. Alternate deployment: Pelipper and Mantine are the bulky special rain pair when the player's lead punishes physical setup. Reserves & disruption: Mega Pidgeot's No Guard-grade Hurricane accuracy is redundant in rain but its Heat Wave is actively weakened by it - the AI must not pick the Fire option under its own weather. Gulp Missile fires only on Surf or Dive and retaliates against whoever damaged Cramorant afterwards. AI executability: RAIN has an automatic setter, TAILWIND a real user, SETUP now has a genuine Dragon Dance rather than a Swords Dance that had nothing to do with the weather. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the change fixes a real defect rather than adding a bigger name. Ninjask was a sun-and-sand-agnostic Swords Dance bug in a rain room, and worse, it was grounded and Water-neutral, so Cramorant's Surf hit its own partner. Gyarados is Water/Flying: it resists the friendly Surf, it wants the rain, Intimidate supports a fragile flock, and Dragon Dance gives the room a physical win condition it did not have. The interaction to remember is Gulp Missile spitting an Arrokuda back into the face of whoever just knocked Cramorant's health down. Honest counterplay: replace the weather, or bring Electric - the whole room is five Water/Flying-adjacent bodies. Level offsets are individual: Mega Pidgeot -1, Gyarados +1 because Intimidate plus Dragon Dance is already a tier, the rest +2.
crack: Electric coverage is close to a clean sweep here. Failing that, Drought or Sand Stream removes Hurricane accuracy, Mantine's Speed and Swanna's Weather Ball in one turn; Rock handles Pelipper, Pidgeot and Cramorant; and Gyarados folds to the same Electric move as everything else the moment it commits to a Dragon Dance.

## E0287 TRAINER_HUMBERTO class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room concept - six Pokemon that refuse to pay the usual price. Poison Heal turns poison into recovery, Magic Guard turns Life Orb recoil into nothing, Mirror Armor returns stat drops to the sender, Unburden turns a consumed item into doubled Speed, Levitate ignores the ground, and Wonder Guard ignores almost everything. First two useful turns: Gliscor lets its Toxic Orb land - after that it is healing 12.5% a turn - and either attacks or takes a Swords Dance beside Corviknight, whose Mirror Armor punishes an Intimidate or Icy Wind lead by sending the drop straight back. Turn two accounts for the real state: Roost grounds Corviknight for the turn, and Poison Heal only exists after the Orb has actually activated. Alternate deployment: Hawlucha's White Herb is the room's cleverest line - Close Combat's own Defense drops consume the Herb, which triggers Unburden, which doubles its Speed; Sigilyph is the special counterpart, taking Life Orb recoil for free through Magic Guard. Reserves & disruption: Rotom-Fan is the Levitate pivot with Will-O-Wisp, and Shedinja is the room's thesis taken to its limit - a Safety Goggles body that only Rock, Fire, Flying, Ghost and Dark can touch at all, and Safety Goggles is specifically there because powder and weather chip are the honest ways to kill it. AI executability: SETUP is backed by two Swords Dances; nothing claims a weather or terrain the room cannot make. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it required removing a duplicate rather than adding a name. Humberto's Choice Scarf Archeops was the same species Flint fields two rooms earlier in the same gym, which is the clearest overrepresentation defect in Fortree. Shedinja replaces it and completes the room's identity perfectly - it is the ultimate drawback-denial Pokemon, it is Hoenn-native, and it turns one slot of six into a puzzle the player must actually answer rather than out-damage. The moment to remember is a 1 HP Bug/Ghost walling an entire turn while Gliscor heals through its own poison beside it. Honest counterplay: every one of these abilities has a named answer, and a player who brings two of them wins. Level offsets are individual: Shedinja +4 (its level buys Attack, never bulk), the rest +2.
crack: Answer the abilities one at a time. Any Rock, Fire, Flying, Ghost or Dark move deletes Shedinja; Mold Breaker, Taunt or a simple knockout handles Gliscor before Poison Heal matters; attacking Corviknight beats Mirror Armor, which only reflects stat changes; and Hawlucha is fast but frail - remove it before the White Herb is consumed. Ice and Water coverage sweep the rest.

## E0288 TRAINER_DARIUS class=gym
strategy: TAILWIND,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
tactic: ACTIVATE TORNADUS TAILWIND KILOWATTREL
plan: Room concept - the wind itself, weaponised. First two useful turns: Tornadus uses Prankster Tailwind before the player's fastest Pokemon can Taunt it, and that Tailwind is a wind move - Kilowattrel's Wind Power reads it and comes out of the turn Charged, so its next Thunderbolt is doubled. Turn two spends the charge and the speed together rather than re-setting Tailwind; Taunt is the alternative opening when the player's lead is a setter rather than an attacker. Alternate deployment: Tornadus supporting Mega Aerodactyl is the physical mode - Rock Slide and Earthquake under Tailwind from a body that outspeeds essentially everything at this stage. Reserves & disruption: Choice Specs Sheer Force Braviary-Hisui is the immediate special breaker, Togekiss is Serene Grace speed control and spread Fairy damage, and Unfezant is the crit lane - Super Luck plus Scope Lens plus Focus Energy reaches a guaranteed critical hit, which ignores the player's Intimidate, Reflect and Defense boosts entirely. Hurricane is inaccurate without rain and nothing here makes rain; do not grant it accuracy because Tornadus is present. AI executability: TAILWIND has a Prankster user, and ALLY_COMBO is now backed by the printed ACTIVATE tactic rather than being decorative. Could this team be cooler? Yes on two counts, both of which were real defects. Kilowattrel converts the room's own Tailwind into a damage buff, which is the single most thematic engine available to a wind room, and the Focus Energy Unfezant becomes the answer to a player who tries to wall Darius with stat boosts. The moment to remember is a priority Tailwind that simultaneously buffs the team's Speed and charges a partner's next attack. Honest counterplay: Taunt, Prankster-blocking Dark types, and simply outlasting eight Tailwind turns. Level offsets are individual: Tornadus +0 because it is a legendary already above its weight class here, Mega Aerodactyl -1, Unfezant +3 to make the crit lane genuinely threatening, the rest +2.
crack: A Dark-type lead ignores Prankster Tailwind and Taunt entirely and is the cleanest answer to the room. Otherwise Rock and Electric coverage hit most of the six, Wide Guard blanks Rock Slide and the spread Fairy damage, Unfezant's Focus Energy turn is a free turn for you, and Kilowattrel loses its charge the moment it attacks - deny it the Tailwind turn and it is an ordinary frail bird.

## E0289 TRAINER_WINONA_1 class=leader
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Leader concept - the whole sky, not one weather. Each of Winona's rooms showed the player a single trick; she brings two legendaries, a custom Mega and three of the best support tools in the type, and no two of her members are answered the same way. First two useful turns: Zapdos applies immediate Thunderbolt/Hurricane pressure while Altaria decides between Tailwind and Haze - Haze is the new and important option, because it erases a player's Dragon Dance, Calm Mind or Belly Drum on the turn it was supposed to pay off, and Natural Cure means Altaria can cycle a status off by switching. Turn two brings Enamorus into the matchup it wants: Contrary means Superpower raises its Attack instead of dropping it, so repeated Superpowers get stronger, and the AI must treat that as a growth curve rather than a one-shot. Alternate deployment: Gale Wings Talonflame gives priority Brave Bird from full health regardless of Tailwind, which is the answer when the player wins the speed war outright; Choice Specs Noivern with Infiltrator ignores screens and Substitutes. Reserves & disruption: Mega Skarmory is a separate endgame rather than a seventh attacker - Iron Defense into Body Press wins a long game that Winona's fast half cannot. AI executability: TAILWIND and SETUP both have real users; Ace Pokemon is correct for a team whose plan pivots on Enamorus. Could this team be cooler? The composition earns retention and the Mega register confirms it should keep it - Skarmorite has no other home in the campaign, so moving the stone to Altaria would orphan a custom Mega. What was genuinely weak was Altaria itself: four slots of Dragon Pulse, Roost, Helping Hand and Tailwind made the leader's signature Pokemon the least threatening thing on the field and gave her six broadly interchangeable attacking turns. Haze replaces Dragon Pulse so Altaria is the reason a player's setup plan fails, which is a much more memorable role for her ace than a resisted Dragon attack. Honest counterplay: Rock and Ice hit almost everything, but Skarmory laughs at both and needs special Fire or Electric instead - Winona is deliberately not solvable with one coverage move. Level offsets are individual and a clear step beyond the +2 gym rooms: Altaria, Talonflame and Noivern +3, Zapdos and Enamorus +2 as already-strong legendaries, Mega Skarmory +1.
crack: No single type answers her. Rock and Ice beat the fast half; Mega Skarmory needs special Fire or Electric damage and ignores both. Taunt Altaria before it Hazes away your setup, keep Talonflame below full health so Gale Wings loses priority, and refuse to let Enamorus stack Superpowers - every Contrary turn you allow makes the next one worse.

## E0297 TRAINER_MYLES class=ace
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Togekiss redirects when it preserves a worthwhile Lucario attack; otherwise Thunder Wave or Air Slash may be better. Turn two chooses the attacker that best uses the protected slot. Alternate deployment: Togekiss supports Serperior's Leaf Storm growth or Whiscash's setup on a later safe entry. Reserves & disruption: Magmortar is immediate special spread, Miltank utility physical pressure and Whiscash a distinct Dragon Dance mode. Counterplay & audit: Spread damage, Taunt, powder-independent disruption and Fairy/Ground pressure. Disposition: Keep; never credit all back-line setup plans to one impossible simultaneous protected board. Could this team be cooler? Retained: Contrary Serperior's Leaf Storm raises its own Special Attack by two every time it fires, so the third one is the one that ends the fight, and Serene Grace Togekiss's Air Slash flinches 60% of the time while Follow Me keeps the setup alive - redirection plus a growth engine is a real doubles plan rather than six attackers. Glare gives Serperior 100%-accurate paralysis when it needs the turn instead. Whiscash's Dragon Dance and Miltank's Milk Drink are the slow and the sustain lanes. The improvement worth naming is that Magmortar's Choice Specs locks the team's only Fire answer. Offsets individual and flat at +1 with Whiscash at +2 as the setup closer.
crack: Spread damage, Taunt, powder-independent disruption and Fairy/Ground pressure. Disposition: Keep; never credit all back-line setup plans to one impossible simultaneous protected board.

## E0298 TRAINER_PAT class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 4
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Feraligatr and Bouffalant apply immediate complementary physical pressure; turn two pivots to special damage when Intimidate/burn makes continuing unfavorable. Alternate deployment: Electivire's Electroweb supports Mega Sceptile's special offense. Reserves & disruption: Simisear is genuinely mixed by EVs and moves, while Lycanroc supplies priority. No allied Electric hit is authored to guarantee Motor Drive. Counterplay & audit: Physical mitigation, priority, Grass/Ground/Fairy pressure and special bulk against the alternate mode. Disposition: Keep; don't invent a Motor Drive activation because two Electric-associated species are present. Could this team be cooler? Kept, and it is deliberately the broadest coverage six in the stretch: Sheer Force Feraligatr trading secondary effects for 1.3x damage, Motor Drive Electivire turning an Electric attack into a Speed boost, Mega Sceptile as the special Grass/Dragon lane, and Tough Claws Lycanroc-Dusk with Accelerock for priority. Reckless Bouffalant behind a Clear Amulet cannot be Intimidated out of Head Charge. The honest weakness is the same as most six-attacker teams - no support turn, no speed control, no way to buy a setup - so Pat is a wall of good individual builds rather than a plan. Flagged rather than rebuilt, because the coverage itself is what makes the fight hard. Offsets individual and flat at +1 with Lycanroc-Dusk at +2 as the priority closer.
crack: Physical mitigation, priority, Grass/Ground/Fairy pressure and special bulk against the alternate mode. Disposition: Keep; don't invent a Motor Drive activation because two Electric-associated species are present.

## E0301 TRAINER_GABBY_AND_TY_5 class=regular
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Choice Scarf Toxtricity attacks or uses Snarl while Kommo-o attacks or takes Iron Defense only behind a safe board; turn two respects the Choice lock and the real Body Press value the Iron Defense created. Alternate deployment: Cloud Nine Altaria is the pair's actual identity - it suppresses every weather effect on the field, which at this point in the campaign means the player's own rain, sun or sand team is switched off for the whole fight, and so is any weather they were relying on to outspeed. Reserves & disruption: Primarina's Liquid Voice makes Hyper Voice a Water attack that hits both slots, and Punk Rock raises Toxtricity's sound moves while halving the sound damage it takes - so Boomburst-style pressure from the player is also blunted. Counterplay & audit: Wide Guard, Fairy and Ground pressure, Taunt on the Tailwind, and special mitigation. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, the answer is no - and that is the point of keeping it. The reporters are the campaign's recurring ordinary encounter, and this is the one team in their run that does something no gym or villain does: it deletes weather as a mechanic for four Pokemon at once, which is a genuinely awkward surprise immediately before an arc built almost entirely on rain, sun and sand. Changing it would remove the only weather-suppression team in the campaign. The sets are correct as built: Cloud Nine is the concept, Punk Rock and Liquid Voice are the sound-and-water lane around it, and Kommo-o is the Body Press body that does not care about the missing weather.
crack: Wide Guard against the spread sound moves; Fairy and Ground pressure; Taunt Altaria before the Tailwind; bring a team that does not need weather to function.

## E0302 TRAINER_BRENDAN_LILYCOVE_MUDKIP class=rival
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 5
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: The Lilycove rival is one design in six printings: five fixed members that are the rival's actual team, plus the starter that beats the player's. First two useful turns: Swellow's Flame Orb activates at the end of turn one and Guts then raises its Attack by 50 percent while the burn cannot reduce it, because Facade doubles against a statused user - so the rival's opening is to survive one turn and then hit with a 140 base power attack. Manectric's Lightning Rod draws every Electric attack on the field to itself and converts it into +1 Special Attack, and Volt Switch keeps the rival's positioning ahead of the player's. Alternate deployment: Breloom's Toxic Orb plus Poison Heal makes it immune to further status while healing an eighth per turn, and Spore is the most reliable disruption in the game; Hariyama's Thick Fat halves the Fire and Ice attacks aimed at the rival's Grass and Flying members and Fake Out buys the Mega turn. Reserves & disruption: Mega Salamence is the rival's constant ace in every variant - Aerilate turns Double-Edge into a Flying attack with a 20 percent boost, and Dragon Dance behind Intimidate makes it the win condition. The sixth slot is the starter chosen to beat the player's, and it is the only thing that changes between the six branches. Counterplay & audit: Grass and powder immunity, Electric immunity or a non-Electric answer to Manectric, Fairy and Ice pressure on Salamence, and denying the free Dragon Dance turn. Could this team be cooler? The old six branches differed in both the starter and the Mega, which meant the rival had three different aces and no identity at all - and two of those Megas, Metagross and Tyranitar, now belong to Magma's Space Center raid and its hideout gate. It is cooler now because the rival is a person the player can learn: the same five Pokemon, the same Guts-and-Poison-Heal status engine, the same Mega Salamence, and one slot that says "I picked the one that beats yours". That is a rival, rather than a randomiser.
crack: Grass typing or Safety Goggles beats Spore; do not aim Electric attacks at Manectric; Fairy, Ice and Rock pressure on Salamence; Fake Out and priority deny the Dragon Dance turn; the Flame and Toxic Orbs are Knock Off targets on turn one.

The shared roster is the improvement; the Guts, Poison Heal, Lightning Rod and Thick Fat package is a genuine competitive core rather than four unrelated Hoenn natives, and it is the same core the player will recognise when this rival returns later in the campaign.
crack: Grass typing or Safety Goggles beats Spore; do not aim Electric attacks at Manectric; Grass and Fairy pressure on Swampert and Salamence; Knock Off the Orbs on turn one.

## E0303 TRAINER_GABBY_AND_TY_6 class=regular
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Noivern sets Tailwind or attacks while Exploud fires Boomburst - 140 base power into every other Pokemon on the field, including its own partner. That is the reporters' actual trick: both Noivern and Gardevoir carry Telepathy, which makes them immune to damage from an ally's moves, so Exploud can spam its best move with no cost while the player's side takes it twice. Scrappy also lets Boomburst and Hyper Voice hit Ghost types, which are the usual answer to a Normal attacker. Alternate deployment: Sylveon's Pixilate makes Hyper Voice a Fairy spread attack and Throat Spray raises its Special Attack the first time it uses a sound move. Reserves & disruption: Super Fang from Noivern halves a wall the sound lane cannot break. Counterplay & audit: Soundproof is a complete immunity to four of the eight moves on this team, Wide Guard blocks every spread sound move, and Steel and Poison typing answers the Fairy lane. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, no - this is already one of the best-built ordinary encounters in the campaign and the audit leaves the roster alone. What it did not do before was state its own combination: Telepathy exists on two of the four specifically so that Boomburst is free, and that is the thing a player should notice and steal. The reporters escalating from weather suppression in their fifth battle to an ally-immune spread engine in their sixth is exactly the recurring-encounter arc this campaign wants.
crack: Soundproof, Wide Guard or a Throat Chop user shuts most of this team down; Steel and Poison answer the Fairy lane; Rock, Ice and Dragon pressure on Noivern; Exploud is slow and frail.

## E0304 TRAINER_BUFFEL class=ace
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 2
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Mienshao's Fake Out buys the turn and Regenerator means it can pivot out with U-turn and come back healed; Togekiss uses Follow Me to pull every single-target attack away from whichever partner is setting up. Alternate deployment: Mega Milotic with Competitive is the redirection payoff - behind Follow Me it takes a turn it would not otherwise get, and any stat drop aimed at it returns +2 Special Attack. Reserves & disruption: Haxorus takes Swords Dance behind Follow Me and Klinklang takes Shift Gear behind it; Togekiss' Serene Grace doubles Air Slash's flinch chance to 60 percent, which is a second, less honest form of turn denial. Volcarona is the Quiver Dance win condition if the physical lane is answered. Counterplay & audit: spread moves ignore Follow Me entirely and are the correct answer, Taunt stops the redirection, and Electric and Grass pressure removes Milotic. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, no - Buffel is a route ace whose team is a clean, complete statement of one doubles principle: buy a turn, protect the turn, spend the turn. Every member either creates a free turn or uses one. The audit's only note is that this trainer teaches Follow Me shortly before a Psychic gym that punishes redirection habits with Armor Tail and Stalwart, which is good campaign structure rather than a defect.
crack: Spread moves defeat Follow Me; Taunt the redirection; Electric and Grass on Milotic; Rock and Fire pressure on Volcarona before the Quiver Dance.

## E0311 TRAINER_GABRIELLE_1 class=ace
strategy: SNOW,SETUP
mega_slots: 1
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Mega Abomasnow's Snow Warning is automatic and snow raises the Defense of every Ice type on the field by 50 percent, so Gabrielle's whole team becomes physically bulkier the moment the battle starts. Alolan Sandslash's Slush Rush doubles its Speed in that snow and Cetitan's does the same, so the team has two sweepers running off one weather. Alternate deployment: Frosmoth's Ice Scales halves every special attack it takes, which is the answer to the Fire and Rock special coverage a player brings to an Ice team, and Quiver Dance behind that makes it the win condition. Reserves & disruption: Avalugg's Sturdy guarantees one survived hit and Body Press converts its 184 base Defense - already raised by snow - into damage; Mamoswine's Thick Fat halves the Fire and Ice attacks aimed at it and Ice Shard is the priority finish. Counterplay & audit: replace the weather and both Slush Rush sweepers halve, Fire and Fighting and Rock and Steel all bite, and Frosmoth folds instantly to physical damage. Could this team be cooler? The old build was flagged RAIN and SNOW at once and actually ran both - a Gorebyss using Rain Dance beside a Snow Warning Abomasnow, so the two engines deleted each other every time either resolved. That is a genuine strategic defect, not a flavour note. It is cooler now because it is one committed weather with two Slush Rush abusers, an Ice Scales special wall and a Body Press physical wall, and because Frosmoth and Cetitan are both first appearances in the campaign. Asked again after the revision: the team now has a real reason to be an ace-class fight instead of a pile of cold Pokemon.
crack: Change the weather and the team loses half its Speed; Fire, Fighting, Rock and Steel pressure; Frosmoth dies to any physical attack; Stealth Rock-style chip undoes Sturdy.

## E0316 TRAINER_GRUNT_MT_PYRE_1 class=grunt
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Mt Pyre is an away mission, not a hideout - Aqua has sent a light scouting party up a graveyard mountain, so this group is deliberately the thinnest crew in the faction and reads that way. First two useful turns: Liepard's Prankster gives priority Fake Out-free disruption; Encore locks a player who spent turn one on setup or Protect, and Taunt denies the support the player needs, with Foul Play as the damage that uses the player's own Attack stat. Alternate deployment: Hisuian Qwilfish brings Intimidate on entry and Barb Barrage doubles in power against a poisoned target, so Toxic Spikes-free poison from its own attacks compounds. Reserves & disruption: Lanturn's Volt Absorb makes the Electric attack the player packed for a Water crew into a heal. Counterplay & audit: Fighting and Fairy pressure, Safety Goggles are irrelevant here, and Prankster is stopped by Dark typing. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Muk, Alolan Raticate, Tentacruel and Mightyena - three of which now belong to the Magma Hideout or the Aqua bilge watch, so the mountain shared a wardrobe with everything else. It is cooler now because the scouting party has its own look: a thief cat, a plague rat, a Hisuian pufferfish and a deep-sea lantern, none of which appear anywhere else in the campaign. The room's memorable moment is Encore turning the player's own Protect into a wasted second turn.
crack: A Dark-typed lead ignores Prankster entirely; Fighting and Ground pressure; Hustle misses often enough to punish; do not aim Electric attacks at Lanturn.

## E0317 TRAINER_GRUNT_MT_PYRE_2 class=grunt

Mismagius' Levitate keeps it above the Ground attacks the player brought for the earlier Poison crew.
