## E0318 TRAINER_GRUNT_MT_PYRE_3 class=grunt
strategy: TRICK_ROOM,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The upper tomb, and the mountain's one Trick Room. First two useful turns: Spiritomb has no weaknesses except Fairy and 35 base Speed, so it is the safest Trick Room setter in the region; it sets the room when the slow pair can spend it and uses Sucker Punch or Will-O-Wisp otherwise, with Pressure draining the player's PP on the long turns. Huntail then attacks first at 52 Speed under reversed order, and Shell Smash is available when the room is already up and the player has no answer left. Alternate deployment: Lumineon's Storm Drain is the room's redirection - it pulls every single-target Water attack on the field to itself, gains +1 Special Attack for each, and it is the Water immunity the player does not expect from a fish. Reserves & disruption: Drifblim's Aftermath punishes the contact knockout and Unburden-free bulk holds a slot for the room's duration. Counterplay & audit: Taunt the setter, Fairy pressure on Spiritomb, spread Water moves ignore Storm Drain, and running out the five turns is always available. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Jellicent, Amoonguss, Lanturn and Golisopod - a Grass mushroom redirecting on a mountain of the dead, and three species other rooms now own. It is cooler now because the redirection and the room come from the location: a ghost with no weaknesses sets the tempo, a deep-sea eel profits from it, and a butterfly fish quietly turns the player's Water coverage into free Special Attack. Storm Drain instead of Rage Powder also means Safety Goggles - the player's usual answer - does nothing.
crack: Taunt or Fairy pressure on Spiritomb; spread Water moves bypass Storm Drain; Protect through the five turns; Ghost and Dark pressure on Drifblim.

## E0319 TRAINER_GRUNT_MT_PYRE_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The summit gate, the last crew between the player and Matt. First two useful turns: Sneasler's Fake Out opens and Unburden is the room's engine - once its held Berry is consumed its Speed doubles for the rest of the battle, and Dire Claw then has a 50 percent chance to poison, paralyse or put the target to sleep on every hit. Absol's Super Luck plus Scope Lens means Night Slash and Psycho Cut crit roughly half the time, which is the room's way of ignoring the player's setup. Reserves & disruption: Froslass' Cursed Body and Ice/Ghost typing give the room a second disabling body and a Destiny Bond-free pressure lane. The old block was Carracosta, Liepard, Qwilfish and Drapion - all four of which now belong to other rooms, and none of which said "summit". It is cooler now because the gate crew is made of predators that belong on a cold mountain: a climbing Hisuian cat, a disaster omen, a snow bride and a psychic piranha, with one clean escalating idea - Fake Out, then a doubled-speed Dire Claw that can shut the player's answer down before it acts. Asked again after the revision: yes, and the crit lane on Absol is the part worth keeping, because it is the one thing in the Aqua arc that punishes a player who wins by stacking defensive boosts.
crack: Deny or Knock Off the Berry to stop Unburden; Fighting and Fairy pressure; Fire, Rock and Steel on Froslass; Bruxish is frail and dies to priority.

## E0320 TRAINER_MATT_MT_PYRE class=admin
strategy: RAIN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Matt's first showing, and the template the player is meant to recognise when he returns to the hideout with screens and a Last Respects clock. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle is free on entry - this is Matt's bird in both of his battles - and its own action is Tailwind or Wide Guard depending on whether the crew needs speed or cover. Araquanid then attacks: Water Bubble doubles the power of its Water moves and halves incoming Fire damage, so a rain-boosted, Bubble-doubled Liquidation off a modest Attack stat hits harder than anything else in the Aqua arc so far. Alternate deployment: Ludicolo's Swift Swim makes it the fastest thing on the field in rain and Fake Out plus Ice Beam gives Matt a disruption lane; Heliolisk's Dry Skin heals it in the same rain that makes its Thunder never miss. Reserves & disruption: Okidogi's Toxic Chain badly poisons on roughly a third of its hits with no move slot spent on it, which is how Matt beats the bulky ground the player brings. Mega Golisopod is the ace - First Impression is priority, and the plain Golisopod guarding the Aqua Hideout later is deliberately the same species without the stone. Counterplay & audit: replace the weather and three of the six engines weaken at once; Electric and Rock on Pelipper; Grass and Fairy pressure. Could this team be cooler? The previous build shared Toxicroak, Seismitoad and Golisopod with the Aqua Hideout grunts, so the admin looked like a grunt with better levels. It is cooler now because every slot states something the grunts cannot: a passive poison engine, an ability that doubles a move's power, a Dry Skin rain healer and a priority Mega. Araquanid is the standout - a water-bubble spider is a genuinely strange, memorable thing for a pirate to keep, and doubled Water damage is the clearest possible statement that the rain is not decoration.
crack: Change the weather first; Electric and Rock pressure on Pelipper; Fairy and Psychic on Okidogi; Wide Guard and Protect blunt the spread turns; Golisopod's First Impression only works on its first turn out.

## E0321 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_1 class=grunt
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room one of the raid, the slag door. First two useful turns: Glimmora attacks with Power Gem or Sludge Wave; if the player answers it with a physical hit, Toxic Debris lays Toxic Spikes for free and every later Magma room profits, so Mortal Spin is only for clearing the player's own hazards. Swalot Yawns the target it cannot remove more profitably and Encores a committed setup or protect. Alternate deployment: Camerupt is the faction mount and the room's only real damage spike; Mightyena is the faction dog with Intimidate and a Band. Reserves & disruption: Toxic Spikes are the hideout's shared opening investment; a grounded Poison switch-in absorbs them. Counterplay & audit: fly or levitate past the spikes, Ground and Water pressure, Taunt the Yawn, and Safety Goggles or Misty ground are not answers here. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it now is: the old block was Swalot/Camerupt/Alolan Persian/Houndoom, two of which belong to other rooms and one of which (Persian) is a sleek casino cat with no business in a volcano. Glimmora is the memorable upgrade - a rare Rock/Poison crystal-flower whose Toxic Debris turns the player's first physical answer into a hideout-wide hazard, which is exactly the "door crew softens you up for the rest of the base" story. Mightyena and Camerupt are the two deliberate Magma uniform species, used on exactly two grunt rooms each and nowhere else in the hideout.
crack: Airborne or Poison-typed leads ignore the spikes; Ground and Water pressure removes Glimmora and Camerupt; Taunt shuts Yawn and Encore; do not feed Toxic Debris with the obvious physical answer on turn one.

## E0322 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_2 class=grunt
strategy: SAND,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room two, the drill gallery, and the hideout's first weather engine. First two useful turns: Hippowdon's Sand Stream is automatic, so its own action is High Horsepower or a Yawn that forces the switch Sandslash wants; Sandslash attacks at doubled Sand Rush Speed immediately rather than waiting. Alternate deployment: Golem's Sturdy plus Weakness Policy means a resisted-but-super-effective hit hands it a free +2/+2, and Donphan's Sturdy gives the room a second one-turn floor. Reserves & disruption: sand chips every non Rock/Ground/Steel body on the player's side for the whole room and is the setup the deeper sand rooms reuse. Counterplay & audit: replace the weather, break Sturdy with a multi-hit or spread move before committing the big attack, and Wide Guard blanks the paired Rock Slides. Could this team be cooler? The old block paired Hippowdon with Gastrodon and Kabutops - two Water Pokemon inside a lava base, which is faction nonsense. It is cooler now because the room is a pure excavation crew: one weather setter, one Sand Rush sweeper and two Sturdy bodies that make the player spend real actions to break a floor before the sweeper connects. It is deliberately the simplest engine in the hideout so that the later rooms read as escalation.
crack: Your own weather, spread moves and multi-hit attacks to strip Sturdy, Wide Guard against the Rock Slide pair, and Water or Grass pressure on Hippowdon.

## E0323 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_14 class=grunt
strategy: TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE

First two useful turns: Talonflame's Gale Wings gives priority Tailwind at full HP, so the room's speed window opens before the player's lead moves; Nidoqueen then fires a Sheer Force Earth Power or Sludge Bomb into the window. Alternate deployment: Houndoom takes a Nasty Plot only behind Talonflame's Tailwind and a survivable board, and its Flash Fire makes the player's obvious Fire answer a mistake. Reserves & disruption: Skuntank's Aftermath punishes the contact kill and Sucker Punch covers the turn after Tailwind lapses. Houndoom's Flash Fire also sets the hideout's recurring lesson that Fire attacks feed this faction.

## E0324 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_3 class=grunt
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room four, the relic dig, and the hideout's junior Trick Room - deliberately a smaller rehearsal of the room Maxie himself runs at the bottom. First two useful turns: Claydol sets Trick Room only when it survives the turn and at least one of Bronzong, Runerigus or Garbodor is on the field to spend it; otherwise it attacks with Earth Power. Bronzong under reversed order fires Gyro Ball at its real 33 Speed, which is close to maximum power. Alternate deployment: Runerigus' Wandering Spirit swaps abilities with a contact attacker, which can strand a Guts, Speed Boost or Protean sweeper with a useless ability for the rest of the fight. Reserves & disruption: Garbodor lays Toxic Spikes only if the door crew's set was removed, otherwise it attacks; Aftermath punishes the contact finish. Counterplay & audit: Taunt the setter, run out the five turns behind Protect, or use Fighting and Fire pressure that does not need contact. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Claydol/Garbodor/Umbreon/Camerupt - a second Camerupt in the same hideout and an Umbreon, which is a moonlight Eeveelution and about as un-Magma as a Pokemon gets. It is cooler now because every member is something the crew dug out of the volcano - a clay idol, a temple bell, a cursed coffin lid and the base's own garbage - and because Wandering Spirit gives the room a signature the player will remember: punching Runerigus can cost you your ability for the rest of the battle.
crack: Taunt or KO Claydol before the room turns; Protect through the five turns; Wandering Spirit only triggers on contact, so spread and special attacks are safe answers.

## E0325 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE

First two useful turns: Scrafty's Fake Out buys exactly one turn and is spent on the turn Magcargo actually wants - Simple Shell Smash is +4/+4/+2 in one action, and the White Herb immediately restores the defence drops, so the room's whole plan is to survive to that second turn. Alternate deployment: if Magcargo is not safe, Revavroom takes Shift Gear instead and the room becomes a physical Steel/Poison race. Reserves & disruption: Heatmor's Tough Claws Fire Lash cuts Defense on the target every hit, which is the follow-up lane when the setup line is denied. Magcargo going from a joke to a +4 attacker in one turn is the memory the room is built to leave.
crack: Fake Out, Taunt, priority and spread damage all beat a 50 HP setup sweeper; Haze or Clear Smog undoes the Smash; Ground and Water attacks remove Magcargo outright.

## E0326 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_5 class=grunt

## E0327 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_15 class=grunt

Alternate deployment: Klawf's Anger Shell means the player's own strong hit flips it to +1 Attack, +1 Special Attack and +1 Speed below half HP, so chipping it is worse than killing it. ## E0328 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_6 class=grunt

Infernape uses Fake Out only when the bought turn is actually worth more than immediate damage. ## E0331 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_9 class=grunt
strategy: ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room eleven, the standing stones, and the hideout's most distinctive small engine. First two useful turns: Stonjourner does not need an action to matter - Power Spot raises every ally attack by 30 percent simply by standing in the slot, so Choice Band Darmanitan's Sheer Force Flare Blitz lands at roughly double a normal grunt's damage on turn one. Stonjourner's own action is Wide Guard against the player's obvious spread answer, or Rock Slide. Alternate deployment: if Darmanitan is removed, Camerupt takes the boosted slot and Heat Wave becomes the spread threat. Stonjourner is also exactly the right image for a volcano base - a henge the crew dug up and stood back on its feet - and this is Camerupt's second and last grunt appearance.
crack: Remove Stonjourner first and the room's damage halves; Wide Guard and Protect blunt the boosted spread turn; Water and Ground pressure covers everything here.

## E0332 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_16 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room twelve, the toxin store. First two useful turns: Gliscor's Toxic Orb activates at the end of turn one and Poison Heal then restores an eighth of its HP every turn while making it immune to further status; it sets Tailwind or attacks according to whether the room's slower members need the window. Scolipede's Speed Boost compounds with that window, so by turn three it is faster than almost anything the player owns. Alternate deployment: Chandelure behind Tailwind is the room's spread damage and its Flash Fire punishes the Fire answer for the third time in this hideout. Reserves & disruption: Alolan Marowak with Thick Club hits like a legendary and its Lightning Rod absorbs the Electric attacks the player brought for the Flying members. Counterplay & audit: Ice and Water pressure on Gliscor, Ground and Rock on Chandelure, Taunt on the Tailwind, and Knock Off on the Thick Club. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Gliscor/Dustox/Chandelure/Drapion, where Dustox is a route-one moth on a late-hideout guard post. It is cooler now because the room is a compounding-speed puzzle rather than a pile of Poison types - Tailwind and Speed Boost stack, so every turn the player spends not solving it costs more - and because Alolan Marowak brings both a genuine Thick Club damage spike and a Lightning Rod that quietly blanks the obvious answer to the room's Flying and Fire bodies.
crack: Taunt or remove Gliscor before Tailwind; Knock Off the Thick Club; Ice, Rock and Ground pressure; Scolipede is fragile if answered before the third boost.

## E0333 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_10 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room thirteen, the ash pit. First two useful turns: Palossand's Water Compaction gives it +2 Defense every time a Water attack hits it, which inverts the answer the player has been using on Fire and Ground rooms all hideout; its own action is Shadow Ball or Shore Up. Hisuian Typhlosion attacks with Infernal Parade, which doubles in power against a statused target - and this hideout has been applying Toxic Spikes, burns and poison since room one, so that condition is usually already met. Alternate deployment: Mightyena's second and final grunt appearance is the Intimidate body that keeps the slower pair alive; Alolan Dugtrio's Sand Force lane comes online if a previous sand room's weather is still up. Reserves & disruption: Shore Up recovers two thirds in sand. Counterplay & audit: do not aim Water at Palossand, bring Dark/Ghost pressure for Typhlosion, and clear your own status before entering. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Cacturne/Arcanine/Palossand/Clefable - a Fairy princess guarding a magma vault. It is cooler now because it is the room that punishes the player's accumulated habits: the Water answer feeds Palossand, the status the hideout inflicted feeds Infernal Parade, and the leftover sand feeds Dugtrio. Hisuian Typhlosion is a genuinely striking, rare pick and a ghost of fire is the right thing to find in an ash pit.

## E0334 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_11 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room fourteen, the armoury, and the first room the player should expect to lose to. First two useful turns: Orthworm's Earth Eater makes it immune to Ground attacks and heals it for a quarter instead, so it holds a slot indefinitely and Coil makes it a real attacker while it does; the player's Ground lane, which this hideout has been demanding since room two, is dead here. Pangoro's Iron Fist Drain Punch is the sustain lane and Parting Shot resets a losing board. Alternate deployment: Aggron's Heavy Metal doubles its weight, which makes the player's Heavy Slam and Grass Knot maths worse and its own Heavy Slam maximal. Reserves & disruption: Toedscruel is the odd one out on purpose - cave fungus, not a Magma recruit - and its Mycelium Might Spore ignores abilities like Overcoat-adjacent immunities but always moves last, so the AI should use it as a board-flip, not an opener. The old block was Mamoswine/Mandibuzz/Scolipede/Delcatty, which included an Ice mammoth and a house cat in a volcano. It is cooler now because the room is a specific, teachable wall: everything the player has been rewarded for bringing to this hideout - Ground coverage and physical priority - is exactly what Earth Eater, Heavy Metal and Iron Fist sustain answer. Orthworm, Toedscruel and Pangoro are all first appearances.
crack: Bring Water, Grass or Special Fighting coverage rather than Ground; Safety Goggles or Grass typing beats Spore; Aggron folds to Fighting and Ground special attacks.

## E0335 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_12 class=grunt
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room fifteen, the caldera floor, and the rehearsal for Courtney's sun. First two useful turns: Torkoal's Drought is automatic, so its first action is a full-HP Eruption - 150 base power, sun-boosted, into both of the player's slots - and the room is designed so the player must answer that before turn two. Scovillain's Chlorophyll doubles its Speed in that sun, and its Rage Powder is a Fire-typed redirection almost nothing else in the campaign offers, so it can buy Torkoal a second full-power Eruption. Alternate deployment: Volcarona takes Quiver Dance behind the redirection; at +1 in sun it is the single most dangerous grunt Pokemon in the hideout. Reserves & disruption: Garchomp is the non-sun lane and its Rough Skin punishes contact. Counterplay & audit: rain or sand replacement immediately halves Eruption's Fire boost, chip damage lowers Eruption's power directly, and Rock pressure ends Volcarona in one action. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Sableye/Torkoal/Excadrill/Garchomp - a Quash gimmick attached to a sun engine that did not need it. It is cooler now because the room is a two-stage threat that teaches the boss fight: Eruption punishes you for not acting, and the thing that buys Eruption a second turn is a Fire-typed Rage Powder user you cannot answer with Safety Goggles habits alone. Chip Torkoal and the room's damage collapses - that is the lesson the player needs before Maxie.
crack: Change the weather, chip Torkoal so Eruption weakens, Rock Slide the Volcarona, Wide Guard or Protect the Eruption turn, and remember Rage Powder does not stop spread moves.

## E0336 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_13 class=grunt
strategy: SAND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room sixteen, the iron gate outside the admins' chamber, and the hardest grunt fight in the campaign so far. First two useful turns: Tyranitar's Sand Stream overwrites whatever weather the player installed in the last room and its Assault Vest makes the special answer to it fail; it attacks immediately. Probopass' Magnet Pull traps the player's Steel switch-ins - the exact Pokemon a player brings to answer a Rock and Ground hideout - and Sturdy plus sand makes it a four-turn problem. Alternate deployment: Steelix takes Wide Guard against the player's spread answer and Body Press converts its enormous Defense into damage. Reserves & disruption: Garchomp is absent here; this room deliberately has no fast member, so the player who solved earlier rooms with speed has to solve this one with damage. Counterplay & audit: Fighting coverage answers three of the four, Water and Ground answer Tyranitar and Probopass, and Steel-typed players must be aware they cannot switch out. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Rhyperior/Amoonguss/Pangoro/Tauros - a mushroom and a cow standing between the player and Maxie. It is cooler now because it is a gate rather than a team: sand to undo the player's weather, a trap to punish the natural Steel answer, and two walls that convert defence into offence. Trapping the player's Steel pivot with Magnet Pull immediately before the admin gauntlet is the most consequential grunt decision in the hideout.
crack: Do not bring a Steel pivot you need to rotate; Fighting, Water and Ground pressure; Taunt on Steelix; the room has no speed control, so fast special attackers dictate the pace.

## E0337 TRAINER_TABITHA_MAGMA_HIDEOUT class=admin
strategy: SAND,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Tabitha runs the excavation, so his battle is the hideout's sand engine executed properly. First two useful turns: Gigalith's Sand Stream with Smooth Rock lasts eight turns and Excadrill's Sand Rush doubles its Speed for all of them, so the opening is Gigalith attacking while Excadrill outruns almost anything the player owns. Gigalith's own alternative action is Wide Guard when the player's answer to a Rock-heavy admin is a spread move. Alternate deployment: Coalossal's Steam Engine is NOT self-activated here - the previous Mt Chimney fight already used that trick and repeating it would be a weaker second showing. Instead Coalossal is a trap: any Fire or Water attack the player aims at it, including the Water attack that is 4x super effective and the obvious answer to a Rock/Fire body, grants +6 Speed if it survives, and Weakness Policy grants +2/+2 on top. Killing it outright is correct; chipping it is a disaster. Reserves & disruption: Volcanion is the faction's captured volcano spirit and Steam Eruption off Choice Specs is the admin's raw damage; Krookodile's Moxie converts a knockout into a sweep. Mega Golurk with Iron Fist is the ace and its Shadow Punch and Drain Punch are both boosted. Counterplay & audit: one large hit on Coalossal rather than two, weather replacement to strip Sand Rush, Wide Guard against the paired Rock lane, and Water/Ground pressure. Could this team be cooler? The previous build had Dragapult firing Surf at its own Coalossal - a 4x super effective Water attack on a Rock/Fire ally, which is a genuine design defect, not a clever combo, and Dragapult is a Ghost dragon with no Magma identity. It is cooler now because removing the broken self-hit turns Coalossal into the fight's real puzzle, because Excadrill here foreshadows the Mega Excadrill Tabitha brings to the Space Center, and because Volcanion - literally the volcano Pokemon - is the correct legendary for a man who digs into volcanoes.
crack: Kill Coalossal in one action or leave it alone entirely; replace the sand to halve Excadrill; Water, Ground and Fighting pressure; Mega Golurk is fast for its class but folds to Ghost, Dark, Grass and Water.

## E0338 TRAINER_COURTNEY_MAGMA_HIDEOUT class=admin
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Courtney is the faction's analyst and her battle is the sun engine executed properly. First two useful turns: Ninetales' Drought is automatic; its own action is Encore, which locks a player who took a setup or protect turn into repeating it, or a sun-boosted Heat Wave. Great Tusk's Protosynthesis triggers on that sun and raises its already enormous Attack, so Headlong Rush lands on turn one. Alternate deployment: Chi-Yu's Beads of Ruin cuts every other Pokemon's Special Defense by 25 percent for as long as it is on the field - a permanent, field-wide debuff that makes Ninetales' and Ceruledge's special lanes hit far harder than their stats suggest, and the player will not see it in the battle text. Reserves & disruption: Salazzle's Corrosion poisons Steel and Poison types, which is how Courtney answers the Steel walls the player brings to a Fire team; Ceruledge's Bitter Blade heals it for half the damage dealt and Weak Armor turns the player's physical answer into +2 Speed. Mega Pyroar is the ace and Competitive means any stat drop hands it +2 Special Attack. Counterplay & audit: remove Chi-Yu early even though it is not the immediate threat, replace the sun, and do not use Intimidate or Icy Wind into Competitive. Could this team be cooler? The previous build's sixth was a second Houndoom, which duplicated Flash Fire with the rest of the team and added nothing. It is cooler now with Ceruledge: a Fire/Ghost blade spirit gives Courtney a physical lane, Ghost coverage against the Psychic answers to her, self-healing that makes chip damage pointless, and Weak Armor so that the player's physical response accelerates her rather than her opponent. Chi-Yu's invisible team-wide Special Defense cut remains the fight's best hidden interaction.
crack: Kill Chi-Yu first; change the weather; never lower Pyroar's stats; Rock, Water and Ground pressure; Salazzle's Corrosion means a Steel wall is not a safe long-term answer.

## E0339 TRAINER_MAXIE_MAGMA_HIDEOUT class=leader
strategy: TRICK_ROOM,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Maxie wants to raise the continent, so the boss fight is the land itself refusing to move at your speed. First two useful turns: Stakataka sets Trick Room - it has zero Speed IVs and 13 base Speed, so it moves last in normal order and first under its own room, and Wide Guard is its alternative when the player's answer to six slow bodies is a spread move. Under the reversed order every other member outruns almost the entire player team. Alternate deployment: Mega Camerupt is the payoff. Quiet, zero Speed, Solid Rock, and Eruption at full HP is 150 base power into both slots from a Mega Special Attack stat - the single largest attack in the campaign to this point - and Trick Room means it fires first rather than last. Reserves & disruption: Regirock uses Curse, which is normally a liability because it lowers Speed; under Trick Room lowering Speed makes it faster, so Curse is pure profit and it becomes an unkillable +2 attacker. Copperajah's Heavy Metal and Rhyperior's Solid Rock are the two bodies that hold the field while the room ticks; Rhyperior's Lightning Rod also draws the Electric attacks aimed at nothing else here. Counterplay & audit: Taunt the setter, Protect or stall the five turns and win the turns after, Fake Out to deny the room turn, Haze the Curses, and chip Mega Camerupt so Eruption weakens. Could this team be cooler? The previous build was Torkoal/Crobat/Mightyena/Victreebel/Hydreigon/Mega Camerupt - which is very nearly the same team Maxie already brought to Mt Chimney, plus a Hydreigon that belongs to no faction. Fighting the same man twice with the same sun-and-Tailwind plan is the weakest thing a villain leader can do. It is cooler now because the second Maxie fight is a completely different machine from the first: Mt Chimney was sun and speed, the hideout is Trick Room and stone, and the same Mega Camerupt that was a slow liability there is the whole point here. Curse under Trick Room, Beast Boost on a fortress, and a full-HP Eruption arriving first are three interactions the player has not seen from this faction before.
crack: Taunt, Fake Out or a fast knockout on Stakataka denies the room; Protect and switch through the five turns; Haze undoes Curse; Water, Ground, Fighting and Grass pressure all bite hard, and chipping Camerupt directly reduces Eruption's power.

## E0341 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_2 class=grunt

The old block was Sharpedo/Dragalge/Overqwil/Crawdaunt, four Dark-and-Poison fish with the same job and no interaction between them. ## E0342 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_3 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The hull breakers, and the hideout's setup room. First two useful turns: Barbaracle takes Shell Smash behind Toxapex's Wide Guard or Seismitoad's immediate damage, and Tough Claws then makes a +2 Razor Shell and Stone Edge lethal in one turn. Drednaw's Shell Armor denies the critical hit that would otherwise end the setup plan, and it can take the second Shell Smash if the first is answered. Alternate deployment: if both Smash users are threatened, the room reverts to Seismitoad's Water Absorb attrition and Toxapex's Regenerator stall. Reserves & disruption: Toxapex's Baneful Bunker-free set is deliberate - it protects with Wide Guard for the side rather than itself, which is the harder and more useful action here. Counterplay & audit: Haze, Clear Smog, Fake Out, Taunt and priority all beat a two-turn setup plan; Electric and Grass pressure removes the Water bodies. Could this team be cooler? The old block put Scrafty - a Magma-side street thug, now correctly housed in the Magma Hideout - next to two shell Pokemon, so the room's idea was half-formed. It is cooler now because it is unambiguously the setup room: two Shell Smash bodies, one of which has Shell Armor specifically so the player cannot crit their way out, behind a Wide Guard that blanks the spread answer. The room asks a clean question - can you remove a 55-Speed sweeper in the one turn before it becomes a 110-Speed one?
crack: Haze or Clear Smog, Fake Out, Taunt, or a spread move on the Smash turn; Electric and Grass pressure; Wide Guard only covers spread moves, so single-target damage still lands.

## E0343 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_5 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The rain callers, and the first automatic weather in the hideout. First two useful turns: Politoed's Drizzle is free and Damp Rock holds it eight turns, so Politoed's own action is Helping Hand into Barraskewda's Swift Swim Liquidation or Icy Wind to bank a speed advantage for after the rain. Barraskewda at doubled Speed outruns essentially the entire player roster at this stage and its rain-boosted Water attacks are the room's damage. Alternate deployment: Kilowattrel's Wind Power charges from Tailwind and its Thunder never misses in rain, so the room can layer speed control on top of weather. Reserves & disruption: Jellicent absorbs the Water attacks the player brings against a rain team and Water Spout under rain is a second spread threat. Counterplay & audit: weather replacement is the single best answer, followed by Electric and Grass pressure and Taunt on the Tailwind. Could this team be cooler? It was already the strongest Aqua block and it survives largely intact - the concrete change is that Politoed is now the hideout's only Drizzle user, because Matt was also carrying one and a faction reads as orchestrated only when the engine has a single owner per room. Asked again after the revision: the room could add a fifth trick, but it should not; this is the plain, correct statement of the rain engine that Matt then escalates, and its value is that the player learns the pattern here and pays for it later.
crack: Replace the weather; Electric and Grass pressure; Taunt Politoed before Helping Hand; Barraskewda is frail and dies to priority or a spread move.

## E0344 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_7 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The ice hold, and a deliberate variation rather than a repeat of the rain room. First two useful turns: nothing here has Drizzle - Lapras sets Rain Dance by hand off a Damp Rock, which costs a real turn, and the room is built so that turn is worth paying for. Beartic's Swift Swim doubles off that manual rain and Cloyster's Skill Link Icicle Spear fires five hits every time, which is the answer to Focus Sash, Sturdy, Disguise and substitutes all at once. Alternate deployment: Cloyster can Shell Smash instead when the board is safe; its White Herb is deliberately absent so the defence drops are real and the player has a window. Reserves & disruption: Kingler's Sheer Force Crabhammer is boosted by both the rain and the ability, and Lapras' Freeze-Dry hits the Water answers the player brings for 2x. Counterplay & audit: Taunt or knock out Lapras before the manual rain, Fighting and Rock pressure on the Ice bodies, Steel typing against Icicle Spear. Could this team be cooler? The old block had a second Barraskewda - the exact species the previous room already used - plus Thievul, a Galarian fox with no maritime identity. It is cooler now because it answers "how do you do rain twice without repeating yourself": this room has to earn its weather, which makes Lapras a real target rather than scenery, and it pairs the weather with multi-hit and Freeze-Dry lanes that the automatic rain room does not have. Killing Lapras on turn one is the correct play and the room knows it.
crack: Remove or Taunt Lapras before Rain Dance and the room loses its engine; Fighting, Rock and Steel answer the Ice lane; Cloyster's Smash turn leaves it fragile.

## E0345 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 4
plan: The quartermaster's cabin, and the only grunt in either hideout who has been trusted with a Mega Stone. First two useful turns: Clawitzer's Mega Launcher doubles every pulse move it owns, and the room's signature is that this includes Heal Pulse - a full half of an ally's maximum HP restored in one action, from a grunt. The crew's plan is therefore to keep Mega Eelektross alive through damage the player expected to be lethal. Crobat Taunts the setup or support the player needs, or pivots with U-turn. Alternate deployment: Mega Eelektross has no weaknesses while Levitate holds and Wild Charge plus Crunch covers the field; it is the ace and the Mega slot. Reserves & disruption: Alolan Muk's Poison/Dark typing is immune to Psychic and its Knock Off strips the item the player was relying on. Counterplay & audit: Taunt or Encore the healer, burst damage that outpaces a 50 percent heal, Ground attacks the moment Levitate is off, and Fairy pressure on the Dark bodies. Could this team be cooler? The old block had the same four members but Clawitzer was a plain three-pulse attacker, which wasted the one ability in the game that turns a support move into a game-changer. It is cooler now because a grunt with a Mega and a doubled Heal Pulse is a genuine difficulty spike that the player will remember and complain about in the right way - and because the counterplay is clean and teachable: Taunt the shrimp, not the eel.
crack: Taunt, Encore or Knock Off the Clawitzer; burst the Eelektross past the heal rather than chipping it; Fairy and Ground pressure; Crobat folds to Rock, Electric and Psychic.

## E0346 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_6 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The pressure crew, guarding the pump room, and the most spiteful four Pokemon in the hideout. First two useful turns: Toxicroak's Fake Out buys the turn, and Pyukumuku is the reason the room is dangerous - Innards Out means that whoever lands the knockout blow immediately takes damage equal to Pyukumuku's remaining HP, so the player must choose between leaving a Recover-spamming wall alive and killing it into a full-HP retaliation. Wishiwashi opens in Solo Form and flips to School Form above a quarter HP, so it looks like free damage and is not. Alternate deployment: Overqwil's Intimidate plus Swords Dance is the physical lane once the stall bodies have soaked two turns. Reserves & disruption: Toxicroak's Dry Skin heals it on the Water attacks the player brought for this whole hideout. Counterplay & audit: chip Pyukumuku with a Pokemon you are willing to lose, or ignore it; Psychic and Ground pressure on the Poison pair; Electric and Grass on Wishiwashi. Could this team be cooler? The old block repeated Toxapex from the hull-breaker room and finished with Falinks, a Roman phalanx of bugs that has no place on a pirate crew. It is cooler now because it commits fully to being the attrition room - Innards Out, Schooling and Dry Skin are three separate ways of making the player's damage not count - and because Innards Out is the single most memorable "wait, what just happened" interaction available at this stage and no other trainer in the campaign has it.
crack: Knock out Pyukumuku with an expendable body or leave it entirely; Psychic, Ground and Electric pressure; Wishiwashi is trivial once below a quarter HP, so commit fully rather than chipping.

## E0347 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_8 class=grunt

Reserves & disruption: Walrein's Thick Fat halves the Ice and Fire attacks aimed at it and Ice Body plus Protect stalls if the room needs time. Relicanth's recoil-free Head Smash is the memorable piece - the player expects a harmless fossil and takes 150 base power.

## E0348 TRAINER_MATT class=admin
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: 5
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Matt at the hideout is the same man the player fought on Mt Pyre, escalated rather than replaced - Pelipper is his bird both times, and the difference is what stands behind it. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle is free on entry and Wide Guard covers the crew's first spread turn while Kingdra, at doubled Swift Swim Speed and holding Choice Specs, fires a rain-boosted Hydro Pump before the player's lead moves. Alternate deployment: Grimmsnarl's Prankster gives priority Reflect and Light Screen, so the admin's screens land before the player's first attack and every subsequent exchange runs at half damage - this, not raw power, is what makes Matt harder than his grunts. Reserves & disruption: Archaludon's Electro Shot charges instantly in rain and fires a 130 base power Electric attack while gaining +1 Special Attack every time; it is the answer to the Water-resist walls the player brings. Basculegion's Last Respects grows 50 base power for every fainted teammate, so the longer the fight runs the more the admin's final slot hurts. Mega Malamar with Contrary turns Superpower's drops into +1/+1 every hit. Counterplay & audit: Brick Break, Defog or simply outlasting the screens; weather replacement; Taunt on Grimmsnarl before the screens resolve. Could this team be cooler? The previous build's Drizzle came from a Politoed that the grunt two rooms earlier already owned, which made the admin look like a bigger grunt instead of the same recurring villain. It is cooler now with Pelipper carried over from Mt Pyre: the player recognises the opening, expects the Mt Pyre plan, and gets screens, an instant Electro Shot and a Last Respects clock instead. Asked again after the revision - the team is a genuine boss because it wins by compounding advantages rather than by one big attack, and each of the four engines has a separate, honest answer.
crack: Taunt Grimmsnarl or break the screens; replace the rain to halve Kingdra and slow Electro Shot; kill Basculegion early while Last Respects is still weak; Fairy and Bug pressure on Malamar, Electric and Rock on Pelipper.

## E0354 TRAINER_LILA_AND_ROY_1 class=ace
strategy: SETUP,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Rillaboom's Grassy Surge covers the field in Grassy Terrain the moment it enters, which heals every grounded Pokemon each turn, boosts Grass moves by 30 percent and halves Earthquake - and, critically, it immediately consumes Hawlucha's Grassy Seed. That gives Hawlucha +1 Defense and triggers Unburden, doubling its Speed for the rest of the battle; a Swords Dance behind that is the pair's win condition. Rillaboom's own opening is Fake Out or a terrain-boosted Grassy Glide, which is priority while the terrain holds. Alternate deployment: if Hawlucha is threatened, Amoonguss uses Rage Powder to pull the attack away, and its Regenerator heals it back on the rotation. Reserves & disruption: Tsareena's Queenly Majesty blocks every priority move aimed at this side, which is the standard answer to a doubled-Speed sweeper, and Trop Kick lowers the target's Attack on every hit. Counterplay & audit: Flying types, Levitate and Safety Goggles all ignore Rage Powder; replacing the terrain stops the healing and Grassy Glide's priority; Fire, Ice and Flying pressure answers all four. Could this team be cooler? The old build was a Tatsugiri and Dondozo Commander pair - a lovely engine, but the Mossdeep gym's Sylvia showcases exactly that combination two chapters later, so the route couple were spoiling the gym's best room. It is cooler now because Grassy Seed Unburden Hawlucha is a famous, spectacular doubles engine that appears nowhere else in the campaign, because the four members form one chain rather than two pairs, and because Queenly Majesty and Rage Powder give the team two different, honest ways of protecting the sweeper.
crack: Replace the terrain; Safety Goggles or a Flying type ignores Rage Powder; Fire, Ice and Flying pressure; Hawlucha is frail, so remove it before the Seed resolves or accept a doubled-Speed attacker.

## E0364 TRAINER_AURON class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Auron is an Eeveelution specialist and the team is built so each member is immune to something the others fear. Jolteon's Volt Absorb heals on Electric attacks, Vaporeon's Water Absorb heals on Water attacks, Flareon's Flash Fire is immune to Fire and gains a 50 percent Fire boost from it, and Umbreon is immune to Psychic - so three of the four most common coverage types on a player's team are liabilities here. Choice Scarf Jolteon attacks or Volt Switches; Vaporeon's Helping Hand fires the turn it would otherwise be chipping. Alternate deployment: Umbreon is the engine. Foul Play uses the target's own Attack stat, so a Swords Dance or Dragon Dance sweeper hits itself harder than Umbreon ever could, and Wish plus Protect heals the partner for half of Umbreon's own large HP. Reserves & disruption: Flareon's Guts-free Flash Fire boost makes Flare Blitz the physical finish. Counterplay & audit: Fighting, Ground and Fairy attacks are the answers none of the four absorb; special attackers beat Foul Play entirely. Could this team be cooler? The old build was Lucario, Vaporeon, Corsola and Jolteon - two Eeveelutions and two unrelated bodies, with no reason for any of them to stand together. It is cooler now because the trainer has a concept the player reads instantly from the first two sprites, and because the concept is competitively real rather than cosmetic: a team of four absorption abilities means the player's usual coverage is actively wrong and they have to find the one type nobody here resists.
crack: Fighting, Ground and Fairy attacks; special attackers ignore Foul Play; Taunt denies Wish and Helping Hand; each member is individually frail once you stop feeding its ability.

## E0367 TRAINER_NIKKI class=regular
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative

Alternate deployment: Eviolite Zweilous is the interesting slot. Hustle raises its Attack by 50 percent at the cost of accuracy, Eviolite raises both defences by 50 percent, and the combination makes an unevolved Pokemon hit and survive like a fully evolved one - it is a small preview of the Mossdeep gym's Eviolite room. Reserves & disruption: Swanna's Hurricane and Hydro Pump are the special lane and Roost undoes the chip. crack: Electric and Grass pressure on the Water pair; Fairy and Ice on Zweilous; Taunt denies Wish; Hustle's misses make Zweilous a favourable trade.

## E0368 TRAINER_BRENDA class=regular
strategy: SNOW,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Glaceon uses Snowscape only when the snow actually changes the race - it doubles Glaceon's own Speed through Slush Rush, raises the Defense of Ice types, and makes Blizzard never miss, so all three payoffs arrive at once and the turn is worth spending. Octillery attacks immediately. Alternate deployment: if the snow is answered, Seaking's Water Veil makes it immune to the burn a physical attacker fears and Drill Run covers the Rock and Electric bodies that beat the Ice lane. Reserves & disruption: Alomomola's Regenerator and Wacan Berry let it absorb one Electric attack and heal it back on rotation, and Safeguard blocks the status a stall fight would otherwise lose to. Counterplay & audit: Fire, Rock, Steel and Fighting pressure; replacing the weather removes Slush Rush and Blizzard's accuracy in one action; Taunt denies Safeguard and Helping Hand. Could this team be cooler? This is an ordinary route trainer and the roster stays. The concrete judgement: Brenda is the campaign's small, honest statement of manual snow - one setter whose own ability profits from the turn it spends, which is a tidier lesson than the automatic Snow Warning the player meets at Gabrielle, and the two encounters read as a matched pair. The one change worth naming is that Blizzard's perfect accuracy in snow is now stated in the plan, because it is the actual reason the Snowscape turn is not wasted.
crack: Change the weather; Fire, Rock, Steel and Fighting pressure; Taunt the support moves; Glaceon is frail and dies to any priority once the snow is gone.

## E0377 TRAINER_ROGER class=regular
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 2
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Roger fields nothing but crustaceans and the team is built around Adaptability and pincers. Crawdaunt's Adaptability makes Crabhammer and Knock Off hit at 2x STAB rather than 1.5x, so a Swords Dance behind a safe board is lethal; Mega Kingler is the ace and Hyper Cutter means its Attack cannot be lowered by Intimidate. Alternate deployment: Crabominable's Iron Fist boosts Ice Hammer, Drain Punch and Mach Punch - three of its four moves - and Mach Punch is the priority that closes a chipped board. Reserves & disruption: Choice Scarf Dracovish is the surprise; Fishious Rend doubles to 170 base power when Dracovish moves first, which a Scarf guarantees against most of the field, and Strong Jaw boosts it further. Counterplay & audit: Electric and Grass pressure answers every member, Fairy answers the Dark lane, and forcing Dracovish to move second removes most of its damage. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, no - a trainer who owns only crabs is already one of the campaign's most memorable ordinary encounters, and the audit keeps it intact. The concrete judgement: the theme is doing real competitive work rather than being a costume, because three of the four share a physical Water lane that a single resist wall cannot hold, and Dracovish punishes the Choice Scarf the player brings to outrun them. The one thing to preserve deliberately is that Mega Kingler, not Crawdaunt, is the ace - the joke lands harder when the biggest crab wins.
crack: Electric and Grass pressure; Fairy on the Dark lane; slow Dracovish down with Tailwind of your own or a Scarf and Fishious Rend halves; Intimidate is useless against Hyper Cutter.

## E0379 TRAINER_ATHENA class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Athena's team is pure speed and the fight is decided in the first two exchanges. Mega Kangaskhan opens with Fake Out, which Parental Bond makes hit twice - the second hit does not flinch again, but it is free chip damage - while Pheromosa, at 151 base Speed, attacks immediately and Beast Boost raises its highest stat after every knockout. Infernape's Mach Punch is the priority lane. Alternate deployment: Accelgor is the team's honest trick. Final Gambit deals damage equal to Accelgor's current HP and faints it, so at full HP it is a guaranteed removal of almost anything on the player's side; the Bug Gem plus Unburden set means it can instead double its Speed after a single Bug attack. The AI must only spend Final Gambit when Accelgor's actual current HP makes the trade worth it. Counterplay & audit: every member here is frail - Pheromosa has 37 base defences - so any priority move, any spread move and Fake Out of your own all invert the fight; Rock, Flying and Psychic pressure answers the Bug and Fighting lanes. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, no, and the audit keeps it. The concrete judgement: it is the campaign's glass-cannon fight, it owns that identity completely, and Final Gambit gives an ordinary ace trainer one genuinely startling moment that costs the player their best Pokemon if they leave it at full HP. The counterplay is clean - hit first, hit both slots, or hit with priority - and the fight punishes exactly the players who have been winning by out-bulking things.
crack: Priority and spread moves beat the whole team; Fake Out denies the opening; chip Accelgor to make Final Gambit worthless; Rock, Flying and Psychic pressure.

## E0382 TRAINER_KATELYN_1 class=regular
strategy: REDIRECTION,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Butterfree is the whole encounter. Compound Eyes raises Sleep Powder's accuracy to a practical certainty, and Butterfree also carries Rage Powder and Tailwind - so on turn one it either removes a player Pokemon from the game or hands its side a speed window, and on turn two it does the other. Fearow and Zebstrika attack into whichever window it created. Alternate deployment: Zebstrika's Lightning Rod pulls the Electric attacks a Flying-heavy team invites and turns them into +1 Special Attack; Fearow's Intimidate cuts the physical answer on entry. Reserves & disruption: Galarian Corsola with Eviolite and Cursed Body is the stall body - Strength Sap halves the target's Attack and heals Corsola by that amount, and Cursed Body can disable the player's best move outright. Counterplay & audit: Safety Goggles, a Grass typing or Insomnia beats both powder moves at once, spread moves ignore Rage Powder, and Rock and Electric pressure removes the Flying pair. Could this team be cooler? This is an ordinary route trainer and the roster stays. The concrete judgement: Compound Eyes Sleep Powder is one of the few things an ordinary trainer owns that can genuinely end a player's battle on turn one, and pairing it with Rage Powder on the same Pokemon forces a real decision - the player must choose between answering the sleep and answering the redirection, and Butterfree is frail enough that killing it solves both. That is a complete, fair encounter at this level and it does not need a rarer species to be interesting.
crack: Safety Goggles, Grass typing, Insomnia or Vital Spirit beats the powders; spread moves ignore Rage Powder; Rock and Electric pressure; Butterfree dies to almost anything that connects.

## E0383 TRAINER_ALEXA class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Feraligatr's Sheer Force removes the secondary effects of Liquidation and Ice Punch and raises their power by 30 percent in exchange, and Dragon Dance behind a safe board makes it the fast physical threat; Alolan Marowak holds a Thick Club, which doubles its Attack outright and makes Shadow Bone hit like a legendary's attack. Alternate deployment: Mega Diancie is the ace and Diamond Storm has a high chance of raising its Defense by two stages, so it becomes harder to remove the longer it attacks. Reserves & disruption: Technician Scizor's Bullet Punch is boosted by 50 percent and is the priority finish; Harvest Exeggutor recycles its Sitrus Berry and Hypnosis is the disruption; Ampharos' Electroweb is the speed control that lets the slower physical members move first. Counterplay & audit: Knock Off is the single best move against this team - it removes the Thick Club and the Sitrus Berry in one action - and Fire, Fighting, Ground and Steel pressure answers the rest. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, no - the audit keeps it. The concrete judgement: Alexa is the campaign's "item matters" trainer. Thick Club, Sitrus-plus-Harvest, a Mega Stone and a Life Orb are four different ways of turning an item into a stat, and a player who carries Knock Off is rewarded enormously while a player who does not will find Marowak hitting far harder than its listed Attack suggests. The roster is rare without being arbitrary and every member has a stated job.
crack: Knock Off strips the Thick Club and the Berry; Fire, Fighting and Ground pressure; Steel and Water answer Diancie; Taunt denies Hypnosis and Dragon Dance.

## E0384 TRAINER_RUBEN class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Ruben is the campaign's healer, and Meganium is the reason - Triage gives every healing move on it +3 priority, so Heal Pulse restores half of an ally's maximum HP before anything else on the field acts. The opening is therefore Heatmor or Dragonite attacking while Meganium holds the board together from behind a Light Screen. Alternate deployment: Mega Lopunny is the ace and Scrappy lets Fake Out and Close Combat hit Ghost types, which are the usual answer to a Normal-Fighting attacker; Fake Out also buys Dragonite the turn its Dragon Dance needs, and Multiscale means Dragonite survives the first hit at full HP regardless. Reserves & disruption: Milotic's Competitive punishes the Intimidate and Icy Wind the player brings against a physical team with +2 Special Attack; Florges' Flower Veil makes every Grass-typed ally - Meganium - immune to stat drops and status, which protects the healer specifically. Counterplay & audit: Taunt is the key, because it shuts off Heal Pulse, Light Screen and Wish at once; burst damage that outpaces a priority half-heal, Steel and Poison pressure on the Fairy lane, and Ice and Fairy on Dragonite. Could this team be cooler? The old build's ace was Mega Latias, which the audit has now given to Kathleen as the Mossdeep gym's examiner, and the team was one slot short of stating its own idea. It is cooler now because every remaining member exists to keep somebody else alive or to punish the player for interfering: priority healing, a Fairy that blocks stat drops on the healer, a Competitive wall, Multiscale, and a Mega whose Fake Out buys the healer its turn. Fighting a team you cannot chip down is a genuinely different problem from anything else on these routes.
crack: Taunt the Meganium and the whole structure stops; burst damage rather than chip; Fire, Ice, Bug and Flying pressure; Steel and Poison answer Florges.

## E0388 TRAINER_PRESTON class=gym
strategy: TAILWIND,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Conservative
plan: Room one of the Mossdeep observatory: reflection and speed. First two useful turns: Xatu's Magic Bounce returns Taunt, Thunder Wave, Will-O-Wisp, Leech Seed, Spore and every entry hazard straight back at the sender, so Preston's opening is simply to stand there and set Tailwind while the player's support move hits their own side. Espeon is the second Magic Bounce body, so the mirror covers both slots at once. Alternate deployment: Sigilyph holds a Flame Orb and Magic Guard means the burn deals it no damage at all; Psycho Shift then hands that burn to a physical attacker, which is the room's way of crippling a sweeper without using a status move the mirror would have to bounce. Reserves & disruption: Harvest Exeggutor recycles a Sitrus Berry, Grumpig's Thick Fat blunts the Fire and Ice answers, and Alakazam's Magic Guard makes Life Orb, hazards, weather and poison deal it nothing at all until it Mega Evolves into Trace. Counterplay & audit: attack, do not status - every support move the player owns is a liability in this room; Dark types ignore nothing here, but U-turn, Knock Off and raw damage all work. Could this team be cooler? The old room was six Psychics whose first move was the word PSYCHIC, led by a Xatu whose Magic Bounce was never mentioned in the plan. It is cooler now because the room has a rule the player discovers by breaking it: the first status move they use comes back. Simple's Swoobat and Magic Guard's Sigilyph state the gym's thesis early - in this building, abilities matter more than stats.
crack: Do not use status moves; raw damage, U-turn and Knock Off; Dark, Bug and Ghost pressure on the Psychic bodies; Taunt Xatu before Tailwind only if you can stomach it bouncing.

## E0389 TRAINER_VIRGIL class=gym
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: 6
plan: Room two: the gym's one dedicated Trick Room, and deliberately the only room besides the twins' that uses it, so the mechanic stays special. First two useful turns: Bronzong sets the room only when something slow is standing beside it, and its own Gyro Ball is near maximum power at 33 base Speed. Under reversed order everything Virgil owns moves first. Alternate deployment: Mega Medicham is the payoff - Pure Power doubles its Attack outright, and at 100 base Speed it is normally too slow to use that safely, but under Trick Room it is both first and doubled. Fake Out is its opening when the room is already up. Reserves & disruption: Eviolite Dusclops is the bulkiest thing in the gym and Pressure drains the player's PP through the stall; Cofagrigus' Mummy erases the ability of anything that makes contact with it, which turns a physical sweeper into a plain attacker for the rest of the fight; Eviolite Porygon2 with Download picks whichever offensive stat the player is weaker against. Counterplay & audit: Taunt the setter, survive five turns behind Protect, avoid contact with Cofagrigus, and remember Trick Room ends and Virgil's whole team is then the slowest in the building. Could this team be cooler? The old room was a competent Trick Room with Musharna and Claydol doing nothing in particular. It is cooler now because every non-setter is chosen for what it does to the player rather than for being slow: Mummy deletes abilities on contact, Pressure deletes PP, Eviolite Porygon2 refuses to die and Mega Medicham ends the fight. Losing your ability to a coffin is the memory the room leaves.
crack: Taunt or knock out Bronzong; Protect and switch through the five turns; do not make contact with Cofagrigus; Dark, Ghost and Fire pressure.

## E0390 TRAINER_BLAKE class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room three: screens, and the gym's positioning room. First two useful turns: the male Meowstic has Prankster, so Reflect and Light Screen go up with priority before the player's first attack lands - the female Meowstic has Competitive instead, so any attempt to lower her stats hands her +2 Special Attack. Blake therefore opens with a screen and dares the player to Icy Wind or Intimidate the pair. Alternate deployment: Farigiraf's Armor Tail blocks every priority move aimed at Blake's side while it is on the field, so Fake Out, Sucker Punch, Extreme Speed, Aqua Jet and priority Taunt simply fail - behind screens, that removes the two fastest ways to break a defensive setup at once. Reserves & disruption: Mr. Mime and Mr. Rime both carry Ally Switch, so a committed attack can land on the wrong target entirely; Mr. Rime's Screen Cleaner is a deliberate irony the AI must not use on its own screens. Counterplay & audit: Brick Break, Defog and Screen Cleaner-style removal, Taunt from a Dark type or a faster body, and spread moves defeat Ally Switch. Could this team be cooler? The old room had Klefki and Metang bolted onto two screen setters, with no reason for the six to be together. It is cooler now because it is the gym's positioning puzzle rather than its bulk puzzle: priority is switched off by Armor Tail, targeting is unreliable because of two Ally Switch users, and damage is halved by screens. The player has to give up precision and win on raw output.
crack: Break the screens with Brick Break or Defog; use spread moves so Ally Switch cannot save anything; do not lower the female Meowstic's stats; do not rely on priority while Farigiraf is out.

## E0391 TRAINER_HANNAH class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6

crack: Haze the Contrary boosts; remove Rabsca before it revives anything; Fairy, Bug and Ghost pressure; Malamar and Veluza are both slow without support.

## E0392 TRAINER_SAMANTHA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Room five: the trap, where the player stops being allowed to leave. First two useful turns: Wobbuffet's Shadow Tag prevents every non-Ghost opponent from switching out, and it has no attacking move at all - Counter returns double a physical hit, Mirror Coat returns double a special one, and Encore locks the player into whatever they just did. The correct read against Wobbuffet is to do nothing large, which is exactly what Samantha's partners need. Alternate deployment: with the player pinned, Reuniclus takes Calm Mind behind Magic Guard, which means Life Orb recoil, poison, burn, sandstorm and hazards all deal it zero damage, and Swoobat's Simple doubles every stat change so one Calm Mind is +2/+2. Reserves & disruption: Munkidori's Toxic Chain badly poisons on roughly a third of its hits with no move slot spent, which beats the stall the player would otherwise use to outlast the trap; male Indeedee and Azelf are the immediate special pressure. Counterplay & audit: a Ghost-typed member ignores Shadow Tag entirely, Taunt stops the Calm Minds, and Knock Off or a U-turn-free pivot plan is essential before entering. Could this team be cooler? The old room had Gothitelle and Grimmsnarl doing two unrelated jobs. It is cooler now because it is one idea executed without compromise: you cannot switch, and the thing preventing you cannot be safely attacked. Swoobat's Simple is the piece worth pointing at - a Pokemon nobody rates becomes a +2 sweeper in a single turn behind a wall that punishes both attack categories.
crack: Bring a Ghost type and switching still works; do not throw a big attack into Wobbuffet; Taunt the setup turns; Dark and Bug pressure on the Psychic bodies.

## E0393 TRAINER_MAURA class=gym
strategy: PERISH_TRAP,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Room six: the Slowpoke shrine, and the gym's perish trap. First two useful turns: Gothitelle's Shadow Tag locks the player in and Jynx sings Perish Song - three turns later everything on the field that was present when it was sung faints, and the player cannot switch out of it. Maura's own side can switch freely, which is the entire point. Alternate deployment: the four Slowpoke-line members are the clock. Slowking, Galarian Slowking and Galarian Slowbro all carry Regenerator, so each of them heals a third of its maximum HP every time it rotates out, which is how Maura survives the three turns without spending actions on recovery. Mega Slowbro keeps Regenerator-free bulk and is the ace. Reserves & disruption: Galarian Slowking's Curious Medicine resets the player's stat boosts when it enters, which undoes a setup sweeper for free. Counterplay & audit: a Ghost type ignores Shadow Tag and breaks the trap outright, Taunt or Soundproof denies Perish Song, and killing Gothitelle within three turns escapes it. Could this team be cooler? The old room was Jynx, Gothitelle, Amoonguss and three unrelated Water Psychics, so the trap and the bodies had nothing to do with each other. It is cooler now because the room is a shrine to one family: four Slowpoke-line Pokemon rotating on Regenerator behind a trap, which is both a genuinely strong competitive structure and the single most visually coherent room in the gym. The player who brought no Ghost type will remember this one.
crack: A Ghost type ignores Shadow Tag; Taunt or Soundproof denies Perish Song; remove Gothitelle inside three turns; Electric, Grass and Dark pressure on the Slow line.

## E0394 TRAINER_SYLVIA class=gym
strategy: ALLY_COMBO,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
tactic: COMMANDER TATSUGIRI NONE DONDOZO
plan: Room seven: terrain and Commander, the gym's two-body room. First two useful turns: Tatsugiri and Dondozo enter together and Commander fires immediately - Tatsugiri climbs into Dondozo's mouth, becomes untargetable, and Dondozo gains two stages in every stat at once. Only Dondozo acts during the occupied turns, so the room is a single enormous body rather than two, and Order Up gains an extra boost from the Curly form inside it. Alternate deployment: female Indeedee's Psychic Surge covers the floor in Psychic Terrain, which blocks every priority move against grounded targets - the answer a player reaches for against a +2 wall - and turns Expanding Force into a spread attack at 120 base power. Terrain Extender holds it eight turns. Reserves & disruption: Indeedee also has Follow Me, so she can redirect a committed attack away from Dondozo entirely, and After You can hand Dondozo the turn order it lacks. Wyrdeer's Intimidate cuts the physical answers and Musharna's Telepathy makes it immune to spread damage from its own partner. Counterplay & audit: knock out Tatsugiri before they pair, or remove Dondozo with special damage while the boosts are only defensive; Grass and Electric pressure; terrain replacement restores your priority. Could this team be cooler? The old room already had the Commander pair and Indeedee, but Sigilyph and Dusknoir were bolted on with no relation to either engine. It is cooler now because every remaining slot serves the same plan: Follow Me protects the whale, Psychic Terrain switches off the priority that answers it, Intimidate blunts the physical answer and Telepathy makes the partner immune to the collateral. It is the most tightly built room in the gym and the hardest to unpick in one turn.
crack: Remove Tatsugiri on turn one or accept a +2 Dondozo; special attacks bypass its Defense boost; Grass and Electric pressure; replace the terrain to get priority back.

## E0395 TRAINER_NATE class=gym
strategy: ALLY_COMBO,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT DELPHOX
plan: Room eight: Instruct, and the gym's "that attack happened twice" room. First two useful turns: Oranguru uses Instruct on Mega Delphox, which immediately repeats Delphox's last move at full power with no charge, no recoil and no turn spent - two Mystical Fires or two Psychics in one turn, from a Mega special attacker. The AI must only spend Instruct when Delphox has actually already moved this turn and its last move is worth repeating; Instructing a Protect or a failed move is worthless. Alternate deployment: Choice Specs Beheeyem is the other Instruct target, and Analytic adds 30 percent when it moves last, which it usually does - an Instructed Analytic Psychic is the single largest number in the gym outside the leaders. Reserves & disruption: Mimikyu's Disguise guarantees it survives one hit and Swords Dance behind that free turn is a real threat; Uxie sets screens or Yawn; Alolan Exeggutor's Harvest and Dragon lane covers the Dark answers. Counterplay & audit: remove Oranguru first even though Delphox is the scarier body, Taunt denies Instruct, and Dark types are immune to nothing here but resist the Psychic half. Could this team be cooler? The old room was a third Trick Room team with Mega Metagross on top, which duplicated both Virgil's engine and the rival's ace. It is cooler now because Instruct is a mechanic no other trainer in the campaign owns and its effect is instantly legible at the table: the player watches the same attack resolve twice. The counterplay is equally legible - kill the ape, not the fox.
crack: Remove or Taunt Oranguru and the room halves; Dark and Ghost pressure on Delphox and Beheeyem; break Disguise with a spread move before Mimikyu sets up.

## E0396 TRAINER_MACEY class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room nine: the apprentices. First two useful turns: five of Macey's six are unevolved Pokemon holding Eviolite, which raises their Defense and Special Defense by 50 percent each - Metang, Girafarig, Duosion, Kadabra and Elgyem are all individually far bulkier than the player expects, and the room's whole plan is to be un-knockable for long enough that Victini's Victory Star makes the accuracy of every attack on Macey's side 1.3x. Metang opens with Earthquake; Elgyem's Telepathy makes it immune to that Earthquake, which is the room's one clean doubles interaction. Alternate deployment: Duosion's Magic Guard plus Eviolite means Life Orb, poison, hazards and weather do nothing to it while it Calm Minds; Girafarig's Sap Sipper turns the Grass attacks the player brought for a Psychic gym into +1 Attack. Reserves & disruption: Kadabra's Magic Guard and Encore lock a player who spent a turn setting up; Victini is the ace and V-create is the largest single attack any non-leader in the gym owns. Counterplay & audit: Knock Off removes Eviolite and the room collapses; spread moves beat the bulk faster than single targets; Dark, Bug and Ghost pressure. Could this team be cooler? The old room was Ferrothorn, Toxapex, Slowking and Reuniclus - a stall pile shared with three other rooms and no identity. It is cooler now because "the gym's student room, where the students have not evolved yet and are still a serious problem" is a concept the player can read off the screen, because five Eviolite bodies is a genuinely awkward thing to break through, and because Knock Off - a move most players carry and rarely think about - is the exact key to the door.
crack: Knock Off or Trick removes Eviolite and halves the room; spread damage; Dark and Ghost pressure; kill Victini early or every attack against you starts hitting.

## E0397 TRAINER_CLIFFORD class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 5
plan: Room ten: Electric Terrain, the gym's machine room. First two useful turns: Pincurchin's Electric Surge is automatic and Terrain Extender holds it eight turns; the terrain boosts Electric moves by 30 percent, prevents every grounded Pokemon from falling asleep, and blocks priority against grounded targets - so the player's Fake Out, Sucker Punch and Extreme Speed answers to a slow room simply fail. Alolan Raichu's Surge Surfer doubles its Speed in that terrain and it is the room's sweeper. Alternate deployment: Magearna's Soul-Heart raises its Special Attack by one stage every time any Pokemon on the field faints, so a long fight makes it progressively unanswerable, and Fleur Cannon off that stack ends rooms. Reserves & disruption: Aegislash switches to Blade Forme when it attacks and back to Shield Forme with King's Shield, which also cuts the Attack of anything that touched it; Rotom-Mow's Levitate keeps it out of the Ground answers and Leaf Storm covers them. Counterplay & audit: replace the terrain and both the speed and the priority block disappear at once, Ground attacks answer four of the six, and Fire and Fighting pressure removes the Steel bodies. Could this team be cooler? The old room shared Grumpig, Beheeyem and Espathra with three other rooms and never explained why an Electric Pokemon was in a Psychic gym. It is cooler now because the terrain is doing three separate jobs the player can feel - damage, sleep immunity and priority denial - and because Magearna's Soul-Heart makes the room actively harder the better the player is doing. Pincurchin is a deliberately unglamorous engine holding up two of the rarest bodies in the gym. Magearna holds Magearnite: the room's Soul-Heart artillery Mega-Evolves when the terrain damage has already forced a knockout, so the stone rewards the room's own plan rather than replacing it.
crack: Change the terrain; Ground attacks on Pincurchin, Raichu, Aegislash and Magearna; Fire and Fighting pressure on the Steel pair; do not attack into King's Shield.

## E0398 TRAINER_NICHOLAS class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Room eleven: Gravity, and the gym's last idea before the twins. First two useful turns: Sableye has Prankster, so its Gravity goes up with priority on turn one; Bronzor is the backup setter, so removing the Focus Sash imp does not end the room. Under Gravity every move on the field gains roughly 67 percent relative accuracy, every Flying type and every Levitate user is dragged to the ground and becomes hittable by Ground attacks, and Fly, Bounce and Magnet Rise stop working. Every attacking move in this room is one that normally misses. Alternate deployment: Bronzor's Hypnosis is 60 percent accurate in an ordinary battle and close to certain under its own Gravity, and Galarian Mr. Mime's Vital Spirit makes it immune to that sleep, so the pair can fire it into a crowded board without a partner casualty. Galarian Mr. Mime then attacks with Blizzard and Focus Blast - two 70 percent moves that Gravity repairs - behind a Nasty Plot. Reserves & disruption: Necrozma is the ace and Photon Geyser uses whichever of its attacking stats is higher while ignoring the target's ability, which is the one attack in the gym that a Magic Guard, Levitate or Flash Fire answer cannot blunt; Prism Armor also cuts every super effective hit against it by a quarter. Banette's Cursed Body can disable the move the player was relying on and Destiny Bond takes its killer with it; Choice Band Dusknoir supplies the physical Ground lane that Gravity has just made universally relevant. Note that Bronzor runs Heatproof rather than Levitate on purpose - Gravity would switch Levitate off anyway, so the room is built out of Pokemon that lose nothing by being grounded, which is the discipline the old build did not have. Counterplay & audit: Taunt Sableye before the priority Gravity and then remove Bronzor before it re-sets; bring Ground coverage of your own, because everything is grounded for both sides; Dark, Ghost, Fire and Steel pressure answers the room. Could this team be cooler? The version this audit first wrote was cooler than the old Claydol/Malamar/Gothitelle pile it replaced, but it leaned on Jirachi, Necrozma and Latios - three legendaries in a single gym room, which is not "occasional" and which meant the room won on raw stats rather than on its idea. It is cooler now for the same reason it is more honest: with the legendary crutch removed, every remaining slot has to earn itself through Gravity, and two of them are Pokemon nobody respects. An unevolved Eviolite bell setting the field effect and then landing a guaranteed Hypnosis, beside a Galarian mime who is immune to it and throws a never-missing Blizzard, is a far better memory than a Latios firing an accurate Draco Meteor. Necrozma stays because Photon Geyser under Gravity is the room's thesis - the attack that cannot miss and cannot be turned off by an ability - and one legendary ace is exactly the budget a twelfth-of-a-gym room deserves.
crack: Taunt Sableye first and be ready for Bronzor to re-set Gravity; Gravity grounds Nicholas' own team too, so Ground coverage is live for both sides; Dark and Ghost pressure on Sableye, Banette and Dusknoir; Fire, Fighting and Ground on Bronzor; Galarian Mr. Mime is frail and dies to any priority; Necrozma folds to Dark and Bug damage once Prism Armor is worn down.

## E0399 TRAINER_KATHLEEN class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Room twelve: the examiner. Kathleen tests students, so she deliberately fields the same species they do, built the way they should have been built - the player has already seen Espeon, Starmie, Reuniclus, Beheeyem and Medicham in this gym, and every one of them is here with a different item, a different ability choice and a different job. First two useful turns: Espeon's Magic Bounce opens as it did in room one, but here it is a Calm Mind sweeper rather than a screener, and Medicham's Pure Power is used without Trick Room because Kathleen's plan is speed control rather than reversed order. Alternate deployment: Mega Latias is the ace and the only thing in the room the player has not seen; Calm Mind behind Recover and Levitate makes it the most patient win condition in the gym. Reserves & disruption: Analytic Starmie is the late damage, Choice Specs Beheeyem is the immediate damage, and Magic Guard Reuniclus is the body that cannot be chipped. Counterplay & audit: Dark and Ghost pressure across the board, Taunt on the Calm Minds, and Steel or Fairy answers for Mega Latias. Could this team be cooler? The old room was a sand team built around Tyranitar, standing inside a Psychic gym for no reason and duplicating the sand engine that Magma now owns. It is cooler now because Kathleen has an actual character - the examiner who shows you what each of her students should have done - which is a better reason for a species repeat than any of the accidental ones the gym used to have, and because the fight is a straight competitive test with no field effect to hide behind. Asked again after the revision: yes, it earns its slot, because after eleven rooms of gimmicks the last door before the leaders is the one that just plays well.
crack: Dark, Ghost and Bug pressure; Taunt the Calm Mind turns; break Espeon's mirror with damage rather than status; Steel and Fairy answer Mega Latias.
