## E0400 TRAINER_TATE_AND_LIZA_1 class=leader
strategy: TRICK_ROOM,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: The twins are one trainer with two minds and the fight is built so that both halves of every pair matter. First two useful turns: Solrock and Lunatone enter together - the sun and the moon, the gym's emblem - and Solrock sets Trick Room with a Mental Herb so Taunt cannot stop the first attempt, while Lunatone spends a Power Herb on an instant Meteor Beam, which is a 120 base power Rock attack plus a free +1 Special Attack with no charging turn. That is the twins' opening statement: the room reverses and the first attack is already at full power. Alternate deployment: Tapu Lele's Psychic Surge is the second half of the engine. Psychic Terrain blocks all priority against grounded targets, which is the only fast answer to a Trick Room team, and it boosts Expanding Force into a 120 base power spread move for Hatterene and Gardevoir. Hatterene, at 29 base Speed with Magic Bounce, is both the fastest thing on the field under Trick Room and immune to every status move aimed at it. Reserves & disruption: Cresselia is the twins' durability - Lunar Blessing-free Moonlight, Helping Hand and a second Trick Room so the room can be re-set when it lapses. Mega Gardevoir with Pixilate turns Hyper Voice into a Fairy-typed spread attack off an enormous Special Attack stat, under terrain, under Trick Room, moving first. Counterplay & audit: this is the hardest board in the region to unpick with one action - Taunt is answered by Mental Herb and Magic Bounce, priority is answered by Psychic Terrain, and speed is answered by Trick Room. The honest answers are Wide Guard against the spread attacks, Steel and Poison typing against the Fairy lane, Dark types that ignore the Psychic half entirely, and surviving the five turns. Could this team be cooler? The six species were already right - Solrock and Lunatone are the twins' identity and Tapu Lele, Hatterene, Cresselia and Mega Gardevoir are the correct escalation. What was missing was the statement that the two field effects are one machine: Trick Room decides the order and Psychic Terrain removes the only tool that beats it. Asked again after the revision: the leaders are a major step beyond their twelve rooms because they run two of the gym's showcased mechanics simultaneously, at the highest levels in the building, and because every individual answer the player learned in a single room is covered by the other half of the pair here.
crack: Wide Guard and Protect against the spread turns; a Dark type ignores the Psychic lane and Psychic Terrain does not stop its priority-free damage; Steel and Poison answer Pixilate; outlast the five Trick Room turns and the twins are the slowest team in the region.

## E0401 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_3 class=grunt
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The Space Center raid is Magma away from its volcano, so the whole group is a machines-and-fuel strike team rather than the stone-and-magma work crew of the hideout - no member of this raid appears in the hideout and the identity shifts from Rock/Ground to Steel, Electric and Poison. This is the perimeter watch on the ground floor. First two useful turns: Salandit's Corrosion poisons Steel and Poison types, which is the only way this room threatens the Steel walls a player brings to a facility raid, and Fake Out buys Seviper its Coil turn. Coil raises Attack, Defense and accuracy, which is what makes Seviper's Poison Jab and Crunch reliable rather than a coin flip. Alternate deployment: Bastiodon's Sturdy plus Soundproof-free Wide Guard holds the pair of slots while the setup lands, and Metal Burst returns the player's biggest physical hit at 1.5x. Reserves & disruption: Rapidash is the room's speed and its Flame Body punishes contact. Counterplay & audit: Fighting, Ground and Water pressure; spread moves beat the Metal Burst plan by splitting damage; Steel-typed players must respect Corrosion. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Salazzle, Palossand, Amoonguss and Hypno - two of which belong to Courtney and the Magma Hideout, and Hypno belongs to the Psychic gym two chapters earlier. It is cooler now because the perimeter reads as a guard post at a rocket facility: a poison lizard that can rust the player's Steel answer, a coiling snake, a living shield wall that reflects your own biggest hit, and a horse for speed. Metal Burst is the memorable piece - the room wants the player's strongest attack.
crack: Spread damage rather than one big physical hit; Fighting, Ground and Water pressure; Taunt denies Coil; Bastiodon has no recovery and folds to special Ground and Fighting attacks.

## E0402 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_1 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The fuel bay. First two useful turns: Rampardos' Mold Breaker Head Smash is 150 base power off a 165 base Attack stat and Mold Breaker means Sturdy, Solid Rock, Filter and Levitate do not save the target - this is the single hardest-hitting attack any grunt in the campaign owns, and the recoil is real, so the room is a race it wants to win in two turns. Emboar's Reckless boosts Flare Blitz, Double-Edge and Wild Charge, so the pair are a matched set of self-damaging brawlers. Alternate deployment: Arbok's Intimidate cuts the physical answer and Glare paralyses regardless of the target's type, which is how the room survives to swing twice. Reserves & disruption: Honchkrow's Moxie converts any knockout into a snowball and Sucker Punch closes out a chipped board. Counterplay & audit: Rampardos is slow and frail on the special side - Protect and Wide Guard buy the turn its recoil needs, and Fighting, Water, Ground and Fairy all answer the room. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Mightyena, Camerupt, Shiftry and Honchkrow - which is the Mt Chimney Maxie team in miniature, fielded by a grunt three chapters later. It is cooler now because the fuel bay has one legible, frightening idea: two recoil attackers who do not care about their own HP, backed by paralysis so they get the turns they need. Mold Breaker Head Smash ignoring Sturdy also quietly answers every Focus Sash and Sturdy lead the player has been leaning on all raid.
crack: Protect or Wide Guard the Head Smash turn and let the recoil work; Fighting, Water and Ground pressure; Fairy answers Arbok; do not let Honchkrow get a free knockout.

## E0403 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The test-fuel bay, and the strangest room in the raid. First two useful turns: both Paradox members are holding Booster Energy, so Protosynthesis and Quark Drive activate on entry with no weather and no terrain required - Sandy Shocks gets +1 in its highest stat, which is Special Attack, and Iron Thorns gets +1 in Attack, immediately and permanently. Sandy Shocks opens with Thunderbolt or Earth Power; Iron Thorns attacks, or takes Dragon Dance when the board allows, which stacks on top of its Booster boost. Alternate deployment: Gravity from Sandy Shocks is the room's second idea - it grounds every Flying and Levitating Pokemon on the field, raises the accuracy of every move, and turns the player's airborne answer to a Ground-heavy raid into a liability. Reserves & disruption: Klinklang's Shift Gear is +2 Speed and +1 Attack in one action and Clear Body refuses the drops; Eviolite Magneton's Analytic adds 30 percent when it moves last, which is often. Counterplay & audit: Ground pressure answers three of the four, Fighting answers the Steel bodies, and Booster Energy is single-use - it is already spent, so there is nothing left to Knock Off. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Torkoal, Claydol, Rhyperior and Magmortar - the Magma Hideout's sun room and Trick Room room welded together and fielded again. It is cooler now because Magma stealing experimental rocket fuel and feeding it to two Paradox Pokemon is exactly the story the setting wants, Booster Energy makes the ability a visible one-time event rather than a weather dependency, and Gravity at a space centre is the kind of joke that is also a real strategic problem.
crack: Ground attacks answer Sandy Shocks, Klinklang and Magneton; Fighting pressure on the Steel bodies; expect to be grounded, so do not rely on Levitate or a Flying typing; Iron Thorns is slow before Dragon Dance.

## E0404 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_2 class=grunt
strategy: TAILWIND,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The hangar, and the raid's support room. First two useful turns: Klefki's Prankster puts Reflect and Light Screen up before the player's first attack resolves, which means the hangar fight runs at half damage from turn one; its alternative priority action is Thunder Wave on whichever target the crew cannot outrun. Aerodactyl then sets Tailwind or attacks - it is the fastest thing in the raid and Unnerve stops the player's Berries from activating at all. Alternate deployment: Skarmory's Sturdy plus screens is close to unkillable for three turns, and Body Press converts its 140 base Defense, doubled again by Iron Defense, into real damage. Reserves & disruption: Obstagoon's Guts turns the status the room's own Thunder Wave-heavy plan invites into a 50 percent Attack boost, and Obstruct cuts the Defense of anything that hits into it. Counterplay & audit: Brick Break and Defog remove the screens, Taunt beats Prankster only if you are faster or Dark-typed, and Fire, Electric and Fighting pressure answer the Steel bodies. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Flygon, Crobat, Drapion and Klefki - three Poison and Flying bodies from the hideout with one screener bolted on. It is cooler now because it is honestly a support room rather than four attackers: screens, Tailwind, paralysis and an Unnerve that quietly switches off the player's Sitrus and Lum Berries for the whole fight. Berry denial at exactly the point in the campaign where the player has learned to rely on Berries is the memorable, slightly cruel idea.
crack: Break the screens with Brick Break or Defog, or simply outlast five turns; a Dark-typed lead ignores Prankster; Fire and Electric pressure on Skarmory and Klefki; Fighting on Obstagoon.

## E0407 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_7 class=grunt

## E0408 TRAINER_TABITHA_MOSSDEEP class=admin

The old build had Steelix, Gliscor and Darmanitan bolted onto the sand core - three species the Magma Hideout grunts already own, so the admin read as a grunt with better levels. It is cooler now because it reads as a person: Gigalith, Excadrill, Coalossal and Krookodile are Tabitha's, the player has fought them before, and the fight is about what he added rather than who he is. Magnet Pull returning as his trapping habit is the character detail worth keeping.
crack: Weather replacement halves Mega Excadrill; one large hit on Coalossal or none at all; keep a non-Steel pivot available; Water, Grass, Fighting and Ice pressure.

## E0409 TRAINER_MAXIE_MOSSDEEP class=leader
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: 3
ai: Ace Pokemon

## E0410 TRAINER_SOPHIA class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a Grassy Terrain dance troupe. Tapu Bulu's Grassy Surge is the stage; Bellossom dances on it and Oricorio's Dancer copies every dance the instant it is performed. First two useful turns: Bellossom opens with Quiver Dance or Petal Dance while Tapu Bulu attacks; Oricorio's Dancer copies the dance for free, so one turn buys two boosted special attackers. Turn two spends the real boosts instead of stacking a third dance. Alternate deployment: when powder or Fire pressure denies the troupe, Mudsdale's Stamina/Body Press mode and Tapu Bulu's Horn Leech carry the fight with no setup. Reserves & disruption: Grassy Terrain heals the grounded members and halves Earthquake; Oricorio is airborne and gains none of it. Dancer copies dances only, never Horn Leech or Revelation Dance. Petal Dance locks Bellossom for two to three turns and confuses it afterwards; that cost is real. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it now is: the old Contrary Lurantis was a solo stat-trick that Oricorio's Dancer could never touch, so the team's signature ability did nothing. Replacing it with Bellossom - a rare, literally dancing Grass mon with both Quiver Dance and Petal Dance - turns Dancer from decoration into the team's engine and gives the player a real decision (stop the dance on turn one or race two snowballing special attackers). Healer also quietly cures Tapu Bulu's status. Overrepresented? Lurantis is retained by Vito (E0487) so nothing is lost; Bellossom and Oricorio-Pom-Pom appear nowhere else in the campaign, and Mudsdale's other owner Herman (E0422) is retooled to Copperajah. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2 - the Mudsdale anchor pays for the two fragile dancers.
crack: Break the dance on turn one: Taunt or Encore Bellossom, or knock it out before Oricorio can copy. Fire, Flying and Ice attacks punish the whole troupe, Haze erases both copies at once, and terrain replacement removes Bulu's healing. Mudsdale folds to special attacks despite the Assault Vest.

BELLOSSOM @FOCUS_SASH HEALER MODEST 4/0/0/252/0/252 1 | QUIVER_DANCE, PETAL_DANCE, GIGA_DRAIN, PROTECT | ivs=31/0/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

## E0412 TRAINER_SEBASTIAN class=ace
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a clean redirection-and-finisher pair backed by two very different walls. First two useful turns: Amoonguss uses Rage Powder only when it protects a Swords Dance or a Technician Bullet Punch worth protecting, and Spore when a legal non-Grass, non-Overcoat target is up; Scizor either dances or attacks. Turn two cashes the boost with Bullet Punch or Bug Bite rather than building a second time. Alternate deployment: Amoonguss plus Donphan is the no-setup mode, trading Rage Powder turns for immediate High Horsepower and Knock Off. Reserves & disruption: Dunsparce is the endgame - Coil raises Attack, Defence and accuracy while Eviolite gives it Dudunsparce-grade bulk it does not otherwise have, and Serene Grace doubles Body Slam's paralysis. Rapid Spin needs real hazards or a valued Speed boost, not automatic utility scoring. Could this team be cooler? The concept is sound and stays, but the Leftovers Dunsparce was a slow wall that could never finish anything. Giving it Eviolite is the smallest change that makes the third phase genuinely threatening: a Coil-boosted Eviolite Dunsparce with 60% paralysis Body Slam is a memorable, faintly absurd wall-breaker and exactly the kind of underdog the campaign wants, and it asks the player to carry a Fighting or special answer rather than out-muscling it. Overrepresented? Scizor, Donphan and Dunsparce are unique in the campaign; Amoonguss's other nearby owner Makayla (E0435) is rebuilt around Abomasnow, so this is now the only Rage Powder team in the stretch. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Fire attacks erase Scizor and Amoonguss together, Safety Goggles or a Grass type ignores the powder, and spread damage goes around Rage Powder entirely. Taunt shuts Coil and Swords Dance; without priority Scizor is slow. Dunsparce cannot outlast a Fighting attacker.

## E0420 TRAINER_SANTIAGO class=regular
strategy: SNOW
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a tide-pool angler whose snow arrives with the lead, not three switches later. First two useful turns: Vanilluxe now leads, so Snow Warning is live before the first attack and its Blizzard is accurate from turn one; Gastrodon attacks or recovers behind it. Turn two chooses between Freeze-Dry into a Water target and Flash Cannon into a Rock or Fairy one. Alternate deployment: Poliwrath and Dewgong are the physical/control second wave - Encore punishes a repeated setup or protect, and Icy Wind keeps the speed advantage the snow team wants. Reserves & disruption: Storm Drain and Water Absorb solve different problems and neither is a general damage immunity; Gastrodon draws opposing single-target Water attacks, Poliwrath only absorbs what actually hits it. Could this team be cooler? The species are a good, unusual set for a coastal regular, but the team advertised SNOW while keeping its only weather setter on the bench, so its signature never happened and Blizzard was a coin flip. Leading Vanilluxe costs nothing and turns a mislabelled team into a real two-phase snow fight; Dewgong's illegal Freeze-Dry becomes Ice Beam so the set is honest. That is the whole improvement this trainer needs - a route angler should not also be a six-slot showcase. Overrepresented? Vanilluxe is unique; Dewgong's only other owner is Daphne's Sootopolis ice room (E0468), and Poliwrath's are Hudson (E0448) and Tiffany, all in distinct roles. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/0/1/1.
crack: Replace the weather and Blizzard becomes a liability; Grass and Electric attacks split the team in half; Taunt stops Encore and Recover. Gastrodon's Storm Drain is useless against spread Water and against anything that is not Water at all.

## E0422 TRAINER_HERMAN class=ace
strategy: TRICK_ROOM,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: a genuine Trick Room crew in which every member is deliberately, correctly slow. First two useful turns: Bronzong sets Trick Room when the reversed order actually helps - Mental Herb protects that turn from Taunt - while Gourgeist attacks or burns the biggest physical threat. Turn two spends the room on damage, not on a second support action. Alternate deployment: Bronzong plus Mega Crabominable is the heavy mode, with Iron Fist Ice Hammer and Drain Punch hitting under the reversed order. Reserves & disruption: Guts Heracross converts its Toxic Orb into Facade and Close Combat, Copperajah's Heavy Metal doubles its weight for Heavy Slam, and Florges is a separate Calm Mind/Wish endgame with Flower Veil protecting the team from stat drops. Body Press still reads the user's real Defence. Could this team be cooler? It already had a good idea and a bad implementation: every slow member carried a 31 Speed IV, so the room was fighting its own team. Zeroing Speed IVs on all five Brave/Relaxed/Quiet members is the difference between a Trick Room team and a team with a Trick Room move, and it is the single most valuable change here. Swapping the duplicated Mudsdale for Copperajah keeps the same slow-anvil role while giving the trainer a rarer, heavier signature that actually rewards Heavy Slam, and Mega Crabominable stays as the payoff nobody else in the campaign fields. Overrepresented? Copperajah, Crabominable and Gourgeist are unique; Bronzong and Gothitelle-style room setters are spaced far apart (Conor E0442, Warren E0439 use different setters). Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Taunt the setter, or simply be slower - a Trick Room of your own flips the whole fight. Fire and Flying attacks shred Gourgeist, Heracross and Copperajah; Haze undoes Coil-free boosts; and after five turns the room ends while the team is still built to be slow.

## E0423 TRAINER_SUSIE class=ace
strategy: TAILWIND,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a high-altitude gale team built around the campaign's only Mega Pidgeot - No Guard Hurricane that simply cannot miss. First two useful turns: Arcanine opens with Intimidate and immediate Extreme Speed or Flare Blitz while Corviknight sets Tailwind or attacks; the team wants the speed window before the Mega arrives. Turn two uses the actual speed order rather than spending a second support turn. Alternate deployment: Probopass's Wide Guard and Magnet Pull trap-and-shield mode supports Sceptile's White Herb Unburden line, which doubles Sceptile's Speed the moment Leaf Storm's drop is cancelled. Reserves & disruption: Volcarona is the Quiver Dance alternative with Heavy-Duty Boots; Corviknight's Mirror Armor reflects stat drops back at the attacker, including Intimidate. Mega Pidgeot's No Guard cuts both ways - opposing Focus Blast and Hypnosis also cannot miss it. Could this team be cooler? It is much cooler now. The old version flagged SNOW with no snow setter and handed Mega Froslass to a route ace even though Glacia already owns that exact Mega in the League, so the trainer's showcase was both a lie and a duplicate. Rebuilding her around Mega Pidgeot gives her the one Mega nobody else in the campaign has, makes the pre-existing custom No Guard the point of the team instead of a curiosity, and Corviknight's Mirror Armor adds a genuinely memorable interaction - the player's Intimidate lead gets its own drop thrown back. Overrepresented? Mega Pidgeot, Corviknight and Probopass's Wide Guard role are unique here; Froslass is returned to Glacia and Dianne, and Volcarona is removed from Katelynn (E0481) so this is its only appearance in the stretch. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Rock and Electric attacks answer the whole flying core, and Mega Pidgeot's No Guard makes it a legal target for your own shaky moves. Taunt stops Tailwind and Quiver Dance; Wide Guard is useless against single targets, so pick Probopass apart directly. Sceptile is frail before Unburden and slow if White Herb is knocked off first.

## E0424 TRAINER_KARA class=regular
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a groomer's show team - Normal-type polish hiding one genuinely dangerous Huge Power hitter. First two useful turns: Miltank uses Helping Hand only when Azumarill's Play Rough or Aqua Jet actually crosses a knockout threshold, and Scrappy Body Slam otherwise, since Scrappy lets it hit Ghost targets that expect an immunity. Turn two must not Milk Drink a healthy slot while the real threat is falling. Alternate deployment: Furfrou's Fur Coat bulk, Thunder Wave and U-turn set up Whiscash's Dragon Dance, which is the team's only way to outpace anything. Reserves & disruption: Clear Amulet protects Azumarill's stats from Intimidate, not from Haze, burns or damage. Furfrou's Kabuki trim is cosmetic and must not be read as a separate strategic form. Could this team be cooler? Honest answer: only slightly, and it should not be. This is a mid-route regular whose job is to punish a player who has stopped reading sets - Scrappy Body Slam through a Ghost switch-in and a Dragon Dance Whiscash are exactly the right size of surprise for the slot, and Huge Power Azumarill already demands a real answer. Inflating it with a legend or a Mega would blur the line between this fight and the aces around it. Levels: cap 76, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Grass and Electric attacks split the team; Fighting hits Miltank and Furfrou hard. Deny Aqua Jet's target and Azumarill is slow. Taunt stops Helping Hand, Milk Drink and Thunder Wave at once, and Whiscash needs a free turn it rarely gets.

## E0430 TRAINER_RONALD class=ace
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: 5
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a fisherman who genuinely believes in his Magikarp, surrounded by the rain team that makes the joke almost work. First two useful turns: Kingdra sets Rain Dance only when its own Swift Swim or Gyarados's pressure repays the turn, otherwise it opens with Hydro Pump or Draco Meteor; Gyarados attacks after Intimidate or dances when the board is safe. Turn two uses the real weather and speed order. Alternate deployment: Raichu's Fake Out and Lightning Rod hold the field for Kingdra before a deliberately timed Mega, and Lightning Rod draws the Electric attack the Water core fears. Reserves & disruption: Magikarp is a genuine novelty slot - under rain its Swift Swim doubles its Speed and Flail from a Focus Sash-preserved 1 HP is a real, ridiculous knockout; it is not a sixth competitive set and must not be scored as one. Tentacruel covers Poison and speed control. Mega Raichu's ability replaces Lightning Rod, so the redirection stops the moment it transforms. Could this team be cooler? It is already one of the campaign's most characterful ideas and the answer is to protect it rather than replace it: the memorable interaction here is rain plus Focus Sash plus Flail, and a player who ignores Magikarp genuinely loses a Pokemon to it. Adding a legend or a second Mega would kill the joke. The only thing worth tightening is the level spread so the surrounding five are actually threatening enough that the player cannot afford to spend two turns laughing. Overrepresented? Kingdra is also Linda's (E0438) and Juan's ace, but this is the only Rain Dance/Life Orb version; Gyarados's other owners are an Aqua grunt and Juan's Mega; Mega Raichu Y appears only here, with Vito holding Raichu X. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/0/1/2.
crack: Replace the weather and both the sweeper and the joke die. Grass and Electric attacks cover the whole roster, spread damage breaks Magikarp's Sash before it can Flail, and Gyarados is one Rock Slide or Thunderbolt from gone. Deny Fake Out with your own and Raichu has no safe turn.

## E0432 TRAINER_PAXTON class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a bare-knuckle Fighting quartet with three entirely different ways to win - sleep, Guts, and a Body Press wall. First two useful turns: Breloom uses Spore only on a legal, non-Grass, non-Overcoat, non-Safety-Goggles target; otherwise Mach Punch or Technician-boosted Bullet Seed is the better opening while Ursaring takes its Flame Orb turn or attacks. Turn two converts Guts Facade and priority rather than hunting a second sleep. Alternate deployment: Luxray's Intimidate and Snarl grind both offensive stats down so Kommo-o can Iron Defence and start returning Body Press damage. Reserves & disruption: Technician boosts each individual Bullet Seed hit but does not fix the hit count the way Skill Link would; Rock Tomb's Speed drop is the set's real speed control. Could this team be cooler? The three win conditions are distinct and the sleep/Guts/wall split is genuinely good design for a route ace, so the composition earns retention. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Safety Goggles, a Grass type or Insomnia turns off Spore entirely. Fairy and Flying attacks answer three of the four; Taunt stops Iron Defence; and Bulletproof does not save Kommo-o from a physical Fairy or Dragon hit. Remove the Flame Orb before it activates and Ursaring is merely slow.

## E0433 TRAINER_DARCY class=ace
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
tactic: AFTER_YOU LILLIGANT AFTER_YOU TORKOAL
plan: Concept: the advanced student's answer to Flannery - the same After You plus Eruption idea, but with a second sun setter so the player cannot simply erase the weather once. First two useful turns: the authored tactic makes Lilligant offer After You to Torkoal when that actually lands a full-power Eruption before the opposing threats move; otherwise Lilligant uses Sleep Powder or Leaf Storm and Torkoal attacks in its own slow order. Turn two rechecks Torkoal's current HP, because Eruption scales with it, and whether the ally has already acted - After You is worthless then. Alternate deployment: Lilligant's sleep and support enable Porygon-Z's Adaptability Tri Attack or a Nasty Plot turn when the sun has been taken away. Reserves & disruption: Mega Charizard Y is the second real Drought, so Lilligant's manual Sunny Day is an emergency reclaim, not mandatory redundancy; Chlorophyll only doubles Lilligant's Speed while sun is actually up. Could this team be cooler? The idea was already strong but it was not executable - the block described an After You engine that no tactic line told the AI to perform, so in play it was a generic sun team. Adding the explicit AFTER_YOU tactic is what turns the concept into the fight the prose promises, and the weather-reclaim second setter is exactly the escalation a post-Flannery sun trainer should have. Beyond that it does not need rarer species; Porygon-Z is the one member a player never prepares for, and that is the fight's real teeth. Overrepresented? Torkoal and Lilligant recur with Flannery and Julie (E0490), which is deliberate - this trainer is explicitly a variation on that theme - but the two sun sources, the Focus Sash Lilligant and the Adaptability pivot make the board play differently. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Take the sun and keep taking it; Sleep Powder fails through Safety Goggles and Grass types. Taunt kills After You, Sunny Day and Nasty Plot together. Wide Guard blanks Eruption and Heat Wave, and Torkoal is helpless once it has been chipped.

## E0434 TRAINER_JONATHAN class=ace
strategy: TAILWIND
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: fossil speed - a Sheer Force glass cannon that only works inside the Tailwind window its partner opens. First two useful turns: Aerodactyl sets Tailwind or Taunts the opposing support while Rampardos attacks; Focus Sash means Aerodactyl survives to do at least one of those. Turn two spends the speed window on damage instead of adding more support. Alternate deployment: base Sableye's Prankster Fake Out and Will-O-Wisp protect Rampardos through a turn before a deliberate Mega transition. Reserves & disruption: Sawsbuck's Sap Sipper gives a free Attack boost against Grass attacks and a Clear Amulet against Intimidate; Mega Sableye loses Prankster, so the priority support and the Mega ability can never be counted at the same time. Sheer Force applies only to moves with secondary effects - Zen Headbutt and Fire Punch, not Rock Slide's flinch-free damage - and it cancels Life Orb recoil on those moves. Could this team be cooler? The Sheer Force plus Life Orb recoil-cancel interaction is a genuinely satisfying thing for a player to discover mid-fight, and the base-Prankster-versus-Mega decision is a real choice the AI has to make, so the concept holds. What it needed was pressure: Rampardos folds to a single resisted hit, so the fight only becomes memorable if the Tailwind turn is actually lethal, which the raised offsets provide. A rarer fossil would not improve it - Rampardos's absurd Attack stat is the point. Overrepresented? Aerodactyl recurs with Roxanne's Mega and Hope (E0479) but never with this Taunt/Sash utility role; Rampardos, Sawsbuck and Mega Sableye are unique in this stretch. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Any priority move beats the Tailwind plan, and Water, Grass and Steel attacks answer the fossils. Wide Guard blunts Rock Slide; Taunt stops Tailwind and Will-O-Wisp; and if Rampardos misses once it usually does not get a second turn.

## E0435 TRAINER_MAKAYLA class=ace
strategy: SNOW,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a snowfield brawler - Abomasnow brings the weather with it, and Beartic's Slush Rush turns that weather into raw speed on the very first turn. First two useful turns: Abomasnow's Snow Warning is active on entry, so Beartic is already fast; Abomasnow attacks with Wood Hammer or Ice Shard while Medicham opens with Fake Out or a Pure Power Close Combat. Turn two uses the real speed order under snow rather than spending a turn establishing weather that already exists. Alternate deployment: Medicham and Flareon are the weather-independent physical mode - Pure Power and Guts Facade both hit hard with no setup at all. Reserves & disruption: snow raises the Defence of Ice types, so Abomasnow and Beartic are tougher than their listed stats suggest; Flareon's Toxic Orb Guts mode gains nothing from the weather and Charti Berry cushions exactly one Rock attack on Beartic. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the fix was structural rather than cosmetic. The authored version flagged SNOW while relying on Beartic spending a turn on Snowscape, so the team's own Slush Rush sweeper had to waste the turn it needed to sweep, and the redirecting Amoonguss duplicated Sebastian's team two encounters earlier. Abomasnow solves both at once: automatic snow, a second Ice attacker, a Grass answer to the Water and Ground types that wall the rest, and Ice Shard priority that pairs with Medicham's Fake Out to steal turns. The memorable interaction is now the double opening - Fake Out plus Ice Shard removing a Focus Sash lead before it acts. Overrepresented? Abomasnow's other owner Dianne (E0492) uses a Quiet special Blizzard set in Victory Road, so the physical version here is clearly distinct; Beartic, Medicham and Flareon are unique in the stretch, and Amoonguss now belongs solely to Sebastian (E0412). Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Fire and Fighting attacks hit the whole team, and replacing the weather strips Beartic's Speed and the Ice-type Defence bonus at once. Medicham and Beartic are both fragile - one resisted hit each - and Fake Out only works on the turn they enter.

## E0438 TRAINER_LINDA class=regular
strategy: RAIN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 1
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a squall on the open water - Mega Kingdra calls the rain and three very different bodies exploit it. First two useful turns: Kingdra sets Rain Dance or opens with Muddy Water, which is spread and therefore cannot be stolen by an ally, while Lumineon attacks or sets Tailwind. Turn two stacks Swift Swim on top of Tailwind for a speed tier nothing at this stage matches. Alternate deployment: Araquanid's Wide Guard and Water Bubble mode covers Arctozolt, whose Bolt Beak doubles in power whenever it moves before its target - which is precisely what the rain and Tailwind are for. Reserves & disruption: Sticky Web is a long-term investment and must beat an immediate attack to be worth the turn; Water Bubble doubles Araquanid's Water damage and halves incoming Fire. Could this team be cooler? It is cooler now for one concrete reason. Lumineon previously ran Storm Drain, which meant the team's own single-target Water attacks were being pulled away from their targets by their own partner - a real strategic defect, not a stylistic one. Switching it to Swift Swim removes the self-sabotage and makes Lumineon the second rain abuser the RAIN tag always implied, so the fight now reads as one coherent squall instead of three unrelated Water mons. The Bolt Beak plus double speed control interaction is the thing a player will remember, and it is worth the Mega slot. Overrepresented? Mega Kingdra exists only here; Lumineon's other owner Connie runs the Storm Drain version deliberately, in the one gym room built to use it. Araquanid and Arctozolt are unique in the stretch. Levels: cap 76, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Grass and Electric attacks cover the whole team and Ground types are immune to Bolt Beak's best turn. Replace the weather or win the speed war with priority and the Bolt Beak plan collapses; Wide Guard only stops spread moves, so attack Araquanid directly.

## E0439 TRAINER_WARREN class=ace
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: two Trick Room setters and four deliberately slow bodies, ending in a Mega Drampa that fires at full power under a room somebody else paid for. First two useful turns: Musharna sets Trick Room or Helping Hands a useful Alolan Golem attack; Galvanize converts Golem's Double-Edge into a same-power Electric attack with STAB, which also means it does nothing at all to a Ground target. Turn two spends the reversed order rather than re-casting the room. Alternate deployment: Cofagrigus is the backup setter for the second half, holding the room open for Mega Drampa's Hyper Voice and Draco Meteor. Reserves & disruption: Torterra and Aggron are the slow physical coverage; Mummy replaces the ability of anything that makes contact with Cofagrigus, which can silently turn off a player's Intimidate, Guts or Multiscale. Telepathy protects Musharna from a partner's spread move, not from opponents. Could this team be cooler? The two-setter structure and the Galvanize trick were good; what made the team weaker than it looked was that every slow member carried a 31 Speed IV, so the room it worked to set was partly wasted on its own side. Zeroing Speed on all five Brave/Quiet/Sassy members is the change that makes the concept function, and Cofagrigus's Mummy is now worth calling out as the signature interaction - a player whose Intimidate lead punches it loses the ability for the rest of the fight. The species set is already unusual enough for a route ace and does not need replacing. Overrepresented? Alolan Golem, Drampa, Cofagrigus and Musharna are unique in the campaign; Torterra recurs only with Owen (E0488) in Victory Road, and the neighbouring room teams (Herman E0422, Conor E0442) use entirely different setters and payoffs. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Taunt the setter, or bring your own Trick Room and reverse the reversal. Ground types ignore Galvanize entirely; Fighting and Fire attacks answer the slow core. Wait five turns and the room ends while the team is still built to be slow.

## E0441 TRAINER_MOLLIE class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a haunted-cold quartet with no weather and no setup - four immediate threats and one very deliberate accuracy gamble. First two useful turns: Gengar attacks or uses Icy Wind for speed control while Carracosta attacks or selects Wide Guard against a genuine spread threat. Turn two acts on the resulting order instead of assuming every attack is now safe. Alternate deployment: Aromatisse's Helping Hand and Wish support Rotom-Frost's Blizzard and Thunderbolt coverage; Aroma Veil protects the pair from Taunt, Encore and Attract but not from damage or ordinary status. Reserves & disruption: Rotom-Frost's Blizzard is its form's signature attack and there is no snow in this team, so it is a knowingly inaccurate 110-power gamble - the Wide Lens is the honest fix rather than pretending a weather source exists. Rock Gem is a single-use Rock Slide amplifier on Carracosta and Sturdy guarantees it survives one hit to use it. Could this team be cooler? The unusual part - a Ghost, a fossil turtle, a perfumer and an appliance ghost with no shared engine - is actually the point: this is a fight about four independent threats rather than one combo, which is a welcome change of pace between two Trick Room aces. The change it needed was honesty about Blizzard. Wide Lens is a small, legal, in-character fix that makes the gamble a real 91% threat rather than a coin flip, and it keeps Rotom-Frost's identity intact instead of replacing a rare form with a safer one. Overrepresented? Rotom-Frost, Carracosta's Rock Gem set and Aromatisse are unique; Gengar's other campaign owners are Samuel - retooled to Mismagius in this pass - Leaf's Kanto team and Phoebe's Mega, so this remains the only Focus Sash Icy Wind version. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Dark, Ground and Rock attacks hit three of the four, and Aromatisse's Aroma Veil does not stop damage. Single-target attacks walk around Wide Guard; Wish only pays out at the end of the following turn, so kill the recipient first. Blizzard can still miss.

## E0446 TRAINER_AARON class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND

First two useful turns: Noivern chooses Tailwind when it creates a real Tyrantrum turn, or Super Fang to halve a wall that nothing else on the team can break; Tyrantrum attacks. ## E0448 TRAINER_HUDSON class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a sailor who beats weather teams by refusing to have any - three different absorption abilities and a Cloud Nine switch that turns everyone else's engine off. First two useful turns: Poliwrath attacks while Raticate either attacks or takes its Flame Orb turn; turn two often U-turns into whichever immunity the opposing board has just revealed it needs. Alternate deployment: Cloud Nine Golduck and Volt Absorb Lanturn are the weather-suppression mode - while Golduck is on the field neither side gets rain, sun, sand or snow benefits, which is a real answer to the rain teams that dominate this stretch of coast. Reserves & disruption: Water Absorb, Volt Absorb and Cloud Nine solve three different problems and none of them is general damage reduction; Cloud Nine suppresses weather effects while active but does not clear the weather, so it returns the moment Golduck leaves. ## E0453 TRAINER_GRUNT_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN_2 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the second landing - a bruiser who buys one Shell Smash, and a poison-reef reserve that does not care whether the smash happened. First two useful turns: Scrafty's Fake Out buys Huntail a survivable Shell Smash, or Scrafty simply attacks when the boost cannot survive; White Herb immediately undoes the Defence and Special Defence drops, so the smash is far less suicidal than it looks. Turn two uses the real boosts, or pivots when opposing priority would end the threat anyway. Alternate deployment: Scrafty's Intimidate and Assault Vest let it front for Nihilego's immediate special coverage when no setup window exists. Reserves & disruption: Muk supplies Helping Hand and heavy physical Poison pressure, and Poison Touch can poison anything it strikes. Beast Boost raises Nihilego's highest stat - Special Attack - after each knockout, so letting it get one free kill is expensive. Water Veil blocks burns only, not the Defence drops or ordinary status. Could this team be cooler? The composition is already good faction design - a thug, a deep-sea gamble, a sludge tank and a stolen Ultra Beast reads exactly like a pirate crew - and the Fake Out into White Herb Shell Smash line is a real, executable two-card combo rather than a described one, so it earns retention as authored. The improvement it needed is escalation: this is the room after the boarding party and before Shelly, so its levels rise a step and its Ultra Beast is what the player should remember. Overrepresented? Nihilego, Huntail's Water Veil set and Muk are unique in the Aqua arc; Scrafty recurs only as Leroy's Mega far into the post-game. Levels: cap 84, offsets 1/1/1/1.
crack: Taunt or a faster attacker stops the smash before White Herb matters; priority removes Huntail after it boosts. Ground and Psychic attacks answer Muk and Nihilego, Grass and Electric cover the Water half, and Fake Out can be denied with your own.

## E0454 TRAINER_SHELLY_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN class=admin
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: Archie's lieutenant and the cavern's first real wall - she brings the rain the boss will inherit, and every member of her crew is built to use it. First two useful turns: Politoed's Drizzle is live on entry and Damp Rock stretches it to eight turns; it supports Choice Specs Keldeo with Helping Hand or slows the board with Icy Wind while Keldeo fires. Turn two respects Keldeo's lock and the actual weather state rather than assuming both. Alternate deployment: Swift Swim Ludicolo is the rain sweeper and answers the Grass and Water types that wall the rest; Hisuian Qwilfish fronts with Intimidate and Eviolite bulk while Barb Barrage doubles in power against an already-poisoned target. Reserves & disruption: Crawdaunt is the Adaptability priority reserve whose Aqua Jet ignores the speed war entirely, and Mega Dragalge is the special Dragon/Poison lane with Adaptability behind it. Secret Sword deals physical damage using Keldeo's Special Attack, which is why the Specs set works; Justified does not make it a physical attacker. Could this team be cooler? Yes - and the version that reaches the player now is. Clefable's Follow Me was a competent but entirely non-Aqua slot: a Fairy support in a pirate hideout, doing a job the faction has no business doing. Hisuian Qwilfish replaces it with something that reads as Shelly's own prized fish, keeps an Intimidate body in the lead slot, and sets up its own Barb Barrage payoff through Muk's and Dragalge's poison. The result is six water/dark/poison bodies, one weather, and three separate ways to convert it - which is what an admin fight should be before the boss does the same thing bigger. Overrepresented? Politoed's Drizzle recurs with Juan four encounters later, which is intentional contrast - Shelly uses rain for burst, Juan uses it for attrition - and Archie now runs Pelipper instead, so the three rain bosses use three different setters. Keldeo, Crawdaunt and Mega Dragalge are unique in the campaign. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/3/3/2/3/3 - a clear step above the grunt rooms and below Archie.
crack: Take the rain away and half the roster slows down; Grass and Electric attacks hit almost everything. Keldeo is locked into one move, so bait the wrong one. Wide Guard blanks Muddy Water and Icy Wind, Haze erases Swift Swim's benefit indirectly by removing the boosts it enables, and Hisuian Qwilfish folds to Ground and Psychic attacks.

## E0455 TRAINER_GRUNT_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN_5 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the brig - past Shelly, the crew gets nastier: two separate physical setup threats behind a genuinely obnoxious poison wall. First two useful turns: Mandibuzz sets Tailwind or Taunts the opposing support while Gyarados attacks after Intimidate, or dances when the board is safe; Lum Berry covers exactly one paralysis or burn attempt aimed at the dance. Turn two spends the speed or the boost instead of adding a second setup on the same side. Alternate deployment: Toxapex's Regenerator, Baneful Bunker and Haze mode is the attrition lead - Baneful Bunker protects and poisons whatever makes contact, and Haze erases the player's boosts as well as its own team's. Reserves & disruption: Skuntank supplies Sucker Punch priority and Aftermath damage when it faints; Overcoat makes Mandibuzz immune to powder and weather chip; Foul Play uses the target's own Attack, so it punishes a physical setup sweeper hardest. Could this team be cooler? It is cooler for a structural reason rather than a species-rarity one. The authored version had Arbok's Coil as a third setup plan, duplicating Maxie's Mt. Chimney Arbok exactly and giving the room three slow boosters that could never all be used. Toxapex replaces it with the thing the raid actually lacked - a wall the player cannot simply out-damage - and Haze turns the room into a genuine trap for a player who has been winning these fights by setting up. The memorable moment is a Baneful Bunker that poisons a contact attacker on the same turn it blanks the attack. Overrepresented? Toxapex recurs with Juan's gym in a Wide Guard support role rather than this Haze/Bunker one; Mandibuzz, Skuntank and this Lum Berry Gyarados are otherwise unique, and Arbok is returned to Maxie alone. Levels: cap 84, offsets 1/2/2/2.
crack: Electric, Fairy and Ground attacks answer the roster; Psychic hits Toxapex hard. Taunt stops Tailwind, Dragon Dance and Haze at once. Do not feed Foul Play by boosting Attack, and beware that killing Skuntank costs you HP.

## E0456 TRAINER_GRUNT_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN_3 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the whale room - the cavern's artillery piece. Wailord brings its own storm and fires a Water Spout that is only terrifying while it is still healthy. First two useful turns: Wailord establishes rain on entry and chooses Water Spout from its execution-time HP, which is why the player must damage it before it acts rather than after; Seaking attacks under Swift Swim, already doubled by its partner's weather. Turn two switches to Hydro Pump once Wailord's HP has fallen far enough that Spout is the weaker option. Alternate deployment: Drapion and Morpeko are the independent physical and control mode that does not care about weather - Sniper turns Drapion's Scope Lens critical hits into genuine removals, and Parting Shot drops both of a target's attacking stats before pivoting. Reserves & disruption: Hunger Switch flips Morpeko's form and therefore Aura Wheel's type at the end of every turn, so the same move is Electric on one turn and Dark on the next; plan around the current form, not the remembered one. Could this team be cooler? The Water Spout artillery idea is one of the best in the Aqua arc and stays. Its defect was self-inflicted: Seaking carried Lightning Rod, which meant the team's own Morpeko had its Electric Aura Wheel pulled away by its partner half the time - a real bug in the plan, not a nuance. Swift Swim fixes it and simultaneously makes Seaking a second rain abuser, so the room finally plays as one rainstorm with a cannon in it. The alternating Aura Wheel type is now a genuine read-the-board puzzle rather than an accident. Overrepresented? Wailord's Drizzle set, Drapion and Morpeko are unique in the campaign; Seaking's other owner Connie deliberately keeps Lightning Rod because her entire gym room is built on redirection. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2.
crack: Hit Wailord first and Water Spout collapses with its HP. Grass and Electric attacks answer the Water pair, Wide Guard blanks Water Spout entirely, and replacing the weather halves Seaking's Speed. Ground types are immune to Electric Aura Wheel but not to the Dark one.

## E0457 TRAINER_GRUNT_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN_4 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the last door before Archie - four sets of jaws. Every member here bites, and the rain exists purely to make them faster. First two useful turns: Purugly's Fake Out buys Gorebyss the turn to set Rain Dance when Swift Swim and Water pressure repay it; otherwise Gorebyss opens with Muddy Water. Turn two attacks under the doubled Speed or pivots when the weather has been denied. Bruxish replaces the wall with a rare, vicious Water/Psychic biter that keeps the pressure up, breaks screens, and gives the room a real identity: the jaws room, where Fake Out, Intimidate and Defiant all interact on the first turn. Overqwil's Intimidate now has an aggressive partner to protect rather than a second wall to hide behind. Overrepresented? Bruxish and Gorebyss are unique in the campaign; Toxapex is moved to the brig room (E0455) where its Haze has a purpose; Purugly and Overqwil appear once each elsewhere in different roles. ## E0458 TRAINER_ARCHIE class=leader
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the Aqua apex. Archie does not borrow the sea's power yet - he commands a storm of his own and six bodies that each convert it differently, and every one of them is a pirate before it is a Water type. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle is live the moment the fight starts and Damp Rock holds it for eight turns; it opens with Wide Guard against a spread lead, Tailwind when the board is a speed race, or Hurricane, which never misses in its own rain. Toxicroak leads beside it with Fake Out, stealing the player's first action while Dry Skin heals it every turn the storm lasts - a Water-immune, self-healing Fake Out lead is the single hardest opening in the Aqua arc. Turn two commits to the real board: Seismitoad's Swift Swim is already doubled, so Hydro Pump and Earth Power come before most of the player's team can answer. Alternate deployment: Dhelmise is the ghost ship's anchor and the fight's signature - Anchor Shot deals Steelworker-boosted damage and traps the target, so a player who was planning to switch out of Archie's rain simply cannot, and Power Whip covers the Grass and Water types that expect to wall this roster. Alolan Muk is the weather-independent lane: Poison/Dark typing, Knock Off, Gunk Shot, and Poison Touch on contact, so the fight does not end when the rain does. Reserves & disruption: Mega Sharpedo is the closer, and its base Speed Boost must be earned before transforming, because the Mega ability replaces it - Archie's Mega is a deliberate foreshadow of the Elite Four's Dark specialist, not a coincidence. Toxicroak's Dry Skin is a liability in sun and Fire, Seismitoad's Speed halves when the weather is replaced, and Dhelmise's trap is broken by Ghost types and Shed Shell. Preserve the chronology repair: Kyogre is not awakened yet and must never appear on this team; Archie's power at this point comes from preparation, not the legend, and the roster is built to beat Maxie's Mt. Chimney and Mossdeep fights on its own merits. Could this team be cooler? Emphatically, and it now is. The authored roster was the weakest boss in the villain arc - Goodra, Eelektross, Tentacruel and Poliwrath were generic bulk with no shared engine, two of them duplicated route trainers the player had just beaten, and nothing on the team punished a player for the switching and stalling that beats rain. The rebuild gives Archie four things he did not have: a Fake Out lead that heals in his own weather, a Swift Swim sweeper that uses the storm on turn one, a trapping move that forbids the standard counterplay, and a non-Water fallback in Alolan Muk so removing the rain does not remove the fight. Every member is now water, dark, poison or drowned-ship in flavour, which is exactly the piratical identity the faction was supposed to have, and the fight ends on a Mega rather than opening on one. Overrepresented? Pelipper's other owner is Bridget's fossil-rain gym room, a different weather payoff entirely; Toxicroak, Dhelmise and Alolan Muk appear nowhere else in the campaign; Seismitoad's other owner Tiffany runs the Water Absorb version in the Sootopolis gym. Levels: cap 84, offsets 3/3/4/3/3/5 - the highest pre-badge-eight opponent in the game and a clear step above both Maxie fights.
crack: Weather replacement is the first move, but it is not enough on its own - Alolan Muk and Toxicroak do not need the rain, and Toxicroak's Dry Skin turns Water attacks into healing. Electric and Grass attacks answer most of the roster; Ground answers Toxicroak and Muk; Fire answers Dhelmise and Toxicroak at once. Wide Guard from your own side blanks Hurricane and Muddy Water, Ghost types and Shed Shell escape Anchor Shot, and denying Fake Out with your own is worth a turn. Chip Sharpedo before it transforms and the closer never closes.

## E0459 TRAINER_ANDREA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: room one of eleven - the volley room. Sootopolis opens by teaching the player that a Water gym can simply out-damage them, with Skill Link multi-hit attacks that ignore Substitutes, Focus Sashes and Sturdy. First two useful turns: Cloyster uses Shell Smash only when Floatzel's Choice Band damage buys it a survivable turn; otherwise both simply attack, because Skill Link Icicle Spear at five hits is already close to a boosted attack. Turn two handles Wave Crash recoil, the Sash and opposing priority before committing the boosted Cloyster. Alternate deployment: Qwilfish's Intimidate and Rocky Helmet front for Simipour's special coverage, which covers the Grass types the rest of the room fears with Grass Knot. Reserves & disruption: Jellicent's Water Absorb, Will-O-Wisp and Recover is the attrition lane and the room's only recovery; Adaptability Basculin is raw immediate pressure with Head Smash as a deliberate all-in. Spikes must beat an immediate attack to be worth its turn against a player with five replacements. Could this team be cooler? The six are a genuinely good spread of Water archetypes and the multi-hit lesson is the right opening note for the gym, so the composition earns retention. What it lacked was weight: as the first of eleven rooms it was authored at flat offsets, which made the gym start soft and never recover. The concrete improvement is a uniform two-level rise with the Adaptability closer higher still, so the player learns in room one that every Sootopolis trainer is a benchmark. Simipour is the one member worth defending explicitly - a Torrent monkey looks like filler, but it is the only member here that answers an opposing Grass lead, and removing it would make the room one-dimensional. Overrepresented? Cloyster, Floatzel, Simipour and Basculin appear only here; Jellicent and Qwilfish each have one distant other owner in different roles, and no other Sootopolis room runs Shell Smash as its headline - Olivia's Carracosta uses a Weakness Policy version with the opposite risk profile. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Grass and Electric attacks hit the whole room. Cloyster is physically bulky and specially fragile, so answer it on the special side before it smashes; priority finishes Floatzel after Wave Crash recoil. Will-O-Wisp is the room's only real disruption, so a Fire type or a special attacker ignores it entirely.

## E0460 TRAINER_CRISSY class=gym
strategy: RAIN
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: room two - the squall room, where the rain is made by hand and three different bodies are built to live inside it. First two useful turns: Swanna sets Rain Dance when its own Weather Ball - which becomes a 100-power Water attack in rain - and Lanturn's pressure both profit; Hydration then cures Swanna's status at the end of every rainy turn, so status is not an answer to it. Lanturn attacks or Volt Switches into the right body. Turn two exploits the actual weather rather than assuming every Water member needs it. Alternate deployment: Alomomola's Regenerator bulk and Mantine-free support line covers Choice Specs Primarina, whose Liquid Voice turns Hyper Voice into a spread Water attack that hits both opposing slots - the room's biggest single click. Reserves & disruption: Wishiwashi is only dangerous in School Form, which requires it to be above one quarter HP at level 20 or higher, so damaging it below that threshold reverts it to a nearly harmless fish mid-fight; Golisopod's Emergency Exit force-switches it at half HP, which interacts badly with its own Choice Band lock and is a real, visible weakness. Could this team be cooler? The room's best feature is that three of its six change form or state mid-battle - Schooling, Emergency Exit and rain-dependent Weather Ball - so the board the player faces on turn five is not the one they planned for on turn one. That is worth keeping. The change made is a swap of Mantine for Alomomola: Mantine's Water Absorb duplicated Tiffany's entire room concept four doors later, whereas Alomomola's Regenerator and Wish support is the natural partner for a manual-rain team that needs to survive the turn it spends setting weather. Overrepresented? Alomomola, Swanna, Wishiwashi, Golisopod and Primarina are each unique in the campaign; Lanturn's other owner is the route sailor Hudson in a Volt Absorb role. Bridget's room uses automatic Drizzle instead, so the gym has one manual-rain room and one automatic one by design. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Grass and Electric attacks cut through everything. Knock Wishiwashi under a quarter HP and it stops being a threat; push Golisopod to half and it removes itself. Replace the weather and Swanna loses both its Weather Ball power and its Hydration cure. Wide Guard blanks Liquid Voice Hyper Voice.

## E0461 TRAINER_BRIANNA class=gym
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: room three - the deep, where the water stops moving. This is the gym's Trick Room room: six deliberately slow bodies, zero Speed IVs across the board, and a drowned-coral ghost whose mere presence puts a three-turn clock on anything that touches it. First two useful turns: Slowking sets Trick Room behind a Mental Herb that protects the turn from Taunt, while Relicanth attacks - Rock Head means Head Smash costs it nothing at all, which under reversed order is the hardest single hit in the gym. Turn two spends the room on damage instead of a second support action. Alternate deployment: Quagsire's Unaware ignores every stat boost the player has accumulated, so a Shell Smash or Dragon Dance sweeper walks straight into a wall that does not care; Yawn forces the player to switch or sleep. Reserves & disruption: Cursola's Perish Body starts a three-turn countdown on any attacker that makes contact with it - and on Cursola itself - which turns the room's slowest, frailest member into a genuine threat the player must avoid punching; Strength Sap drains the target's Attack and heals Cursola by that amount. Overqwil's Intimidate and Barb Barrage punish an already-poisoned target, and Avalugg's Body Press and Wide Guard close the room. Could this team be cooler? The authored version was the gym's clear weak point and it is fully redesigned. It was six unconnected Water mons whose own block admitted "the defensive opening must not be the only admissible lead" - a stall room with a Fur Coat Dewgong, an Unaware Quagsire, and then three fast attackers that contradicted them. The rebuild gives the gym the distinct room it was missing and the brief asked for: a real Trick Room room, where Slowking's Regenerator keeps the setter alive across switches, Relicanth's recoil-free Head Smash is the payoff, and Cursola's Perish Body is a memorable interaction found nowhere else in the campaign - the player has to consciously stop making contact. Overrepresented? Cursola, Slowking and Quagsire appear only here; Relicanth is moved out of Olivia's room to give this one its physical payoff, and Avalugg's other owner Conor is a route ace many encounters earlier. The gym's three other slow-ish rooms use Shell Smash, redirection and burst instead, so no engine repeats. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Taunt the setter, or bring your own Trick Room and reverse the reversal. Do not make contact with Cursola - use special attacks, spread moves or simply kill it from range. Grass and Electric attacks answer most of the room; Fighting hits Relicanth and Avalugg; and after five turns the room ends while every member is still slow.

## E0462 TRAINER_CONNIE class=gym
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: NONE
tactic: ACTIVATE LUMINEON SURF GASTRODON
plan: Concept: room four - the redirection room, and the gym's cleverest single lesson. Two Storm Drain bodies stand side by side, so Lumineon's Surf damages both opposing slots and simultaneously raises its own partner's Special Attack by one stage. First two useful turns: the authored tactic fires Lumineon's Surf into the board, hitting both opponents while Gastrodon absorbs the friendly half and grows; Gastrodon then attacks with a boosted Earth Power or recovers according to the resulting state. Turn two picks the best real target rather than repeating the mutual boost forever. Alternate deployment: Seaking's Choice Band physical line and Octillery's Sniper special coverage are the mode to use when Storm Drain would get in the team's own way. Reserves & disruption: this is the one room in the gym where allied Storm Drain is an asset rather than a hazard, which is why Huntail's set carries no single-target Water attack at all - Shell Smash into Double-Edge, Crunch and Ice Fang keeps it entirely out of the redirection matrix, and the Double-Edge recoil is the price of staying off the Water lane. Starmie's Analytic adds 30% damage whenever it moves last, which the Tailwind is deliberately not built to prevent. Could this team be cooler? The authored concept is one of the best ideas in the gym and stays exactly as it is - but it had a live defect its own prose flagged: Huntail's Waterfall was a single-target Water attack standing beside two Storm Drain allies, so the room's reserve regularly fed its own partner instead of attacking. Replacing it with Double-Edge makes Huntail a clean Shell Smash breaker with no Water attack at all, and it means the Surf lesson now works in every deployment rather than only the lead one. The memorable interaction - an opponent deliberately hitting its own partner to power it up - is preserved intact. Overrepresented? Lumineon's Storm Drain is unique to this room by design (Linda's route version is switched to Swift Swim to avoid exactly this defect); Gastrodon, Octillery and Starmie appear once each; Seaking's Lightning Rod is kept here and removed from the Seafloor grunt for the same redirection reason. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Grass attacks ignore the whole redirection plan and hit both Storm Drain bodies for double damage. Wide Guard blanks Surf entirely, which shuts the engine off in one move. Taunt stops Tailwind and Recover; exploit Seaking's Choice lock; and Starmie is fast but extremely frail.

## E0463 TRAINER_BRIDGET class=gym
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: room five - the fossil reef. Automatic Drizzle plus a wall of prehistoric shells: three Swift Swim fossils that double their Speed the instant the rain lands, and a Mega Swampert to close. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle is live on entry, so Omastar is already fast; it opens with Tailwind, a U-turn pivot, or spread Muddy Water, and Omastar either attacks or takes a Shell Smash that White Herb immediately repairs. Turn two uses the real combined Swift Swim and Shell Smash speed rather than stacking further setup with no payoff. Alternate deployment: Pelipper and Mega Swampert are the physical rain mode once the transformation grants its actual post-Mega ability - base Swampert has Torrent and none of that Speed, so the order of operations matters. Reserves & disruption: Armaldo's Swift Swim is the room's surprise, a Rock/Bug fossil nobody expects to outrun them in rain; Drednaw's Strong Jaw powers Crunch and Liquidation specifically; Kabutops supplies Aqua Jet priority for when the rain is finally taken away. Could this team be cooler? The Drizzle-plus-Swift-Swim concept was already right for a Sootopolis room, and the honest judgement is that it needed sharpening rather than replacing. Armaldo is the concrete improvement: swapping the duplicated Ludicolo for a rarely used fossil with Swift Swim makes the room read as a single coherent idea - every non-Mega member is a fossil or a seabird - and gives the player a genuine surprise, because an Armaldo that moves first is not something the campaign has taught them to expect. That also frees Ludicolo to belong solely to Shelly's Aqua crew. Overrepresented? Armaldo, Omastar, Kabutops and Drednaw are unique in the campaign after this pass; Pelipper's other owner is Archie, whose Damp Rock storm serves an entirely different roster; Mega Swampert appears only here. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Replace the weather and four members lose half their Speed at once. Grass attacks are devastating against the entire room; Electric answers Pelipper and Omastar. Wide Guard blanks Muddy Water, Taunt stops Tailwind and Shell Smash, and priority catches Omastar before White Herb ever matters.

## E0464 TRAINER_OLIVIA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: room six - the artillery room. Mega Launcher pulses, a Water Bubble spider that doubles its own Water damage, and a cormorant that spits its catch back at whoever hit it. First two useful turns: Carracosta takes a Shell Smash only when Araquanid's attack or Wide Guard buys a survivable window; unlike every other Shell Smash in the gym this one holds a Weakness Policy rather than a White Herb, so the Defence drops stay and the gamble is real - the payoff is that a super-effective hit raises both attacking stats by two more stages on top. Turn two uses the actual boosts and Policy state. Alternate deployment: Tentacruel's Liquid Ooze punishes Giga Drain, Leech Life and Draining Kiss by damaging the drainer instead of healing it, which is a specific and nasty answer to the Grass types brought to beat a Water gym; it fronts for Choice Specs Clawitzer, whose Mega Launcher raises every pulse move by half again. Reserves & disruption: Cramorant is the room's signature - after it uses Surf it holds a catch, and the next attacker to strike it takes damage and either a Defence drop or paralysis in return, whichever form it swallowed. Relicanth-free Rock coverage comes from Carracosta's Stone Edge. Note Surf also hits the ally slot, which is why Cramorant is paired with the Water-resistant members rather than the frail ones. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and this is the room that gained the most. Carracosta's Policy gamble and Clawitzer's pulse artillery were good, but the roster around them was the campaign's third and fourth Tentacruel-and-Relicanth pairing, and Relicanth's Choice Band anvil belonged in the Trick Room room instead. Cramorant is the concrete upgrade: Gulp Missile is one of the most distinctive doubles mechanics in the series and appears nowhere else in this campaign, it gives the player a real reason to think before attacking a seemingly harmless bird, and it turns a generic bulky-Water room into the one room people will describe afterwards. Overrepresented? Cramorant and Clawitzer appear only here; Araquanid's other owner is the route trainer Linda with a Sticky Web set; Tentacruel keeps this Liquid Ooze slot and is removed from Archie and from Owen in Victory Road; Relicanth moves to Brianna. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Grass and Electric attacks cover the room, but do not drain against Liquid Ooze. Attack Carracosta with a neutral hit so the Weakness Policy never fires, and finish it with priority once the Defence drops land. Do not strike Cramorant after it has surfaced; kill it from range or let it waste the catch. Wide Guard blanks Surf and Muddy Water.

## E0465 TRAINER_TIFFANY class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: room seven - the tide pool, built entirely on Water Absorb. Three of the six are immune to Water attacks, so Vaporeon's spread Surf is a full-power attack on both opposing slots that heals its own partner instead of hurting it. First two useful turns: Vaporeon Surfs while a Water Absorb partner stands beside it, gaining a free quarter of HP for that ally; Seismitoad or Mantine attacks in the other slot. Turn two sets Wish only when a real recipient will still be alive at the end of the following turn. Alternate deployment: Vaporeon's support enables Barbaracle's White Herb Shell Smash, but Surf is genuinely unsafe beside Barbaracle, so the AI must switch lanes rather than blindly repeat the engine - the lead pair and the setup pair are two separate modes. Reserves & disruption: Whiscash's Assault Vest physical line and Feraligatr's Sheer Force Dragon Dance are the non-absorbing finishers; Tough Claws boosts every contact move Barbaracle has, and Sheer Force removes Life Orb recoil on Feraligatr's Liquidation, Ice Punch and Crunch. Could this team be cooler? The Water Absorb Surf engine is a clean, teachable idea and the right seventh room, but the authored version only half-committed: Walrein's Choice-locked Blizzard was inaccurate with no snow anywhere in the gym, and the block's own prose admitted the partner matrix was unsafe. Moving Walrein to Daphne's ice room, where snow actually exists, and bringing Mantine in as a third Water Absorb body makes the engine genuinely dominant in its lead mode while keeping the honest cost that two members break it. That is a better fight than a room with six loosely related Water types. Overrepresented? Vaporeon's other owner is Hope in Victory Road with a Helping Hand support set; Mantine moves here from Crissy's room precisely so that absorption belongs to one room; Seismitoad's other owner Archie runs Swift Swim; Barbaracle, Feraligatr and Whiscash's Assault Vest set are unique here. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Grass attacks ignore the whole absorption plan and hit almost everything twice as hard; Electric answers Vaporeon and Mantine. Taunt stops Wish and Shell Smash. Attack Barbaracle or Feraligatr and the engine's own Surf becomes a liability the AI has to route around.

## E0466 TRAINER_BETHANY class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: room eight - the strike team. This is the gym's speed-and-priority room: everything here either moves first or hits before the player can react. First two useful turns: Palafin leads and uses Flip Turn or a hard switch only when a healthy receiver exists, because leaving the field transforms it into Hero Form - on its second entry it is a different, far larger Pokemon with a Jet Punch that outspeeds everything. Barraskewda attacks beside it with Propeller Tail, which cannot be pulled away by Follow Me, Rage Powder or Storm Drain. Turn two commits to whichever of the two transformations or boosts actually landed. Alternate deployment: Azumarill's Belly Drum is admitted only after a survival check - it costs half its HP for a maximised Attack that Huge Power then doubles again, making Aqua Jet a priority attack with no realistic counterplay except killing it first. Reserves & disruption: Greninja's Protean changes its type to match each move as it uses it, so it is never the type the player prepared for; Sniper Inteleon converts its critical hits into removals; Mega Kingler is the closer, with Crabhammer's high critical rate behind it. Palafin's late placement must not strand an untransformed Hero with no reserve left to pivot into. Could this team be cooler? The Zero to Hero idea is the best single mechanic in the gym and stays as the room's spine, but the authored roster buried it: Dhelmise and Crawdaunt were slow bodies in a room about speed, and Mega Sharpedo made this the campaign's third owner of that exact stone after Archie and the Elite Four's Dark specialist. Rebuilding around Barraskewda, Greninja and Inteleon makes every slot serve the same idea, and Mega Kingler gives the room a Mega nobody else in the campaign fields. The memorable moment is the pivot itself - the player kills a modest dolphin and then meets Hero Form two turns later. Overrepresented? Mega Kingler, Palafin and Inteleon appear only here; Greninja's other owner is Felix's Mega version deep in Victory Road; Barraskewda's other owner is a Seafloor grunt with a Focus Sash; Azumarill's remaining owners are the route trainer Kara and nobody else, since Wally's Victory Road team is rebuilt to his canonical partners. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Kill Palafin before it ever leaves the field and Hero Form never happens. Electric and Grass attacks answer the room; priority of your own beats Jet Punch's users after Belly Drum. Fake Out and Intimidate both blunt the opening, and Inteleon and Greninja fold to any neutral hit.

## E0467 TRAINER_ANNIKA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: room nine - the still room. Annika is the gym's contrarian: her Golduck's Cloud Nine switches off every weather effect in the building, including her own colleagues' rain, and the rest of her team is built to win without any. First two useful turns: Golduck attacks or takes a Nasty Plot while Quaquaval opens with Aqua Step, which raises its own Speed on every use and stacks with Moxie's Attack boost after each knockout - a self-accelerating sweeper that needs no weather at all. Turn two uses the real boosts rather than adding a third. Alternate deployment: Empoleon's Competitive doubles its Special Attack whenever any stat of its is lowered, so an opposing Intimidate, Snarl or Icy Wind hands it a free Nasty Plot; Assault Vest bulk lets it stay in for that. Reserves & disruption: Milotic's Flame Orb self-burn activates Marvel Scale for a 50% Defence boost at the cost of residual damage, and Hisuian Samurott's Sharpness sharply boosts its slicing moves while Ceaseless Edge lays Spikes on every hit. Basculegion is the Adaptability special closer. Note that Aqua Step is a dance, so an opposing Dancer copies it. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it needed the change more than any other room except Brianna's. Pyukumuku's Counter-and-Toxic opening was pure passivity in a gym that is supposed to be eleven consecutive benchmarks, and the block's own prose flagged it for anti-passivity testing. Replacing it with Basculegion gives the room a real sixth threat, a Water/Ghost typing nothing else in the gym has, and an immunity to the Fighting and Normal attacks the player brings for Empoleon and Milotic. What remains is the room's genuinely distinctive idea - three different self-boosting mechanisms (Aqua Step, Moxie, Competitive) that all reward the player for attacking, so patience is punished and impatience is punished differently. Overrepresented? Quaquaval, Empoleon, Hisuian Samurott and Basculegion are unique in the campaign; Milotic's other owner is Champion Wallace's Mega; Golduck's Cloud Nine echoes the route sailor Hudson deliberately, as the player-side lesson resurfacing as an opponent. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Do not lower Empoleon's stats. Electric and Grass attacks answer most of the room; Dark and Ghost hit Basculegion. Haze erases Aqua Step and Moxie together, Taunt stops Nasty Plot, and Milotic's burn is doing steady damage to itself all fight.

## E0468 TRAINER_DAPHNE class=gym
strategy: SNOW
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: room ten - the freeze. The last room before Juan is the one that changes the weather itself: Lapras lays down snow by hand, which raises every Ice type's Defence by half, and the room's Ice Body members then heal from the same storm every turn. First two useful turns: Lapras opens with Snowscape when the team can bank the Defence boost and the passive healing, or with Freeze-Dry, which is the room's signature - an Ice attack that is super effective against Water, so this room beats the rest of its own gym. Walrein attacks beside it with Thick Fat cutting incoming Fire and Ice damage in half. Turn two uses the real weather state and picks between Blizzard's now-perfect accuracy and single-target damage. Alternate deployment: Cryogonal's Levitate makes it immune to Ground attacks, and its Icy Wind and Rapid Spin support Samurott's physical line. Reserves & disruption: snow makes Blizzard never miss, so Walrein's Choice Specs lock is far less dangerous than it looks once the weather is up; Rotom-Wash supplies the Electric coverage the room otherwise lacks and Levitate keeps it out of Ground range; Fur Coat halves physical damage against Dewgong. Could this team be cooler? It is now the room the gym was missing. The authored version had no engine at all - four unrelated Water types plus a Tapu Fini that duplicated Champion Wallace's own legend, which is exactly the kind of collision that makes a later fight feel less special. Rebuilding Daphne as the snow room gives Sootopolis its eleventh distinct strategy, hands Freeze-Dry to the one trainer for whom being a Water gym's worst matchup is the joke, and lets Walrein's Blizzard finally be accurate somewhere. Cryogonal is the rare, genuinely strange body the room deserves: a floating ice crystal with enormous Special Defence and no Ground weakness. Overrepresented? Cryogonal, Walrein and Dewgong appear only here in the gym; Lapras's other owners are the route trainer Everett's Perish Song set and Micah's Mega far into the post-game; Rotom-Wash recurs with Keira after the League. Tapu Fini is returned to Champion Wallace alone. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Replace the weather and the Defence boost, the healing and Blizzard's accuracy all go at once. Fire, Fighting, Rock and Steel attacks cover the Ice core; Samurott and Rotom-Wash are the only members that are not weak to Fighting. Taunt stops Snowscape and Rapid Spin, and Walrein is locked into whatever it picked.
