## E0469 TRAINER_JUAN_1 class=leader
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the eleventh door - Juan takes the same rain his gym has been teaching and uses it for attrition rather than burst, then ends the fight with a Mega. Everything the ten rooms showed the player in isolation arrives here at once. First two useful turns: Politoed's Drizzle is live on entry with a Damp Rock behind it and opens with Encore to lock the player into a support move they have just wasted, or spread Muddy Water; Suicune picks Tailwind, Snarl or immediate pressure according to the board. Turn two must introduce a real attacker if the opening pair is being out-paced - Juan's failure mode is passivity and the plan explicitly forbids it. Alternate deployment: Suicune's control supports Manaphy's Tail Glow, which raises Special Attack by three stages in one turn and, under Hydration, is protected from any status the player tries to interrupt it with while the rain lasts. Reserves & disruption: Toxapex is the wall with a job - Wide Guard actively protects a partner from the spread attacks this roster invites, Acid Spray halves a target's Special Defence for a real follow-up, and Regenerator refunds a third of its HP on every switch. Choice Specs Swift Swim Kingdra is the burst reserve, doubling its Speed in the rain it did not have to set. Mega Gyarados is the closer and its Intimidate is deliberately spent before transformation. Could this team be cooler? This was already the strongest authored encounter in the range and the honest judgement is that it earns retention almost unchanged - the difference between Juan and Shelly's identical-looking rain team is that Shelly wants the fight over in four turns and Juan wants it to last twelve, and both are legible in play. The changes are about weight rather than concept: the leader was authored barely above his own gym trainers, so the offsets rise to place him a clear tier above all eleven rooms and above Archie, and the AI trait moves to Double Ace Pokemon so Manaphy and Mega Gyarados are both treated as finishers instead of one. Overrepresented? Politoed's Drizzle is shared with Shelly by design and with nobody else; Suicune, Manaphy and Mega Gyarados are unique to this fight; Kingdra's other owners are two route trainers at far lower tiers; Toxapex's other owner is a Seafloor grunt using Haze instead of Wide Guard. Levels: cap 84, offsets 3/3/3/4/4/5 - badge eight should cost the player its whole team.
crack: Take the rain and keep taking it - three members lose their engine. Grass and Electric attacks hit almost the entire roster; Taunt stops Encore, Tailwind, Tail Glow and Wide Guard. Haze erases Tail Glow and Dragon Dance together, priority answers Gyarados after it boosts, and Toxapex can be walked past rather than broken.

## E0472 TRAINER_ALEXIS class=regular
strategy: REDIRECTION
mega_slots: 4
plan: Concept: a psychic quartet that turns its own terrain into a damage multiplier - Expanding Force becomes a spread attack with half again the power while Psychic Terrain is up, so three of the four are firing a boosted two-target move. First two useful turns: Indeedee-F establishes Psychic Surge on entry and redirects with Follow Me only when it preserves a worthwhile Expanding Force; Hatterene attacks. Turn two checks actual groundedness - the terrain and its priority-blocking effect only apply to grounded targets - and whether the terrain is still the team's own. Alternate deployment: Indeedee-F supports Reuniclus's Magic Guard coverage when Hatterene's typing is unfavourable; Magic Guard means Reuniclus takes no damage from Life Orb, burn, poison, hazards or sandstorm. Reserves & disruption: Hatterene's Magic Bounce reflects Taunt, Thunder Wave, Leech Seed and hazards straight back at the sender, so status is not the answer here. Mega Meowstic's screens and its form's ability transition are separate matters; base Prankster gives its screens priority but never makes Expanding Force a priority attack. Could this team be cooler? The terrain-plus-Expanding-Force stack is a real doubles engine and Magic Bounce is a genuine puzzle for a player who has been solving fights with Taunt, so this earns retention as a route regular. The block also carried a live legality error - Mega Meowstic was given Expanding Force, which it cannot learn - so its fourth slot becomes ordinary Psychic, which is honest and still benefits from the terrain. What would make it cooler is deliberately withheld: a Trick Room would turn it into a third room-team in a stretch that already has three, and the psychic-terrain identity is cleaner without one. Overrepresented? Indeedee-F recurs only with Wally's post-game rematch; Hatterene, Reuniclus and Mega Meowstic appear once each; Alexis is the only Psychic Terrain owner before the League. Levels: cap 90, offsets 0/1/1/2.
crack: Replace the terrain and three attacks lose a third of their power. Dark and Steel attacks answer the whole team and Magic Bounce does nothing against damage. Wide Guard blanks Expanding Force and Dazzling Gleam; attack around the redirection with spread moves; and Meowstic's screens fall to Brick Break or Defog.

## E0473 TRAINER_MARIELA class=casual

Turn two uses the actual post-transformation ability rather than the base one. ## E0475 TRAINER_EVERETT class=regular
strategy: PERISH_TRAP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the campaign's perish-trap prototype - Wobbuffet's Shadow Tag forbids the player from switching, Lapras starts a three-turn countdown, and both sides watch the clock. First two useful turns: Lapras uses Perish Song when Wobbuffet can hold the trap and both friendly slots still have a legal exit of their own; Wobbuffet Encores a committed move or waits with Counter and Mirror Coat, which return double the physical or special damage it just took. Turn two plays the countdown honestly - the trapper must leave or deliberately trade before it reaches zero, and switching the trapper releases the opponents early. Alternate deployment: Beheeyem and Musharna are the slower control fallback when the trap is answered - Analytic adds 30% to Beheeyem's damage whenever it moves last, which a Trick Room makes reliable. Reserves & disruption: Life Dew heals both allies, Safeguard blocks the status a trapped player reaches for, and Forewarn tells Musharna the strongest move on the opposing side. Wobbuffet is deliberately given no Protect - the script must not be made easy. Could this team be cooler? The perish trap is the rarest strategy in the campaign and appears exactly once, which is the right frequency; a second owner would make it a gimmick rather than an event. The block carried one legality error - Beheeyem cannot learn Helping Hand - so it becomes Energy Ball, which also gives the control half a real answer to the Ground and Water types that otherwise wall it. The honest limitation stays recorded: countdown ordering, simultaneous faints and escape sequencing are native acceptance gates, not assumptions. Overrepresented? Wobbuffet, Beheeyem and Musharna's Forewarn set are unique; Lapras's other owners are Daphne's gym ice room and Micah's post-game Mega. Levels: cap 90, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Ghost types ignore Shadow Tag entirely, and so do Shed Shell and U-turn. Soundproof blocks Perish Song. Kill Wobbuffet with a spread move so Counter and Mirror Coat cannot answer, or simply out-damage the clock - three turns is not long, and Lapras dies to Electric and Grass attacks.

## E0476 TRAINER_WALLY_VR_1 class=rival
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the rival arc, part one. This is Wally's own team - the partners he has actually raised since Petalburg - brought to Victory Road standard and crowned with the Mega he was always going to earn. First two useful turns: Delcatty opens with Fake Out to steal the player's first action, or Helping Hand when Gardevoir's Moonblast or Psychic is already lethal; Gardevoir attacks, with Trace having copied the most useful opposing ability the moment it entered. Turn two picks the reserve that answers whatever Trace revealed, and Heal Bell is spent only when a real status is crippling a real threat. Alternate deployment: Altaria's Tailwind and Roost line supports Magnezone, whose Magnet Pull traps the Steel types that wall the Fairy half of this team and removes them at leisure. Reserves & disruption: Eviolite Roselia is Wally's oldest partner and is genuinely bulky with it, landing Sleep Powder while Natural Cure keeps it clean; Mega Gallade's Sharpness sharply boosts Psycho Cut, Sacred Sword and Night Slash. Fake Out only works on the turn Delcatty enters, and Wonder Skin halves the accuracy of status moves aimed at it but does nothing against damage. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and this is the change the whole rival arc needed. The authored team was Garchomp, Togekiss and Azumarill - three Pokemon that belong to Cynthia and to route trainers, none of which Wally has ever owned - so the campaign's rival arrived at Victory Road as a generic good team with a Gallade attached. Rebuilding it from his actual line-up gives the player a fight they can read as a character: the Ralts from Petalburg is now a Gardevoir and a Mega Gallade standing side by side, the Roselia is still a Roselia, and the Delcatty that opened his original team is still the one buying the first turn. It is both cooler and more personal, and it removes the Cynthia collision three encounters from the end of the campaign. Overrepresented? Delcatty, Altaria and Roselia appear nowhere else; Magnezone's other owner is Wattson's gym far earlier; Gardevoir recurs only with Wally's own post-game rematch, where it becomes the Mega and Gallade steps back - a deliberate mirror. Togekiss is returned to Cynthia alone and Garchomp to Cynthia and Caroline. Levels: cap 90 incoming; his defeat is what raises the cap to 96, so the League tier is earned here. Offsets 1/2/1/1/1/3.
crack: Deny Fake Out with your own and Delcatty's whole turn is wasted; spread damage kills it outright. Steel and Poison attacks answer the Fairy core; Ice and Dragon attacks answer Altaria; Fire answers Roselia and Gallade. Taunt stops Tailwind, Sleep Powder and Helping Hand, and Mega Gallade is fast but not bulky - priority ends it.

## E0477 TRAINER_EDGAR class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: the first door of Victory Road - a mechanical gauntlet where three separate members set up in three incompatible ways and the player cannot answer all of them. Turn two answers whichever setup the player failed to stop. Alternate deployment: Venomoth's Tinted Lens makes even resisted Bug Buzz and Sludge Bomb land at neutral damage, so a Quiver Dance behind a Focus Sash is a genuine sweep threat; Klinklang's Shift Gear doubles its Speed and raises Attack, and Clear Body refuses every stat drop the player aims at it. Reserves & disruption: Choice Scarf Manectric with Lightning Rod is the emergency speed control and Electric absorber; Mega Hawlucha's Mold Breaker ignores abilities entirely, so Sturdy, Levitate and Water Absorb do not save anything from Close Combat or Brave Bird. Could this team be cooler? It earns retention. The improvement it needed was tier: authored at flat offsets it read as a route ace that had wandered into Victory Road, so the whole team rises and Mega Hawlucha rises further. Tinted Lens Venomoth is the member worth defending - it is the only thing in the range that makes resistances irrelevant, and that is a better kind of pressure than raw stats. Overrepresented? ## E0478 TRAINER_ALBERT class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 6

Could this team be cooler? The Quash-plus-Solid-Rock-Policy interaction is a real, unusual plan and Mega Glimmora is a genuinely memorable Mega, so the concept holds. ## E0479 TRAINER_HOPE class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a support-and-snipe team where the entire roster exists to give one small fish a free Nasty Plot. First two useful turns: Forretress sets Stealth Rock behind Sturdy or pivots with Volt Switch while Lilligant lands Sleep Powder or a Leaf Storm from a Focus Sash. Turn two brings the member that punishes whatever the player committed to. Alternate deployment: Salazzle's Corrosion lets it poison Steel and Poison types that believe they are immune, which is a specific answer to the Steel walls this team otherwise cannot break; Aerodactyl's Tailwind and Taunt handle the speed race. Reserves & disruption: Vaporeon's Muddy Water is spread, so it cannot be stolen by its own Storm Drain ace, and Helping Hand is its real job; Mega Tatsugiri's Storm Drain draws every opposing single-target Water attack and converts it into a Special Attack boost, which is the team's signature - a player who leads with Water hands the sweeper a free stage before it even acts. Could this team be cooler? The shape is right and unusual: five support-shaped bodies and one tiny, absurd Mega sushi dragon that ends the game. It earns retention. The improvement made is tier and pacing: at flat offsets the support turns were free for the player, so everything rises and the Mega rises further, making the two-turn setup an actual race. Overrepresented? crack: Do not use single-target Water attacks. Fire answers Forretress, Lilligant and Salazzle at once; Rock answers Aerodactyl and Tatsugiri; Dragon and Fairy answer the ace. Taunt stops Stealth Rock, Tailwind, Sleep Powder and Nasty Plot, and Safety Goggles ignores the powder entirely.

## E0480 TRAINER_QUINCY class=ace
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
tactic: SUPPRESS WEEZING NONE SLAKING
plan: Concept: the cleverest single idea in Victory Road - a team built entirely around turning off the worst ability in the game. Slaking has Truant and can only attack every other turn; Weezing's Neutralizing Gas suppresses every ability on the field while it is alive, Truant included, so a Choice Scarf Slaking with 160 base Attack simply attacks every turn instead. The authored SUPPRESS tactic is what tells the AI to keep Weezing alive for exactly that reason. First two useful turns: Weezing enters beside Slaking and stays; Slaking fires Double-Edge or High Horsepower with no downtime. Turn two protects the suppressor - the moment Weezing dies, Slaking loses half its turns. Alternate deployment: Durant's Entrainment is the backup plan and the fight's second joke - it forcibly gives Truant to an opposing Pokemon, locking the player's own sweeper out of every other turn. Butterfree's Compound Eyes makes Sleep Powder effectively accurate and Rage Powder protects the suppressor. Reserves & disruption: Terrakion's Quick Guard blocks the priority aimed at Weezing; Mega Zygarde's Thousand Arrows hits even airborne targets and grounds them, and Power Construct is suppressed while Weezing lives, which is a real cost the AI must weigh. Glare paralyses regardless of type. Could this team be cooler? No - this is the best-designed encounter in the range and it is kept intact. Every slot serves one absurd, legible idea, the player can beat it by killing a Weezing, and doing so visibly halves the boss's output; the Entrainment mirror means the trick can be turned on the player as well. The only change is tier, so that the Slaking turns actually hurt while the player is solving the puzzle. Worth noting for the AI audit: Neutralizing Gas suppressing the team's own Power Construct is a genuine tension and the tactic line must not be read as an instruction to Mega at all costs. Overrepresented? Slaking, Weezing, Durant, Butterfree, Terrakion and Mega Zygarde appear nowhere else in the campaign; this is the only Neutralizing Gas or Entrainment owner. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Kill Weezing and the fight halves. Psychic and Ground attacks answer it; Fighting answers Slaking; Fire answers Durant and Butterfree. Do not let Durant touch your sweeper with Entrainment. Quick Guard only blocks priority, so remove the suppressor with an ordinary attack, and Thousand Arrows means Flying types are not safe.

## E0481 TRAINER_KATELYNN class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 5
plan: Concept: a furnace - five Fire bodies, one healer, and a legendary that makes every one of them accurate. First two useful turns: Audino's Regenerator bulk lets it Wish or Helping Hand while Infernape's Fake Out and mixed Naive spread buy the turn; Wish pays out at the end of the following turn to whoever is in the slot, so it must be aimed at a body that will still be there. Turn two commits to the damage the support turn bought. Alternate deployment: Bewear's Fluffy halves contact damage while doubling Fire damage taken, which is a real and specific weakness in a Fire team; Choice Specs Victini with Victory Star raises the accuracy of every move on its own side by 10%, so the team's Heat Waves, Overheats and Focus Blasts are all steadier while it is out. Reserves & disruption: Centiskorch's Fire Lash lowers the target's Defence on every hit and Leech Life restores its own HP, giving the team a grinding physical lane that Mega Delphox's special barrage does not cover. Flash Fire on Delphox absorbs Fire attacks aimed at the team. Could this team be cooler? The Victory Star accuracy aura is an unusual and genuinely useful team-wide effect, and the Fluffy drawback gives the player a real lever, so the structure earns retention. The change is Volcarona, which was an exact duplicate of Susie's set far earlier on the same coast: Centiskorch replaces it with a rarely seen Fire/Bug body, adds the Defence-shredding physical lane the roster lacked, and stops Victory Road from repeating a route ace's signature sweeper. Audino is the slot worth defending - a Regenerator healer in an all-out offensive team is the reason this fight lasts long enough to be dangerous. Overrepresented? Centiskorch, Victini, Bewear, Audino and Mega Delphox are unique in the campaign; Infernape appears once elsewhere; Volcarona now belongs solely to Susie (E0423). Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/2/1.
crack: Water, Rock and Ground attacks cover the whole roster, and Fire attacks specifically melt Bewear through Fluffy. Kill Victini and the team's accuracy drops back to normal. Taunt stops Wish and Helping Hand; exploit Victini's Choice lock; and Centiskorch is slow without support.

## E0482 TRAINER_SAMUEL class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a web-and-rain-of-Thunder team - Galvantula's Compound Eyes makes its Thunder accurate without weather, and its Sticky Web halves the Speed of everything the player sends in afterwards. First two useful turns: Galvantula lays Sticky Web when the fight will last, or fires a Compound Eyes Thunder when it will not; Nidoking's Sheer Force removes the secondary effects of Sludge Bomb and Ice Beam in exchange for a damage bonus and, with Wise Glasses, hits far above its reputation. Turn two attacks into the speed advantage the web created. Alternate deployment: Adaptability Porygon-Z's Tri Attack and Mismagius's Levitate-backed special coverage form the mode that needs no setup at all. Reserves & disruption: Zacian's Intrepid Sword raises its Attack by one stage the moment it enters, every time, which makes it the only member that threatens an immediate knockout with no preparation; Mega Latios's Tailwind is the alternative speed plan for when the web is removed by Defog or Rapid Spin. Could this team be cooler? The double speed-control plan - permanent web plus temporary Tailwind, so removing one still leaves the other - is genuinely well built and stays. The change is Gengar, which appeared identically on a route ace, on Leaf's Kanto team and as Phoebe's League Mega: Mismagius takes the ghost slot with Levitate instead of Cursed Body, giving the team a Ground immunity it otherwise lacks and a Nasty Plot sweeper that pairs with the web rather than duplicating a set the player has already beaten twice. Overrepresented? Galvantula's other owner is Wattson's gym far earlier; Nidoking, Mismagius, Zacian and Mega Latios are unique in the campaign; Porygon-Z's other owner Darcy uses a Life Orb version at a much lower tier. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Fly, Levitate or an Air Balloon ignores Sticky Web entirely, and Defog removes it. Fire and Rock attacks answer Galvantula; Ground answers Nidoking; Fighting and Fire answer Zacian; Dark, Ghost, Ice and Fairy answer Latios. Taunt stops the web and Tailwind together.

## E0483 TRAINER_SHANNON class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 2
plan: Concept: a screens-and-speed team that puts its Mega in the second slot rather than the last - Mega Emboar arrives immediately, and everything else exists to keep it alive long enough to Reckless its way through the gauntlet. First two useful turns: Ribombee's Focus Sash guarantees a Tailwind, and Shield Dust means no secondary effect - no flinch, no burn, no drop - ever lands on it while it works; Emboar attacks. Turn two spends the speed window rather than adding a second support turn. Alternate deployment: Orbeetle's Light Screen and Telepathy mode is the patient build, with Telepathy making it immune to its partner's own spread attacks; Goodra's Assault Vest bulk gives the team a special sponge it otherwise lacks, and Gooey lowers the Speed of anything that makes contact with it, so the physical attackers brought to break it end up slower than the Tailwind team every turn they connect. Reserves & disruption: Vikavolt's Levitate and Choice Specs make it a Ground-immune artillery piece that punishes the Water and Flying answers brought for Emboar; Sawsbuck's Sap Sipper turns an opposing Grass attack into a free Attack boost. Reckless raises Flare Blitz, Head Smash and Double-Edge but not Drain Punch, and all three hurt Emboar badly. Could this team be cooler? The early-Mega structure is a genuine change of rhythm in a gauntlet where every other trainer saves the transformation for last, so it stays. Two slots changed for duplication rather than taste: Escavalier's Bug Gem set was a copy of Marley's route ace and Dragonite duplicated both Aaron's Mega and Drake's League ace, so Vikavolt and Goodra replace them. Both are rarer, both cover a hole the original pair did not - Ground immunity and a contact-punishing special wall - and neither repeats a set the player has already solved. Overrepresented? Vikavolt, Goodra, Ribombee, Orbeetle and Mega Emboar are unique in the campaign after this pass; Sawsbuck's other owner is Jonathan far earlier with a Clear Amulet. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/2/1/1/1/1.
crack: Water, Ground and Rock attacks answer Emboar, and its own recoil does the rest. Fire and Rock answer Ribombee, Vikavolt and Orbeetle. Break the Sash before Tailwind resolves, strip the screens with Brick Break or Defog, and exploit Vikavolt's Choice lock.

## E0484 TRAINER_MICHELLE class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 1
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: the wall. Michelle is Victory Road's attrition gate - a Mega Meganium whose Triage gives Heal Pulse priority, a Chansey behind an Eviolite, and three bodies that simply refuse to die on schedule. First two useful turns: Mega Meganium uses Heal Pulse, which restores half a partner's HP and, through Triage, does so before almost anything on the field can act - so the player must out-damage a full heal every turn, not merely out-damage a Pokemon. Machamp attacks beside it with No Guard making Dynamic Punch a guaranteed hit and a guaranteed confusion. Turn two chooses between another heal and Light Screen depending on which damage type is actually killing. Alternate deployment: Chansey's Seismic Toss deals fixed damage that ignores every defensive stat, and Soft-Boiled keeps it there; Umbreon's Foul Play uses the target's own Attack, so it punishes exactly the physical sweeper a player brings to break walls. Reserves & disruption: Hydreigon is the one genuinely fast special attacker and the answer to a player who has switched to passive play; Probopass's Magnet Pull traps Steel types and Stealth Rock taxes every switch this team is trying to force. Could this team be cooler? The Triage Heal Pulse idea is excellent and rare, and a pure stall gate is the right change of pace in a gauntlet of sweepers, so the concept holds. The change is Tyranitar, which set sand for a team with no sand abusers at all and duplicated Caroline's dedicated sand team five doors later - a mislabelled strategy flag and a collision in one slot. Umbreon replaces it with a body that actually belongs in a stall team, adds a Dark type to a roster that had no answer to Psychic and Ghost attackers, and makes Foul Play the trap that punishes the player's obvious counterplay. The SAND flag is corrected to SETUP. Overrepresented? Umbreon, Meganium, Machamp, Chansey and Hydreigon are unique in the campaign; Probopass's other owner is Susie's route team; Tyranitar is returned to Caroline alone. Levels: cap 96, offsets 2/1/1/1/1/1.
crack: You cannot out-grind this team - you have to out-burst it. Fire, Ice, Poison and Flying attacks answer Meganium; Fighting answers Chansey and Umbreon; Fairy answers Hydreigon. Taunt is the single best move here: it turns off Heal Pulse, Soft-Boiled, Light Screen and Stealth Rock at once. Do not boost Attack into Foul Play.

## E0485 TRAINER_MITCHELL class=ace
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: 4
plan: Concept: Victory Road's Trick Room gate, and the one that traps you inside it - Gothitelle's Shadow Tag means the reversed order is not something you can simply switch out of. First two useful turns: Shiinotic's Spore puts a legal target to sleep with certainty while Gothitelle sets Trick Room; Effect Spore additionally inflicts a random status on anything that makes contact with Shiinotic, and Strength Sap drains the target's Attack while healing by that amount. Turn two spends the reversed order on damage. Alternate deployment: Guts Conkeldurr and Thick Club Alolan Marowak are the heavy mode - the Thick Club doubles Marowak's Attack, and Lightning Rod makes it the team's Electric absorber and a redirector against single-target Electric attacks. Reserves & disruption: Mega Scovillain's Insomnia keeps it awake beside its own Spore user, and its dual Grass and Fire special attacks cover the Water and Steel types brought against the room; Magic Guard Reuniclus takes no Life Orb recoil at all, which is why it can afford the item at this tier. All five slow members carry zero Speed IVs so the room works for them and not against them. Could this team be cooler? The trap-plus-room pairing is the right identity for this door, and Mega Scovillain is a genuinely strange, memorable ace. The improvement made is the same one the earlier room teams needed: every Brave, Quiet and Relaxed member now has a zero Speed IV, which is the difference between a Trick Room team and a team carrying the move. The Conkeldurr duplication with Albert is resolved on Albert's side rather than here, because Guts under Trick Room is the more natural home for the set. Overrepresented? Shiinotic, Alolan Marowak and Mega Scovillain are unique; Gothitelle recurs with the route ace Conor at a much lower tier, reading as escalation; Reuniclus's other owner is Alexis with an Assault Vest support role. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/2/1/1.
crack: Taunt the setter before Spore resolves, or bring your own Trick Room. Ghost types and Shed Shell escape Shadow Tag. Fire, Flying and Psychic attacks answer the room; do not make contact with Shiinotic; and wait five turns if you can afford to - everything here is slow on purpose.

## E0486 TRAINER_HALLE class=ace
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: a redirection-and-snowball door. Pachirisu holds the field while two very different self-boosters build, and the Mega closer boosts all five of its own stats in a single turn. First two useful turns: Pachirisu's Follow Me pulls single-target attacks off Pinsir, and Nuzzle guarantees paralysis on a target rather than gambling; Pinsir's Mold Breaker means Sturdy, Levitate, Water Absorb and Volt Absorb do nothing to stop its attacks. Turn two uses whichever body the redirection kept alive. Alternate deployment: Naganadel's Beast Boost raises its Special Attack after every knockout, so a Nasty Plot plus one kill is usually the end of the fight; Zarude is the immediate physical option with Knock Off stripping items. Reserves & disruption: Lickilicky's Cloud Nine suppresses every weather effect on the field, which is a specific answer to the sun, sand and snow teams either side of it in the gauntlet, and its single-use Normal Gem makes one Body Slam far larger than it looks. Mega Falinks's No Retreat raises Attack, Defence, Special Attack, Special Defence and Speed by one stage each at the cost of being unable to switch out - an all-in that the player can answer by simply surviving it. Could this team be cooler? It is already an unusually well-varied door: two Ultra-Beast-tier boosters, a legendary monkey, a redirector and the campaign's only Mega Falinks, with no shared weather or terrain to exploit. It earns retention. The honest criticism is that Pachirisu duplicates Brawly's gym support from very early in the campaign, but that is deliberate escalation rather than repetition - the same tiny squirrel, now protecting something that ends games - and replacing it would cost the fight its one memorable callback. The change is tier only. Overrepresented? Naganadel, Zarude, Lickilicky and Mega Falinks are unique in the campaign; Pinsir's Mega is owned elsewhere but this base Mold Breaker version is not; Pachirisu's only other owner is Brawly. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Spread damage ignores Follow Me and kills Pachirisu at the same time. Fire and Flying attacks answer Pinsir and Zarude; Ground and Psychic answer Naganadel; Fighting answers Falinks and Lickilicky. Let No Retreat happen and then survive it - Falinks cannot leave, so a single bulky body can wall the closer outright.

## E0487 TRAINER_VITO class=ace
strategy: SUN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: the sun door - Drought on entry, Solar Beam with no charge turn, a Contrary Grass type that gets stronger every time it uses its strongest move, and a Mega Raichu X to close. First two useful turns: Ninetales establishes sun on entry and either fires an instantly charged Solar Beam or Encores a wasted opposing action; Bisharp attacks, and its Defiant means any stat drop aimed at it - an Intimidate, a Snarl, an Icy Wind - raises its Attack by two stages instead. Turn two uses the sun rather than reinforcing it. Alternate deployment: Lurantis's Contrary turns Leaf Storm's Special Attack drop into a two-stage raise, so it snowballs by attacking, and Superpower makes it physically tougher each time; Synthesis restores two-thirds of its HP under sun instead of half. Reserves & disruption: Choice Band Dodrio with Early Bird shrugs off sleep in a single turn, and Flygon's Yache Berry cushions the Ice attack that is otherwise its death; Mega Raichu X's Fake Out and Knock Off give the closer utility as well as damage. Could this team be cooler? The Contrary Lurantis and Defiant Bisharp pairing is the fight's real identity: both members are rewarded for the things a player normally does to weaken a team, which makes this door punish reflexive play rather than bad play. That earns retention and is why Lurantis stays here rather than with the route ace Sophia, who is rebuilt around a Dancer engine instead. The one duplication left is Bisharp against Dianne five doors later, and that is resolved on Dianne's side by promoting hers to Kingambit, so this remains the campaign's only Defiant Bisharp. Overrepresented? Mega Raichu X, Dodrio, Flygon and Lurantis are unique after this pass; Ninetales's Drought recurs only with Leaf's Kanto team in the post-game and with Maxie's Magma team far earlier, all with different payloads. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Take the sun away and Solar Beam needs a charge turn while Synthesis halves. Do not use Intimidate, Snarl or Icy Wind into Defiant. Fighting and Fire attacks answer Bisharp and Lurantis; Rock and Ice answer Dodrio and Flygon; Ground answers Raichu. Taunt stops Encore, Tailwind and Synthesis.

## E0488 TRAINER_OWEN class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a no-engine door built purely on threat density - six bodies with no shared weather, terrain or room, each of which beats a different thing. First two useful turns: Torterra attacks while Choice Scarf Staraptor picks the target its Intimidate has already weakened; Staraptor also carries Final Gambit, which deals damage equal to its own remaining HP and faints it - a genuine, unexpected trade the player cannot block with resistances. Turn two brings whichever body the opening revealed the player cannot answer. Alternate deployment: Sylveon's Pixilate converts Hyper Voice into a spread Fairy attack, and the Throat Spray it holds raises its Special Attack the moment that sound move connects; Helping Hand makes it a support turn when the spread would not kill. Reserves & disruption: Toxtricity's Punk Rock raises the power of its sound moves by 30% and halves incoming sound damage, so Overdrive is a spread Electric attack with no friendly-fire cost; Mega Lucario Z is the physical closer; Hisuian Lilligant's Victory Dance raises Attack, Defence and Speed at once and Chlorophyll does nothing here, which is the deliberate cost of a fast physical Grass body. Could this team be cooler? The answer is yes and it is now more varied: Tentacruel was this roster's fourth appearance in the audit range and Victreebel duplicated Julie's Mega two doors away, so two slots were doing nothing the player had not seen. Toxtricity brings a sound-based spread attacker that no other trainer in the campaign fields and pairs deliberately with Sylveon's own sound attack; Hisuian Lilligant brings a physical Grass dancer that is genuinely rare and threatens after a single free turn. The fight keeps its identity - no gimmick, six real threats - while stopping the repetition. Overrepresented? Toxtricity, Hisuian Lilligant, Staraptor, Sylveon and Mega Lucario Z are unique in the campaign; Torterra's other owner is Warren's Trick Room team far earlier. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/2/1/1.
crack: Ice and Fire attacks answer Torterra and Lilligant; Electric and Rock answer Staraptor; Steel and Poison answer Sylveon; Ground answers Toxtricity and Lucario. Watch for Final Gambit from a healthy Staraptor. Taunt stops Victory Dance and Helping Hand, and the Choice lock can be baited.

## E0489 TRAINER_CAROLINE class=ace
strategy: SAND
mega_slots: 3
plan: Concept: the sandstorm door, and the only one in the gauntlet - five bodies that either double their Speed in sand, gain power from it, or are simply immune to its chip damage. First two useful turns: Tyranitar's Sand Stream is live on entry and Smooth Rock stretches it to eight turns, so Stoutland and Sandslash are already at doubled Speed before the player's lead acts; Tyranitar attacks or Protects to bank a turn. Turn two spends the Sand Rush window on damage, because the whole plan expires with the weather. Alternate deployment: Mega Garchomp arrives in the third slot rather than the last, which is deliberate - its Sand Veil raises evasion in the storm it is standing in, so it is hardest to remove exactly while the sand is up. Alolan Dugtrio's Sand Force raises its Ground, Rock and Steel attacks by 30% in sand, and Sucker Punch gives the team priority. Reserves & disruption: Eternatus is the weather-independent closer with Dynamax Cannon, Pressure doubling the PP cost of every move aimed at it, and enormous HP; it is the answer to a player who solves the sand and then expects the fight to be over. Could this team be cooler? It is the correct sand team for this position and the composition holds - three distinct sand abilities and a legendary that does not care about the weather is a genuinely good structure, and Sand Veil placed early rather than late is a smart, slightly unfair touch. The duplication with Michelle's mislabelled SAND flag five doors earlier is resolved on her side, so Caroline is now the campaign's only Sand Stream owner outside the Magma arc and the sandstorm reads as this trainer's signature. The remaining fix here was a legality error: Stoutland cannot learn Double-Edge, so it takes Retaliate instead, which doubles in power after an ally has fainted - a better fit for a team that expects to trade. Overrepresented? Stoutland, Alolan Dugtrio, Sandslash and Eternatus are unique in the campaign; Mega Garchomp recurs only as Cynthia's custom Garchomp Z variant in the final optional fight, a deliberate mirror; Tyranitar's other owners are the Magma arc far earlier. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/2/1/1/2.
crack: Replace the weather and three members lose half their Speed and their evasion boost at once. Water, Grass, Fighting and Ground attacks answer Tyranitar; Fighting answers Stoutland; Ice and Dragon answer Garchomp; Ground and Psychic answer Eternatus. Wide Guard blanks Rock Slide, and sand chip damage is hurting the player far more than it hurts this team.

## E0490 TRAINER_JULIE class=ace
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: the second sun door, deliberately built the opposite way round to Vito's - his sun powers a Contrary snowballer, hers powers two Chlorophyll sweepers and a Solar Power glass cannon. First two useful turns: Torkoal's Drought is live on entry and Eruption fires at full power only while Torkoal is at full HP, so the player must damage it before it acts; Venusaur is already at doubled Speed from Chlorophyll and opens with Sleep Powder or Giga Drain. Turn two uses the real HP and speed state rather than the opening one. Alternate deployment: Heliolisk's Solar Power raises its Special Attack by half in sun at the cost of HP every turn, and Glare paralyses regardless of the target's type; Lycanroc's Accelerock is the priority that punishes a weakened lead. Reserves & disruption: Zamazenta's Dauntless Shield raises its Defence on entry, and Iron Defence plus Body Press turns that defence directly into damage - a defensive legend inside an all-out sun team is a deliberate change of gear; Mega Victreebel's Weather Ball becomes a 100-power Fire attack under its own sun. Could this team be cooler? Two sun teams inside sixteen doors is a real question, and the answer is that they are legitimately different fights - Vito's rewards you for not weakening him, Julie's punishes you for not being fast - so both hold. What makes Julie's memorable is the Zamazenta slot: a player who has correctly brought a Water or Rock answer to a sun team then runs into a wall that gets tougher every turn and converts that toughness into damage. That contrast is worth more than a rarer sixth Fire type would be. The change here is tier only, plus the Victreebel duplication being resolved on Owen's side so the Mega is unique. Overrepresented? Heliolisk, Lycanroc, Zamazenta and Mega Victreebel are unique in the campaign; Torkoal and Venusaur recur at lower tiers with Darcy, Flannery and Marley in different ability roles. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Take the sun and everything slows down at once. Water, Rock and Ground attacks answer the Fire half; Fire, Ice, Flying and Psychic answer the Grass half; Fighting answers Zamazenta only if you can out-damage Iron Defence. Chip Torkoal before it moves and Eruption collapses. Taunt stops Sleep Powder, Glare and Iron Defence.

## E0491 TRAINER_FELIX class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 5
plan: Concept: the last non-rival door before the League, and the only one that opens with sleep and closes with a stance change - six threats with no engine except raw quality. First two useful turns: Jynx's Lovely Kiss or Fake Out steals the opening turn while Xerneas, holding a Power Herb, uses Geomancy - a two-turn move that the Herb completes instantly, raising Special Attack, Special Defence and Speed by two stages each in a single turn. Turn two is the player's last chance to stop a fully set-up Fairy Aura legend. Alternate deployment: Sceptile and Mega Greninja are the fast mixed-attacking mode, with Protean changing Greninja's type to match each move it uses so its STAB is always live and its resistances are never the ones you prepared for. Reserves & disruption: Eviolite Duraludon is a genuine special wall because it still evolves, and Stalwart means its attacks cannot be redirected by Follow Me, Rage Powder or Storm Drain; Aegislash's Stance Change flips it to Blade Forme when it attacks and back to Shield Forme when it uses King's Shield, and its Weakness Policy means a super-effective hit while it is shielded makes the next attack enormous. Could this team be cooler? This is the correct final gate: one instant setup, one type-shifting Mega, one stance-shifting Policy closer, and a Stalwart wall that specifically defeats the redirection strategies the gauntlet has been teaching the player to use. It earns retention unchanged in composition. The one thing worth stating plainly is that Geomancy through Power Herb is the single most dangerous turn in Victory Road, and the level spread now reflects that the fight is meant to be decided on turn one or two. Overrepresented? Jynx, Xerneas, Duraludon, Aegislash and Mega Greninja are unique in the campaign; Sceptile's other owner is Susie's route team with a White Herb Unburden set; Greninja's base form appears once in the Sootopolis gym at a far lower tier. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/2/1/1/2/1.
crack: Stop Geomancy or lose. Deny Lovely Kiss with your own Fake Out, or lead with a Fire, Rock or Steel body that survives Jynx. Poison and Steel attacks answer Xerneas; Fighting and Ground answer Aegislash while it is in Blade Forme; and do not hit Aegislash super-effectively while it is shielded. Fighting and Ground answer Duraludon despite the Eviolite.

## E0492 TRAINER_DIANNE class=ace
strategy: SNOW,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the blizzard door - automatic snow, an Aurora Veil that halves both kinds of damage, and a closer that smashes through the screens it hid behind. First two useful turns: Abomasnow's Snow Warning is live on entry, so Froslass can immediately use Aurora Veil, which needs snow to work at all and then reduces physical and special damage for five turns; Abomasnow's Blizzard never misses in its own weather. Turn two attacks from behind the veil rather than adding more setup. Alternate deployment: Magmortar and Virizion are the deliberately off-type answers to the Fire, Steel and Fighting attacks a player brings to beat an Ice team; Justified raises Virizion's Attack whenever a Dark attack hits it. Reserves & disruption: Froslass's Destiny Bond takes the attacker down with it, so removing the veil-setter has a real price; Kingambit's Supreme Overlord raises its Attack for every ally that has already fainted, which makes it strongest exactly when the fight is nearly over, and Sucker Punch turns that accumulated power into priority. Mega Barbaracle's Shell Smash under a still-active Aurora Veil is the intended finish. Could this team be cooler? The Aurora Veil plus Destiny Bond pairing is a genuinely nasty trap - killing the screen-setter costs the player a Pokemon - and the snow is properly sourced, so the concept holds. The change is Bisharp, which was an exact duplicate of Vito's five doors earlier: Kingambit is its evolution, it is rarer, and Supreme Overlord makes it a late-fight threat that scales with the gauntlet's own attrition, which is a far better fit for the last ace before the Elite Four than a second copy of a set the player just beat. Overrepresented? Kingambit, Magmortar, Virizion and Mega Barbaracle are unique in the campaign; Abomasnow's other owner Makayla runs a physical route set; Froslass recurs as Glacia's League Mega, which reads as this trainer studying under her. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Replace the weather and Aurora Veil cannot be set at all; Brick Break, Defog and Infiltrator get through it once it is up. Fire, Fighting, Rock and Steel attacks answer the Ice half. Do not kill Froslass with your ace - Destiny Bond takes it with her. Kingambit gets stronger as the fight goes on, so do not save it for last.

## E0495 TRAINER_GLACIA class=elite
strategy: SNOW
mega_slots: 4,6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Snow endurance with physical and special closers. Mega Froslass replaces Articuno as the fast special reserve and second Snow Warning opportunity. Keep Specs Kyurem, Chien-Pao, Slush Rush Sandslash and Mega Baxcalibur; Baxcalibur gains High Horsepower over redundant Ice Shard. Stats: Ninetales invests in HP/Speed; Kyurem special offense; Chien-Pao/Sandslash/Baxcalibur physical offense; Froslass fast special. Mega Baxcalibur has175 Attack/115HP/117Defense and retains Thermal Exchange. Snow's Ice Defense bonus does not cover special attacks. Could it be cooler? If the first snow setter goes down or weather is replaced, Mega Froslass can establish the second snow phase through actual Mega activation. Sword of Ruin and the physical sweepers convert that window into pressure; Icy Wind offers speed control when snow-speed alone does not solve the board. Overrepresented? This is the League culmination of Lucas's snow formation. No unnecessary third snow setter or fourth generic Ice setup sweeper. Froslass/Baxcalibur are separate offensive roles and do not duplicate another League Mega. crack: Weather replacement, strong special Fire/Fighting coverage, screen removal and denying the fast attackers. Sword of Ruin also reduces eligible teammates' Defense; snow is not an excuse to ignore that liability. Glaive Rush leaves its real retaliation risk.

## E0498 TRAINER_WALLY_VR_2 class=rival
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the rival arc, part two. Wally comes back as Champion-tier and the arc completes its mirror - the Gallade that was his Mega at Victory Road now stands beside him as an ordinary partner while Altaria, the Swablu he caught on Route 114, carries the stone. First two useful turns: Indeedee-F sets Psychic Terrain on entry and redirects with Follow Me to keep Gallade's Sharpness-boosted Psycho Cut or Sacred Sword alive; Psychic Terrain also blocks all priority moves aimed at his grounded members, which is what makes the setup viable at this level. Gallade attacks. Turn two picks the special half rather than repeating the redirect. Alternate deployment: Tapu Lele is the raw damage mode - its Psychic Surge re-establishes the terrain if it has been replaced, and under it Psychic and Psyshock hit half again as hard. Gardevoir's Trace copies the most valuable ability on the opposing side the moment it enters. Reserves & disruption: Magearna's Soul-Heart raises its Special Attack every time any Pokemon on the field faints, so a long fight makes it progressively lethal, and Fleur Cannon is enormous at the cost of a two-stage drop each use. Mega Altaria's Pixilate converts Return into a Fairy-type attack with a power bonus, and at maximum friendship Return is already at full power - a Dragon Dance behind that is the closer, and its Dragon/Fairy typing means the Steel answer the player saved for Magearna is the only thing that also answers this. Note that Psychic Terrain blocks the team's own priority as well, so Gallade has no safe priority option while it is up. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the rebuild is what makes the two Wally fights a rival arc rather than two unrelated bosses. The authored roster was Dragapult, Heatran and Incineroar behind a Mega Magearna - a strong team with no connection to Wally at all, and one that borrowed Steven's and the League's identities. The new version keeps a single coherent Psychic and Fairy identity across both fights, promotes his own Altaria to the Mega slot using the campaign's one unused Altarianite, and makes the memorable moment structural: the player recognises every Pokemon, and the one that was a support in the first fight is now the thing that kills them. Overrepresented? Mega Altaria, Tapu Lele and Magearna appear nowhere else in the campaign; Indeedee-F's other owner is the route trainer Alexis at a far lower tier; Gardevoir and Gallade are Wally's alone. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Steel and Poison attacks answer four of the six; Fire answers Magearna and Gallade; Ghost and Flying answer the Psychic half. Replace the terrain and your priority works again. Spread damage ignores Follow Me. Let Magearna use Fleur Cannon twice and it has halved its own Special Attack; Mega Altaria is bulky but must dance before it threatens, and Steel and Poison attacks answer it outright.

## E0499 TRAINER_VINCENT class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a post-game control specialist - a Prankster that dictates the turn order, a Regenerator pivot that never stays broken, and three closers that each punish a different mistake. First two useful turns: Klefki's Prankster gives Thunder Wave, Light Screen and Foul Play priority, so the speed control lands before anything else happens, and its Steel/Fairy typing walls the Dragon and Dark attacks the rest of the team invites; Mienshao attacks and U-turns out, restoring a third of its HP through Regenerator each time it leaves. Turn two pivots only when the replacement genuinely improves the board. Alternate deployment: Poison Heal Gliscor is the independent Swords Dance mode - the Toxic Orb heals it every turn instead of damaging it, so it is immune to further status and gains HP while setting up. Reserves & disruption: Xurkitree's Beast Boost and Electroweb combine speed control with a Special Attack that grows after each knockout; Choice Scarf Typhlosion's Eruption is calculated from its current HP at the moment it fires, so it must come in clean. Mega Zeraora's Plasma Fists converts all Normal-type moves used afterwards that turn into Electric ones, which can matter for a player's own Extreme Speed. Could this team be cooler? The control-plus-three-closers structure is right for an optional post-game ace, and the honest weakness was the lead: Sableye was an exact duplicate of Albert's Victory Road set, so the fight opened with a puzzle the player had already solved. Klefki keeps the Prankster identity while adding a typing and a screen the old lead did not have, and Foul Play still punishes the physical sweeper a player brings for Gliscor. The rest earns retention - Eruption on a Choice Scarf is a genuine, unforgiving read for both sides. Overrepresented? Klefki, Mienshao, Gliscor, Xurkitree, Typhlosion and Mega Zeraora are unique in the campaign after this pass; base Zeraora is removed from Leaf's Kanto team so the Mega is the only one. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Fire and Ground attacks answer Klefki; Fairy and Flying answer Mienshao; Ice and Water answer Gliscor; Ground answers Xurkitree and Zeraora. Taunt stops the Prankster support and Swords Dance together. Chip Typhlosion once and Eruption stops mattering, and Magic Guard or a Steel type ignores the Toxic Orb plan.

## E0500 TRAINER_KEIRA class=ace
strategy: SAND,REDIRECTION,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a sandstorm that exists to protect a single reactive nuke. Necrozma holds a Weakness Policy, and everything else on the team decides whether the player is allowed to trigger it. First two useful turns: Gigalith's Sand Stream is live on entry with a Smooth Rock behind it, so Sandslash is already at doubled Speed; Sandslash attacks. Turn two either spends the sand window or transitions to support - but only when that protects a body worth protecting. Alternate deployment: Clefable's Follow Me pulls a single-target attack away from Necrozma, which is a genuine decision rather than a free action, because a super-effective hit landing on Necrozma would raise both its attacking stats by two stages through the Policy - the redirection can correctly prevent the team's own payoff, and both benefits must never be counted at once. Prism Armor separately reduces super-effective damage against it by a quarter, making the Policy far likelier to fire safely. Reserves & disruption: Whimsicott's Prankster Tailwind and Encore are the backup speed plan for when the sand is replaced, not automatic redundancy; Rotom-Wash covers Water and Electric and takes sand chip every turn, which is a real cost. Mega Clefable is the closer. Could this team be cooler? The plan's internal tension - a redirector that can accidentally disarm its own ace - is unusually sophisticated and is exactly the kind of decision the AI audit should care about, so the concept earns retention unchanged. The honest note is that this is the second sand team in the post-game and the third overall; it is kept because its sand is a shield for a reactive item rather than a Sand Rush speed engine like Caroline's, which makes it a different fight despite the shared weather. Levels rise to post-game tier. Overrepresented? Gigalith, Necrozma, Whimsicott and Mega Clefable are unique; Sandslash's other owner is Caroline in Victory Road; Rotom-Wash's other owner is Daphne's gym ice room. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Hit Necrozma with neutral damage only, or do not hit it at all until the Policy holder is the last thing standing. Water, Grass, Fighting and Steel attacks answer Gigalith; Dark and Ghost answer Necrozma; Steel and Poison answer Clefable. Taunt stops Tailwind, Encore and Follow Me. Replacing the weather removes Sandslash's speed and the team's chip damage together.

## E0501 TRAINER_LEROY class=ace
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the post-game Trick Room, built around the one legendary that is genuinely happier moving last. First two useful turns: Cresselia sets Trick Room behind a Mental Herb, or Helping Hands a Volcanion attack when the speeds already favour the team; Volcanion's Steam Eruption is a 110-power Water attack with a 30% burn chance from a Fire/Water body that resists almost everything a room team fears. Turn two spends the reversed order rather than cycling Moonlight. Alternate deployment: Mega Scrafty's Fake Out protects Cresselia's room turn before the transformation, and Intimidate on entry softens the physical lead. Reserves & disruption: Mawile is base Intimidate and deliberately not a second Mega, so its slot is a second Intimidate body and a Sucker Punch, not a hidden ace; Slurpuff's Sweet Veil makes the whole team immune to sleep, which is the specific answer to the Spore and Sleep Powder leads the post-game is full of; Celesteela's Beast Boost, Wide Guard and enormous bulk close the room. Every slow member carries a zero Speed IV so the room works in the team's favour. Could this team be cooler? Volcanion as a Trick Room attacker is a genuinely inspired pairing - Water Absorb plus a Fire typing means the two attack types that usually break a slow team both fail - and Sweet Veil is a deliberate, well-chosen answer to the format's most common opening. It earns retention. The improvement made is the same structural one as the other room teams: zero Speed IVs on the Brave, Sassy and Quiet members, which the authored version lacked, turning a described room into a functioning one. Overrepresented? Cresselia, Volcanion, Slurpuff, Celesteela and Mega Scrafty are unique in the campaign; Mawile's base Intimidate set appears only here. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Taunt the setter, or be slower yourself. Dark, Ghost and Bug attacks answer Cresselia; Ground and Electric answer Volcanion; Fire and Ground answer Mawile and Celesteela. Sweet Veil stops sleep but nothing else - paralysis, burn and confusion all still land. Wait out the five turns if you can survive them.

## E0504 TRAINER_COLTON class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND

Eternal Floette deserves specific defence: it is one of the rarest forms in the series, it is not simply a stat upgrade, and pairing its Calm Mind with Flower Veil and Wish makes it a win condition the player has to break through rather than out-damage. ## E0505 TRAINER_MICAH class=regular
strategy: SNOW
mega_slots: 2
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a ship passenger with a Mega Lapras that brings its own winter. First two useful turns: Jolteon fires a Choice Specs Thunderbolt or pivots with Volt Switch while Lapras either attacks or lays down Snowscape; Freeze-Dry is the set's point, a special Ice attack that is super effective against Water types and therefore beats the deck's own element. Turn two evaluates the actual post-transformation ability and the real weather rather than the base-form assumption. Alternate deployment: Luxray's Intimidate and Snarl reduce both of the opposing attacking stats before Clawitzer's Mega Launcher pulses land; Water Pulse, Dark Pulse and Aura Sphere are each raised by half again. Reserves & disruption: snow raises Ice-type Defence, which matters only for Lapras here, and Volt Absorb on Jolteon turns an opposing Electric attack into healing. Could this team be cooler? For a shipboard regular the answer is that it should stay small, and the change needed was a duplication fix rather than an upgrade: the authored lead was an exact copy of Edgar's Choice Scarf Manectric from Victory Road, which made a post-game encounter feel like a repeat. Jolteon takes the slot with a different item, a different ability role and the same job, which keeps the fight brisk while removing the echo. The Freeze-Dry Mega Lapras is the memorable part and is left alone; a four-member regular that beats your Water switch-in with an Ice move is a good, compact joke. Overrepresented? Mega Lapras and Jolteon are unique in the campaign; Clawitzer's other owner is Olivia's Sootopolis artillery room; Luxray's other owner is the route ace Paxton; Manectric is returned to Edgar alone. Levels: cap 100, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Ground attacks answer Jolteon and Luxray outright; Grass and Fighting answer Lapras; Grass and Electric answer Clawitzer. Replace the weather, exploit the Choice lock on Jolteon, and remember Freeze-Dry does not care that your switch-in resists Water.

## E0506 TRAINER_THOMAS class=regular
strategy: ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 4
ai: Conservative
tactic: ACTIVATE WEAVILE ICE_SHARD ARMAROUGE
plan: Concept: the deliberate friendly-fire combo - Weavile shoots its own partner on purpose. Armarouge has Weak Armor, so taking a physical hit lowers its Defence by one stage and raises its Speed by two; a resisted Ice Shard from a zero-Attack Weavile is the cheapest possible way to buy that boost. First two useful turns: the authored tactic fires Ice Shard into Armarouge only when the resisted damage and the Defence drop are genuinely worth the Speed, and Armarouge must not Protect through the trigger; Armarouge then attacks with Armor Cannon, which is enormous and lowers its own defences further. Turn two plays the real speed and survival state, not an imagined item boost. Alternate deployment: Weavile's Fake Out supports Choice Scarf Genesect's immediate special pressure with no friendly damage at all, which is the mode to use when Armarouge cannot afford the hit; Download raises Genesect's Attack or Special Attack on entry depending on which of the opposing defences is weaker. Reserves & disruption: Mega Scolipede's Speed Boost must be earned before transforming, and it is a separate timed plan rather than part of the combo. Weavile's Attack IV is deliberately zero to minimise the activation damage, which is a real cost to its Knock Off. Could this team be cooler? The activation combo is one of the campaign's most distinctive authored ideas and is kept exactly as designed - a trainer whose plan is to hurt their own Pokemon is memorable precisely because it looks like a mistake. The previously flagged amendment is applied here: the zero Attack IV is now in the set rather than only in the prose, so the combo is survivable as described. The one honest limitation recorded is that native survival and ordering tests are still required; this is an executable plan, not a proven one. Overrepresented? Armarouge, Genesect and Mega Scolipede are unique in the campaign; Weavile's other owner is Albert in Victory Road with a full-Attack Clear Amulet set, which is deliberately the opposite build. Levels: cap 100, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Deny the combo with your own Fake Out or with priority that kills Weavile first. Fighting, Rock and Steel attacks answer Weavile; Water, Ground and Dark answer Armarouge, and its Defence is already dropping every time it fires. Redirection pulls the activation away where supported, and Armor Cannon's own drops make Armarouge fragile by turn three.

## E0507 TRAINER_LEA_AND_JED class=casual
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a ranch couple's herd - four heavy Normal and Fighting bodies, no gimmick, and one of them hitting far harder than a casual slot suggests. First two useful turns: Miltank uses Helping Hand only when Tauros's Double-Edge or Close Combat actually crosses a threshold, and Body Slam otherwise - Scrappy lets it hit Ghost types that expect an immunity. Tauros attacks after its own Intimidate has softened the lead. Turn two may bring Primeape, whose Defiant turns any stat drop aimed at it into a two-stage Attack raise. Alternate deployment: Bouffalant and Primeape are the pure aggression mode - Reckless raises Head Charge and Megahorn, at a recoil cost Bouffalant's HP can afford. Reserves & disruption: Milk Drink restores half Miltank's HP and is the herd's only recovery; Choice Band Bouffalant is locked once it commits. Could this team be cooler? It is cooler than it was, for a legality reason as well as a design one. The authored fourth slot was a Cosmog with Double-Edge and Teleport - a charming idea, but Cosmog cannot learn Double-Edge at all, so the set was illegal and the slot was in practice a two-move Pokemon that could not fight. Bouffalant replaces it with something that genuinely belongs on a ranch, is rarely seen anywhere in the series, and gives the pair a fourth real threat so the fight is a fight. The novelty is not lost - a Choice Band Head Charge from a woolly bull is its own kind of joke - and the encounter no longer depends on a form whose attack legality could not be validated. Overrepresented? Bouffalant, Tauros and Primeape are unique in the campaign; Miltank's other owner is the route trainer Kara far earlier with the same Scrappy identity, which is the intended echo for a pair of ranchers. Levels: cap 100, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Fighting attacks answer Miltank, Tauros and Bouffalant; Psychic and Fairy answer Primeape. Do not lower Primeape's stats. Special attackers walk through the whole herd, and every recoil user here is damaging itself on your behalf.

## E0509 TRAINER_NAOMI class=regular
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 3
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a night-garden quartet - two Grass-type ambushers and the campaign's Mega Darkrai waiting behind them. First two useful turns: Tsareena attacks or pivots while Salazzle uses Fake Out only when it buys a useful action; Queenly Majesty blocks all opposing priority moves aimed at Naomi's side, which is specifically what protects the setup turn that follows. Turn two can introduce Darkrai once the opening is safe. Alternate deployment: Salazzle's Corrosion lets it Toxic a Steel or Poison type that believes it is immune, which is the answer to the walls this team cannot break; its Fake Out supports Darkrai's Nasty Plot or a risk-bearing Hypnosis. Reserves & disruption: Meowscarada's Protean changes its type to match each move, and Flower Trick is a Grass attack that always lands and always critically hits - it ignores accuracy checks, evasion, Focus Sash-preserving defence boosts and Reflect entirely. Bad Dreams damages any sleeping opponent each turn, so Hypnosis and Bad Dreams reinforce each other, but Hypnosis is never a guaranteed setup window and must not be scored as one. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and one slot changed. Overqwil was this roster's third appearance in the audit range and did nothing the team needed; Meowscarada replaces it with a genuinely rare starter whose Flower Trick is the single most reliable attack in the range - a guaranteed critical hit that no defensive boost, screen or evasion can stop - which pairs with Queenly Majesty to make this a fight about being unable to defend rather than unable to attack. The Mega Darkrai remains the draw and the honest limitation stays recorded: its base ability may not survive transformation. Overrepresented? Meowscarada, Tsareena and Mega Darkrai are unique in the campaign; Salazzle's other owner is Hope in Victory Road with the same Corrosion identity at a lower tier. Levels: cap 100, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Fire, Flying, Ice and Psychic attacks answer the Grass half; Fighting and Bug answer Darkrai. Sleep immunity - Insomnia, Vital Spirit, Safeguard, Misty Terrain or a held Lum Berry - turns off both Hypnosis and Bad Dreams at once. Spread damage ignores Queenly Majesty, which only blocks priority.

## E0510 TRAINER_WALLACE_DOUBLES_LEGENDS class=elite
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the Champion off duty, with no type restriction and no reason to hold back - a primal storm with five legends standing in it. First two useful turns: Kyogre's Drizzle is live on entry and its Mystic Water raises Origin Pulse further; Thunder never misses in that rain, which is why the whole roster is built around electricity and wind. Therian Tornadus sets Tailwind when the speed race matters or fires Bleakwind Storm, which is perfectly accurate in rain and lowers the Speed of both targets it hits. Turn two makes a real Regenerator switch only when the replacement improves the board - Tornadus refunds a third of its HP on the way out. Alternate deployment: Zapdos is the second rain abuser and the fight's most punishing body - Thunder at full accuracy and Hurricane at full accuracy from a Pokemon with no Ground weakness through the storm. Lugia's Multiscale halves every hit while it is at full HP, and Calm Mind behind that bulk makes it the win condition a player cannot out-stall. Reserves & disruption: Terapagos supplies the Tera Starstorm lane, and its exact supported form and move behaviour remain an explicit source gate - the move's name alone neither permits Terastallisation nor proves the set illegal. Mega Mewtwo X is the physical closer in an otherwise entirely special roster, which is the point: the defensive answer the player has been building all fight is the wrong one. Could this team be cooler? It is cooler now, and the change was about making the storm mean something. The authored roster carried an Arceus with no plate - an ordinary Normal type wearing the most famous name in the series - and a base Calyrex whose Helping Hand support was far below the tier of everything around it. Replacing them with Zapdos and Lugia turns a list of famous names into a team: Zapdos converts the rain into guaranteed 110-power attacks, and Lugia is the wall that makes the fight last long enough for that to matter. Every remaining member now either makes the weather, uses the weather, or deliberately ignores it. Overrepresented? Kyogre is shared with Champion Wallace's own League team by design - this is the same trainer showing the same partner without the type restriction - and Tornadus, Zapdos, Lugia, Terapagos and Mega Mewtwo X appear nowhere else in the campaign. Mewtwo's other form, Y, belongs to Leaf's optional fight, which is a deliberate pairing. Levels: cap 100, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3. This is optional post-game content and is meant to be the hardest fight in the game.
crack: Take the rain away and Thunder and Hurricane both become coin flips - that is the single highest-value action available. Electric and Grass attacks answer Kyogre; Rock and Ice answer Tornadus and Zapdos; Dark and Ghost answer Lugia and Mewtwo. Chip Lugia once and Multiscale is gone. Wide Guard blanks Origin Pulse and Bleakwind Storm; priority denial and Haze answer the Calm Mind plan; and the closer is physical, so an Intimidate or a physical wall held in reserve is worth more than another special sponge.

## E0511 TRAINER_LEAF_ALTERING_CAVE class=rival
strategy: SUN,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the Kanto challenger in Altering Cave - six Pokemon from a different region's memory, led by sun and ended by the strongest Psychic attacker in the series. First two useful turns: Ninetales establishes Drought on entry, so Solar Beam needs no charge turn and Encore can lock a wasted opposing action; Kangaskhan opens with Fake Out or immediate Scrappy pressure, which lets its Normal attacks hit Ghost types that would otherwise be immune. Turn two pivots to whichever support and threat pair the opening revealed. Alternate deployment: Chlorophyll Tangrowth is already at doubled Speed in its own sun and uses Rage Powder to hold the field for Mega Mewtwo Y's special offence, or Sleep Powder to remove a threat outright. Reserves & disruption: Gengar supplies Icy Wind speed control and Sludge Bomb, and Snorlax is the immovable object - Thick Fat halves incoming Fire and Ice damage, Recycle restores its consumed Leftovers, and its HP is large enough to absorb the burst the player was saving for Mewtwo. Rage Powder has ordinary target restrictions and cannot protect against spread attacks. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and one slot changed for both theme and duplication. Zeraora is not a Kanto Pokemon and it duplicated Vincent's Mega two doors earlier, so it was doing double damage to the fight's identity. Snorlax replaces it with the most recognisable Kanto body in the series, gives the roster the physical wall it completely lacked, and adds a genuinely memorable interaction in Recycle - a Snorlax that keeps eating its own Leftovers is a wall the player has to actually kill rather than outlast. The team now reads as exactly what it is: a Kanto champion's party, unchanged in spirit, ending on Mega Mewtwo Y. Overrepresented? Snorlax, Kangaskhan's base Scrappy set, Tangrowth and Mega Mewtwo Y are unique in the campaign; Ninetales's Drought recurs with Vito and the Magma arc at lower tiers; Gengar's other owners are the route ace Mollie and Phoebe's League Mega, all with different items and roles. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2, scaled to the live cap rather than the discarded fixed level-100 floor.
crack: Take the sun and Solar Beam needs a charge turn while Tangrowth loses half its Speed. Rock and Water attacks answer Ninetales; Fighting answers Kangaskhan and Snorlax; Fire, Ice and Flying answer Tangrowth; Dark and Ghost answer Mewtwo. Safety Goggles ignores both powders, spread damage ignores Rage Powder, and Mega Mewtwo Y is extraordinarily frail on the physical side.

## E0512 TRAINER_STEVEN class=rival
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the Steel champion's exhibition team - a steel spine, a fossil support, and two of Hoenn's and Sinnoh's heaviest legends, with exactly one Mega between them. First two useful turns: Skarmory chooses Tailwind only when Rayquaza's attack or a safe Dragon Dance actually benefits, and Body Press otherwise, converting its own enormous Defence into damage behind Sturdy; Rayquaza attacks, with Air Lock suppressing every weather effect on the field for as long as it is out - which quietly turns off a player's rain, sun or sand team without spending a move. Turn two attacks through the speed window or brings the appropriate heavy reserve. Alternate deployment: Cradily's Storm Drain draws opposing single-target Water attacks away from Melmetal and converts them into a Special Attack boost, and Melmetal's Double Iron Bash hits twice with a flinch chance on each hit, which the Iron Fist ability boosts further. Reserves & disruption: Dialga is the special coverage lane with Pressure doubling the PP cost of every move aimed at it. The Mega designation belongs to Metagross alone - Rayquaza's move-based eligibility must never be allowed to create a second transformation for the same trainer, and the team is built assuming Rayquaza stays untransformed. Steven's fixed allied party in the Mossdeep multi battle is a separate roster and must never be replaced with this exhibition team. Could this team be cooler? The composition is right and stays: Air Lock as free weather suppression, Storm Drain protecting a Steel giant from its worst matchup, and a Mega Metagross closer is a legible, characterful Steven team, and the restraint of one Mega among two legends is what keeps it from being a list. What the block needed was the amendment made explicit rather than pending - Metagross is now the designated Mega in both prose and slot - plus post-game level offsets so the exhibition is a real fight. Overrepresented? Skarmory, Rayquaza, Cradily, Melmetal and Dialga are unique in the campaign; Mega Metagross recurs in the earlier Magma and multi-battle blocks, which is correct - it is Steven's signature and the player has seen it fighting alongside them before meeting it across the field. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/2/1/2/2/3. Optional exhibition only; never a story gate.
crack: Fire, Fighting and Ground attacks answer the Steel core; Ice, Dragon and Fairy answer Rayquaza and Dialga; Bug and Steel answer Cradily. Do not use single-target Water attacks into Storm Drain. Air Lock means your own weather plan is off while Rayquaza is out, so remove it first. Skarmory's Sturdy guarantees it one turn, and Metagross is fast but not bulky enough to survive a super-effective hit.

## E0513 TRAINER_CYNTHIA_1 class=leader
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the last fight in the campaign. Cynthia brings her own team - the Sinnoh partners the player will recognise on sight - with one future-paradox legend and a custom Mega Garchomp to finish. First two useful turns: Togekiss uses Follow Me to pull a single-target attack away from Spiritomb or Lucario, or Air Slash, whose flinch chance Serene Grace doubles to 60% - the highest-value single click on the board; Milotic attacks or recovers, and Competitive doubles its Special Attack the moment any stat of its is lowered, so an Intimidate or a Snarl aimed at her lead hands her the fight. Turn two brings a real attacker rather than repeating the support. Alternate deployment: Togekiss supports Miraidon's special pressure after the deliberate Electric Terrain transition - Hadron Engine sets the terrain on entry and raises Miraidon's own Special Attack by a third while it is up, and Electro Drift does extra damage against anything it is super effective against. Reserves & disruption: Spiritomb has no type weaknesses at all and its Infiltrator ignores screens and Substitutes; mixed Choice Scarf Lucario is the surprise speed tier, capable of both Close Combat and Aura Sphere; Mega Garchomp Z is a custom form whose stats and ability must be read from source rather than imported from the ordinary Mega. Note that Electric Terrain prevents grounded Pokemon from falling asleep, which is exactly why this team carries no sleep move - the terrain phase and the support phase do not fight each other. Could this team be cooler? It is cooler now for one specific reason. The authored version paired Roserade's Sleep Powder with Miraidon's Electric Terrain, which makes that Sleep Powder fail against every grounded target on the field - the team's own ace disabled its own lead, and the block could only flag the conflict rather than fix it. Replacing Roserade with Milotic resolves it completely, restores one of Cynthia's most recognisable partners, and swaps a fragile sleep gamble for a Competitive trap that punishes the Intimidate and Snarl leads a player brings to a physical-looking team. The result is a final boss whose six members are all hers and whose two phases no longer contradict. Overrepresented? Spiritomb, Lucario, Miraidon and Mega Garchomp Z are unique in the campaign; Togekiss is Cynthia's alone now that Wally is rebuilt around his own partners; Milotic's other owner is Champion Wallace's Mega, which is the right contrast - the Champion's Milotic is a Mega wall, hers is a Competitive attacker; Garchomp's other owner is Caroline's sand team in Victory Road. Levels: cap 100, offsets 2/2/3/3/3/4 - the highest-level opponents in the game, and deliberately so.
crack: Do not lower Milotic's stats. Spread damage ignores Follow Me and kills Togekiss, which is the whole support plan. Replace the terrain and Miraidon loses a third of its damage; Ground attacks answer it outright and are immune to nothing it has. Fairy and Ice answer Garchomp, Fighting and Fairy answer Lucario, and Spiritomb has no weaknesses but also very little Speed - Trick Room, Taunt and sustained pressure beat it rather than type advantage.
